# Lasci's Board — Master Project Guide

> Read this before touching any code. Single source of truth for product/feature state and coding rules.
> Keep this file lean — no clutter, no outdated info, no verbose explanations. Every line must earn its place.

> **DATE FORMAT — the owner's hard rule: every visible date is `DD.MM.YYYY` (`29.09.2026`), nothing else, anywhere.** No month names (`Sep`, `September`), no `/`, no ISO, no `en-US`/`en-GB` locale output, no time on a date-only field. Times are `HH:MM` 24h. Use `src/shared/utils/dateFormat.ts` for display and `DateInput` for typing a date. Only chart axis letters/ticks may be shorter.

**Doc map** — read in this order depending on what you're doing:
- `README.md` (repo root) — human/AI-agnostic orientation: what this app is, tech overview, quick start. Start here if you've never seen this repo.
- **`CLAUDE.md` (this file)** — the definitive guide for any coding agent (Claude or otherwise) before making a change: routes, features, coding rules, edge functions, key patterns.
- `AGENTS.md` (repo root) — supplementary rules specific to database/schema/Supabase work (migrations, RLS, edge function conventions).
- **`docs/design/THEME.md`** — the binding UI guide: tokens, typography, primitives, layout, popups, the entity-modal system and the hook/query/mutation conventions. **Read it before any UI change**; extend it in the same change when something new comes up.
- `docs/` — indexed in `docs/README.md`, in three kinds: **runbooks** (`iphone-examples.md`, `web-push-setup.md`, `scriptable-food-logger.md`, `scriptable-widgets.md`, `health-auto-export/`) that are authoritative for one-time setup steps and must be followed exactly; **live coordination** (`coord/`, `codex-shortcuts.md`) that must be read fresh every session; and **verified reference** (`ai-cost-capability-analysis.md`, `iphone-web-integration.md`, the EnTur Postman collection). For feature/architecture state this file still wins; the runbooks and the coordination logs are **not** "secondary".
- **Backlog** = the in-app Dev Requests drawer (`dev_requests` table), not a markdown list. This file's "Not done yet" is a coarse long-term list only.
- **Cross-AI task board** = `docs/codex-shortcuts.md`.

---

## Session Workflow

Runs on **Claude Code on the web** (claude.ai/code). Container clones repo fresh each session — git is the only memory.

```
1. User describes task
2. Claude branches: claude/<descriptive-name>
3. Code → npm run build → commit → push → draft PR
4. User marks ready → merges → GitHub Actions auto-deploys (~1 min)
```

- Never push directly to `main`
- Update this file when features complete or architecture changes
- **Small data migrations/inputs**: the in-app AI (Ask AI panel) has a generic DB layer (`db_query/insert/update/delete`, see AI section). For small one-off data loads (seed rows, backlog items, bulk inserts) just hand it a JSON or SQL-ish spec in chat — it inserts the rows — instead of writing a migration. Reserve SQL migrations for schema changes.
- **Direct Supabase data access (curl/REST with the user's own credentials) is opt-in per request** — only query/inspect live production data when the user explicitly asks for it in that turn (e.g. "check the DB", "look at my data"). Never do this proactively/unprompted, even mid-investigation of a bug — it's the user's personal data.
- **No unit-test framework exists in this repo** (`package.json` ships only `@playwright/test`). Logic verification ships as a throwaway `scripts/*.cjs|.mjs` run through `sucrase` against the real modules — precedent: `scripts/verify-health-source-resolver.cjs` (25 assertions), `scripts/generate-matvaretabellen-seed.mjs`. **Do not introduce vitest/jest as a side effect of an unrelated change.**
- **Dev-server gotcha:** an old vite process holding `:5173` serves a STALE Tailwind config (new plugins silently missing). Kill all vite processes; don't trust the port.

**Live URLs:** App: `https://lasciviens.github.io/Project_Daily/#/login` · Repo: `https://github.com/Lasciviens/Project_Daily`

## Two-AI setup (Claude + Codex)

Two AIs work this repo. **Ownership is exclusive — never edit outside your side.**

- **Claude** = manager: `src/**`, `supabase/**` (functions + migrations), all `docs/**` except the generator readme, PR review.
- **Codex** = owner of **`scripts/iphone-shortcuts/**` and nothing else** (the Apple Shortcuts generator + its README).
- **Durable spec + task board:** `docs/codex-shortcuts.md` (roles, the 6 rules, C-numbered tasks with statuses). Not a conversation.
- **Messages:** two append-only logs — `docs/coord/to-codex.md` (Claude → Codex) and `docs/coord/to-claude.md` (Codex → Claude). Newest entry on TOP, header `### YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM · <task id> · re: <prior|new>`. **Never edit the other side's log**; a new entry in the other log with `re:` pointing at yours IS the reply. Protocol: `docs/coord/README.md`.
- **The one sanctioned exception to the English-only rule:** Turkish is allowed inside generated **on-phone user-facing strings** (Shortcut/widget titles, notification text). Never in prose, comments, commits or docs.

---

## Communication Style (per user request)

The user is learning software engineering terminology as we go (comes from a SAP/ABAP background). When describing what was done, **use the correct technical term and add a short parenthetical explaining what it means** — e.g. "yeni bir **hook** yazdım (component'ler arası paylaşılan, tekrar kullanılabilir mantık — SAP'deki bir class method'una en yakın karşılık)" instead of just describing it in plain words with no term attached. Do this consistently, not just when explicitly asked — it's meant to build vocabulary over many sessions. Don't overdo it into a glossary dump; one clear parenthetical per term, only for terms actually used that turn.

Also: don't narrate routine PR housekeeping (subscribing to PR activity, "checking CI status", "no comments yet") — the user checks PRs themselves and considers this noise. Only speak up about a PR when there's something requiring their input or a genuinely notable outcome (merged, a real CI failure, a review comment needing a decision).

## Stack

| Layer | Choice |
|---|---|
| Framework | React 19 + TypeScript 6 + Vite 8 |
| Routing | `react-router-dom` v7, HashRouter (`/#/route`) — GitHub Pages has no server rewrite |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS v3 (+ `@tailwindcss/container-queries`); semantic tokens as CSS variables (`bg-surface`, `text-fg-muted`, `border-line`, tones) in `index.css`; accent presets with full 50–950 scales (`shared/theme/accent.ts`, **blue** default); Inter Variable bundled (`@fontsource-variable/inter`). Rules: `docs/design/THEME.md` |
| State (UI) | Zustand (`src/app/store.ts`) |
| State (server) | TanStack Query v5 |
| DB | Supabase (Postgres + Auth + RLS + Edge Functions) |
| Hosting | GitHub Pages via GitHub Actions |
| E2E / verification | `@playwright/test` (no unit-test framework — see Session Workflow) |

UI primitives: `src/shared/ui` (Card, Button, PageContainer/PageHeader, **PageBoard**, StatTile, Tone, Truncate, AnimatedNumber, ProgressRing…) on `@headlessui/react` v2 (Combobox, Popover, Menu; Dialog only inside `ModalShell`/`SideDrawer`); `@floating-ui/react` (explicit dependency) powers `<Truncate>`'s tooltip and bubble. Charts: `recharts` + `useChartColors`. No animation library, no shadcn/ui, no SSR, no AI keys in client code.

## Platform & Shell

**PWA / service worker (real bug, fixed):** `vite-plugin-pwa`'s `registerType: 'autoUpdate'` does NOT auto-update the way the name implies — the auto-injected `registerSW.js` is a bare `navigator.serviceWorker.register(...)` with no update-detection logic. A new service worker installs but sits **waiting** until every open tab is fully closed; a hard refresh does NOT activate it, because a hard refresh bypasses the HTTP cache, not an already-controlling service worker. A shipped fix was invisible to the user even after a successful deploy + hard refresh. Fixed: `vite.config.ts` sets `injectRegister: false` + `workbox: { skipWaiting: true, clientsClaim: true }`, and `src/main.tsx` calls `registerSW({ immediate: true })` from `virtual:pwa-register` (types in `src/vite-env.d.ts`) — checks for updates immediately (and periodically) and reloads automatically, so deploys actually reach the browser.

**App shell** — `src/app/shell/` (`layout.tsx` is gone). `AppShell` (one tree for every breakpoint, so rotation/resize never remounts the page) mounts `AppSidebar` (232px with group labels from 1280px, a 68px icon rail from 768–1279px) + `AppTopBar` (route title, search trigger, Ask AI, Requests, `SettingsMenu`) on tablet/desktop, `MobileHeader` + `BottomTabBar` + `MoreSheet` on phones (< 768px or a landscape phone — `useBreakpoint()`), plus `DevRequestsDrawer`, `AIPanel` (both on `SideDrawer`: right edge, a draggable bottom sheet on phones), `CommandBar` and the one `ModalHost`. On desktop the sidebar has a footer toggle (Collapse/Expand sidebar), saved in `useSidebarStore` (`app-sidebar`); a registry entry flagged `collapseSidebar` (Games) opens with it folded every time, and the toggle there doesn't change the saved preference. Toasts sit right of the rail whenever it is folded. **`src/app/navigation.ts` is the ONE nav registry** — sidebar, header titles, tab bar, More sheet, command bar and Dev Requests' page list all read it; adding a page = one entry there. `useAppBootstrap()` holds app-wide side effects (today: the Calendar token refresh). A route change resets `main.scrollTop` (restores it on Back/POP), resets the chrome and **closes the whole popup stack**. **The shell's title is the ONLY visible page name** — `PageHeader`'s `title` is a screen-reader-only h1 at every width (`showTitle` only when the title is content, e.g. Daily's date nav); with no visible title, children and actions share the first row (THEME.md §6.2). Each bullet below is a device-verified gotcha:
- **The document NEVER scrolls** — `html/body/#root` are fixed-height (index.css) and `<main data-app-scroller>` (AppShell) is THE scroll container. This is what made custom pull-to-refresh reliable on a real iPhone: with the document as scroller, iOS decides gesture ownership on the FIRST touchmove — one uncancelled move commits the root rubber-band and flips `e.cancelable` false for the rest of the gesture, so the pull only worked when the first move happened to be cleanly downward.
- `usePullToRefresh` has a slop + axis-lock state machine (horizontal swipes on snap strips are never hijacked; pull distance re-anchors at commit) and an `isReady` state (indicator flips to solid accent = "release to refresh").
- **Tab navigation slides directionally:** BottomTabBar passes `forward`/`back` (tab order) → `useViewTransitionNav` stamps `data-vt-dir` on `<html>` → slide keyframes in index.css. The chrome stays put while content slides because each bar has its OWN view-transition name (`.vt-pin-header` / `-topbar` / `-sidebar` / `-tabbar` — names must be unique per element, or the transition aborts).
- **View Transitions don't exist on pre-18 iOS Safari** — there, AppShell keys the Outlet wrapper by pathname and plays a `.page-in` rise (universal fallback, else navigation is INSTANT = "animations aren't there" on the primary device); with VT support the key stays constant (no double animation). That wrapper is `h-full` ONLY on routes flagged `fullHeight` in the registry (today `/shop` and `/games`) — everywhere else it must stay auto-height or tall pages overflow the fixed box and swallow main's bottom padding (last card pinned under the tab bar; real regression, caught by E2E).
- **ALL view-transition CSS lives OUTSIDE `@layer`** — same Tailwind purge gotcha as the recharts rules; the original crossfade rule inside `@layer base` was silently purged from every build.
- `.stagger-in` (mobile-only ≤639px) cascades a container's children in with 30ms-stepped delays — HomePage, Daily's glance board and TasksPanel, Food, MediaPage.
- **BottomTabBar is a flat, full-width glass bar** (`h-tabbar` = `--app-tabbar-h` 64px + safe area; active tab = `text-accent-600` icon + label, no chip) — on phones `<main>` reserves `pb-[calc(var(--app-tabbar-h)+env(safe-area-inset-bottom)+16px)]`. `MoreSheet` lists the `more` pages (`moreFirst` entries — Wishes — on top), then Appearance/Accent/Display size/Notifications/Subscriptions/Sign out. `MobileHeader` hides on scroll-down, returns on any upward scroll and gains a shadow once scrolled (`chromeHidden`/`chromeScrolled` in `useUIStore`).
- **`.glass-chrome`** (index.css, `@layer components` — safe there because the class name appears in source, unlike runtime-only selectors) gives the phone header + tab bar their glass: `cream-50/0.82` + `backdrop-filter: blur(18px) saturate(160%)`, with an `@supports not` near-opaque (0.97) fallback. **Write only the standard `backdrop-filter`** — autoprefixer emits the `-webkit-` Safari needs; hand-writing both made the minifier drop the standard one. The glass frosts because `<main>` scrolls BEHIND the chrome.
- **iOS input auto-zoom is disabled app-wide** (real bug: tapping the AI chat box zoomed the page and the zoom stuck after blur, misaligning the frame — iOS zooms whenever a focused text control's font-size is <16px): `maximum-scale=1` in the viewport meta (pinch-zoom still works — iOS ignores max-scale for pinch since iOS 10) + a `@media (pointer: coarse)` rule forcing all text-entry controls to `font-size: 16px !important`. This removes the trigger itself — never "fix" this per-field.
- Touch polish: `-webkit-tap-highlight-color: transparent` + `touch-action: manipulation`, press-scale on buttons/interactive cards, `animate-tabPop` on the active tab.
- iOS PWA is edge-to-edge (`black-translucent` status bar + safe-area top padding on the header); `theme-color` matches the canvas (`#F3F5F9` light / `#06090F` dark). Pages sizing to the viewport use `min-h-full` — never `min-h-[calc(100vh-56px)]`.
- **Back closes only the top overlay** (`useHistoryDismiss`, built into `ModalShell`/`SideDrawer` and used by the Games page's sheets): open overlays form a stack, only the top one reacts to a Back, and an overlay closed by other means drops its own history entry with a Back that nothing else treats as a Back. (Before, closing a sheet over a ScreenScraper review also closed the review, and opening a sheet while another overlay unmounted could close the new one.) Known gap: popups closed by a route change leave their history entries behind, so Back may need an extra press.
- **iOS standalone short-viewport bug (reported: the tab bar floated ~a status bar above the screen bottom on every page):** in the installed PWA the layout viewport can be short by the top safe area, so `100dvh` and `fixed bottom-0` stop early. `app/iosViewportFix.ts` (standalone only) sets `--ios-viewport-gap` = screen height − the height a `position: fixed; inset: 0` probe actually covers (0–80px, else 0; re-measured on resize, return from background and `pageshow`). `innerHeight` was tried first and is wrong: it can stay short while fixed layout is already full height, which pushed the bar below the screen; `#root` adds it to its height and the phone tab bar (`BottomTabBar`) sits at `bottom: calc(-1 * gap)`. A no-op in the browser. Not yet confirmed on a device.
- **CommandBar (⌘K) has a phone trigger**: `MobileHeader`'s Search icon button (⌘K can't be typed on a phone).
- **Motion test set** (THEME §7): card lift, count-ups, ring sweeps, check pop, row arrival and toast fade — always on (the Animations setting was removed 2026-09-29), never with reduced motion (a global reduced-motion rule also stops the base slides; JS motion checks `useMotion()`).

**iOS platform limits — hard constraints for anything "while the app is closed"** (full rationale: `docs/iphone-web-integration.md`):
- **No background execution at all.** PWAs get no Background/Periodic Sync; Shortcuts has no daemon. Unattended runs need a trigger (time/location/NFC) and **iOS forces a visible banner on every auto-run automation**. Server→phone delivery must go through Web Push.
- **iOS Web Push:** home-screen-**installed PWA only** (never a Safari tab), iOS 16.4+, direct-tap permission grant, and **every push must display a visible notification** (no silent data push). Delivery is best-effort and may be minutes late.
- **Web Share Target is unsupported on iOS** — "share a photo into the app to log it" would need a native Share Extension (a real App Store app).
- **Scriptable widgets:** refresh budget ≈40–70/day, ≥5 min apart, iOS decides the real cadence; a **Small widget has ONE tap target** and cannot run code in place — a tap opens a deep link (the `HizliLog` runner pattern).

## Routes

```
/#/login          → LoginPage (public)
/#/reset-password → ResetPasswordPage (public — reachable only via Supabase's recovery email link or LoginPage's "Forgot password?")
/#/home       → HomePage
/#/daily      → DailyPage    (nav: "Personal" — Daily ONLY, no in-header group tabs)
/#/recipes    → RecipesPage  (nav: "Food" — top-level entry, renders FoodTabs)
/#/shop       → ShopPage     (reached from the Food nav entry, renders FoodTabs)
/#/media      → MediaPage
/#/work       → WorkPage
/#/projects   → ProjectsPage
/#/training   → TrainingPage
/#/health     → HealthPage   (Apple Health; a Training tab until 2026-09-27)
/#/games      → TestGamePage (inside AppShell, fullHeight; ?section=&platform= in the address)
/#/games-legacy → redirects to /#/games (the old Games page was deleted 2026-09-26; /#/games-demo and /#/games-cover-demo redirect too)
/#/wishes     → WishesPage   (reached from the phone More sheet — the 5 tab-bar slots are full — and the sidebar)
/#/developer  → DeveloperPage (sidebar footer, More sheet, Settings ⚙ menu)
/#/settings   → SettingsPage  (avatar menu, More sheet, sidebar footer; tabs Places · Appearance · Subscriptions · Integrations and APIs; old `?tab=integrations` opens Subscriptions)
/#/test-game  → redirects to /#/games (its name while under test)
```

Everything except `/login` and `/reset-password` is protected by `SessionGuard` in `src/app/router.tsx`. No `/football` route — Football is deferred (see Features).

**Nav grouping** (all in `src/app/navigation.ts`; routes are stable so deep links never break): sidebar groups **Overview** (Home) · **Life** (Personal, Food, Training, Health, Wishes) · **Media & play** (Media, Games) · **Work** (Work, Projects), Developer in the sidebar footer. Phone tab bar = 5 slots (Home · Personal · **Food** · Media · Training) + More; **Wishes leads the More sheet** (`moreFirst`), Health follows it (a wish list that has to be hunted for is dead in three weeks). Shop is a sub-page of Food (`parent: 'food'`, `match` lights Food on `/shop`) — no nav row of its own; `PersonalLayout.tsx` only exports **`FoodTabs`** (a Food | Shop segmented control rendered in both pages' header), the old route wrapper and its inner scroller are gone. Daily renders no group tabs.

**Password reset (real bug, fixed):** the app had no recovery flow — Supabase's reset email redirected back with a recovery token, but nothing read it or showed a "set new password" form, so the link silently did nothing (a very common gap — see the `gotrue-js`/`auth-js` issues about apps not handling `PASSWORD_RECOVERY`). Fixed: LoginPage's "Forgot password?" → `requestPasswordReset()` (`security/supabaseClient.ts`) calls `supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail(email, { redirectTo })` pointing at `#/reset-password`; `ResetPasswordPage` listens for the `PASSWORD_RECOVERY` auth event (plus a session-check fallback in case the event fires before the listener attaches). **One-time manual step:** the redirect URL (GitHub Pages URL + `http://localhost:5173/#/reset-password`) must be added to Supabase Dashboard → Authentication → URL Configuration → Redirect URLs, or Supabase silently refuses to send the token.

## Features

| Feature | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Auth | ✅ | LoginPage + password recovery (`/reset-password`, see Routes) |
| Daily + To-Do | ✅ | See Daily section below. Four modes (Day/Week/Month/Tasks) + a glance board. |
| Food | ✅ | See Food section below. Diary + plan in one table, coaching, barcode/online search, water. Nav: top-level "Food" (`/recipes`), holds Shop. |
| Shop | ✅ | See Shop section below. Reached from the Food nav entry (`FoodTabs`). |
| Media | ✅ | See Media section below |
| Work | ✅ | **Command-center layout** on PageBoard (`workBoard.ts`), flowing and scrolling in `<main>`: a sticky translucent header (date, done/active/overdue status pills, New task, rail toggle) → `FocusStrip` (focused tasks, horizontal snap-scroll) → collapsible `OverdueStrip` (overdue is a property, not a column — except in_progress tasks, which stay on the board) → `WorkToolbar` (Board/List toggle, persisted, + quick-add + search + priority filter; search and priority wrap as one group) → `WorkBoard` (4 explicit columns from `lg`, each at most 28rem, per-column scroll + DnD; stacked collapsible below `lg`) or `WorkListView` (dense triage rows, status-cycle dot, overdue-first sort). `WorkSidebar` (Today/Notes/This week/Pinned links) counts its columns by its own width (1 · 2 from 36rem · 4 from 60rem): a band under the board on phones, tablets and 1280/1469 laptops, a column beside it from 1920 (the board spans 2 tracks at 1920, 3 at 2450). List view keeps the list in the 56rem main track with the rail beside it from the laptop (two tracks, 2×2, from 1920), stops at three tracks and ignores "Hide side panel" — that toggle exists only in Board view from 1920 and gives the rail's track back to the board. The command bar stops where the board does (`workHeaderCapRem`). Status/priority/due tones and helpers in `components/workMeta.ts`, shared chips in `WorkTaskBits.tsx`. There is no live clock and no Now/Next timeline (`WorkDayTimeline` was removed on request; `useTimeBlocks` still feeds Daily's `DayAgenda`). |
| AI | ✅ | See AI section below. Gemini 3.5 Flash (+3-model fallback) via `ai-proxy`; generic DB tool layer, vision, semantic search, aggregation, memory. |
| Calendar | ✅ | Google OAuth (unified consent, see `calendar-oauth`), read + write events. Sync/refresh button in the Daily agenda header. Two-way block↔event link — see Key Patterns. |
| Games | ✅ | See Games section below. Migration `089` — `games`/`game_platforms` moved off the separate RP5 Supabase project into this app's own database. Full-width page (deliberate exception) — the page itself is the "Games page" row below (bookcase / grid / list, Play Queue with drag, Add game, Needs review, Analytics). Steam and PlayStation games sit on their own shelves and under Advanced. Steam (`steam-api` edge function, no local table) and PlayStation (`psn-api` edge function + `psn_tokens`, migration `091`) are both wired to real APIs — Steam is stable; PlayStation is explicitly best-effort/unverified (Sony has no official API). See Games Feature Detail. **Data import from RP5 not yet done — see Pending manual steps.** **ES-DE library import (in progress, 2026-09-15):** the RP6 handheld pushes its EmulationStation-DE library to the new `esde-sync` edge function (migration `093` adds the `(user_id, esde_system, esde_path)` key it upserts against, plus per-variant play-stat columns — 089's `games.esde_*` trio is the ROLL-UP across a game's variants, which was documented before it was computable: a push carries one variant and the others' contributions were nowhere on record). **Order is import first, scrape second** — a real export was measured at **1150 games across 24 systems, 1125 of them scrapeable** once launcher shortcuts and macOS AppleDouble sidecars are excluded, with metadata already **97-98 % filled** because ES-DE has scraped this library before. So the import alone lands a working library, and ScreenScraper is left with only the ~2 % gap plus artwork; scraping first would mean matching against an empty database. The device-side reader/pusher is **Codex's** (task C7); Claude owns the migration, the function and the contract. Full record incl. the measured export, ScreenScraper's real premium limits and the payload contract: `docs/games/screenscraper-integration.md`. |
| Training | ✅ | See Training section below. Tabs Next · Program · Progress · Log · Library · Coach; Hevy + Strava + AI PT Coach. |
| Health | ✅ | `/health`, `src/features/health/`. Apple Health (Health Auto Export) + smart scale + Hevy body data, science-based reference ranges, vitals reading, Goal progress (cut / maintain / gain). See "Health page" under the Training section. |
| Projects | ✅ | Phases, items, status tracking. Add/edit share one popup (`ProjectItemModal`, opened as the `project-item` entity modal) — title/notes/phase/type/status/priority. Colours per enum (and each project's own colour, from the chart palette) live in `projectTones.ts`. The detail is a PageBoard (`projectBoard.ts`): header card (capped at 56rem), view controls and phases in main (spanning 2 tracks at 1920 and 3 at 2450), notes + activity in the last track. Phases are dealt into column stacks by index (`PhaseColumns`: phase i → column i mod N; two columns from 61rem of their own width, three from 92rem, so a card stays ≥ 30rem), each sized/collapsed independently. Board view's three status columns stop at 28rem each; at 2450 the work spans two tracks (`PROJECT_KANBAN_BOARD`). The project list is a collection (auto-fill 19–22rem cards) whose header stops over the last card (`projectsHeaderCapRem`). |
| Wishes | ✅ | See Wishes section below. `wish_items` (migration `069`) — things to do and places to go, scoped to a **reminder period that is never a deadline**. `/wishes` + a resurfacing row in Daily's `DayView` + a once-per-season `push-send` segment. |
| iPhone surface | ✅ | See iPhone section below. `phone-gateway` (12 actions) + 11 Apple Shortcuts + a Scriptable food logger + 4 widgets. |
| Web Push | ✅ | See iPhone section below. Migration `068` + `push-send` + pg_cron morning brief + `public/push-sw.js`; toggle in Settings (⚙) → Notifications. |
| Dev Requests | ✅ | **The live backlog.** A `SideDrawer` opened from the Requests button in the top bar / phone header; jot bugs/features/improvements/integration ideas/long-term wishes about the app itself. `dev_requests` (migration `042`, owner-only RLS, `user_id DEFAULT auth.uid()`): title, description, `page` (a dropdown from the nav registry — `PAGE_CHOICES` in `devRequestMeta.ts`; an old free-text value stays selectable), `category`, `priority`, `status` (open/in_progress/done/dismissed), `effort`, `sort_order` (a new request goes on top, min − 1). See **Dev Requests Feature Detail**. |
| Developer | ✅ | Standalone `/developer` (sidebar footer, More sheet, Settings ⚙), pill tabs synced to `?tab=`. Tabs: **Connections** (see Connections below), **Memory** (see AI), **Activity** (CRUD audit trail — `audit_logs`, written by DB triggers, migration `037`; filters by table/op/actor/days, 30-day retention swept probabilistically inside the trigger) and **Errors** (`app_error_logs`), plus a "Reindex AI search" button. Activity, Errors and Memory are list + detail (`developerBoards.ts` → `LIST_DETAIL_BOARD`): phones and tablets open a row in place (Memory: its edit sheet); from the laptop toolbar + list sit in main and the picked row opens in a sticky pane beside them (two tracks from 1920; it stops at three). `paneSelection` keeps the picked row, else the first listed row; Activity shows no pane for an empty list; Memory asks before discarding an unsaved pane edit. The pane is `ActivityLogDetail` (the diff), `ErrorLogRows`' `ErrorDetail` (message + context with copy buttons) or `MemoryDetail` (an inline editor sharing `MemoryFields` with the sheet); the picked row is `bg-accent-50` + `aria-current`. The header stops over the tab's board. Audit triggers cover user-authored tables only — bulk-synced tables (hevy_*/health_*/strava) are deliberately excluded to avoid sync-spam; `actor` distinguishes 'web' (browser session) from 'service' (AI/webhooks, service-role writes). Activity is a **timeline** (`ActivityLogRows.tsx`, tones for created/updated/deleted): a plain-language sentence per change, same-transaction cascades grouped, a readable diff on expand. Both log tabs read through `developer/hooks/useLogs.ts` and confirm "Clear all" first. |
| Home | ✅ | See Home section below. Rule-based daily brief, weather, transit, currency, news, media, games, training, projects, glance board. |
| Command Bar | ✅ | `CommandBar` (⌘K) via `useUIStore.openCommandBar` |
| Dark Mode | ✅ | Light/Dark/System (+ accent swatches) in `SettingsMenu.tsx` and the phone More sheet → `useThemeStore` (Zustand + persist, key `theme-preference`; `version: 1` moved the accent in from its own `accent-theme` key and reset everyone to the **blue** default once). `tailwind.config.js` (`darkMode: 'selector'`) resolves every token (semantic `surface`/`fg`/`line`/tones plus the legacy `canvas`/`cream`/`ink` aliases) through `rgb(var(--x) / <alpha-value>)`; `index.css` defines cool slate light values (`:root`) and dark overrides (`:root.dark`) once, so token classes are theme-aware with zero per-file branching. The accent's light/dark scales are inline vars on `<html>` (`applyAccent`, re-applied after every `.dark` toggle). An inline script in `index.html` stamps `.dark` synchronously from the persisted value (no flash-of-wrong-theme); a `matchMedia` listener keeps `'system'` live. Charts read `useChartColors()` (SVG can't read CSS vars); **identity colours** (sleep stages, Strava orange, transit lines, Activity Rings) stay fixed literals. Details: `docs/design/THEME.md` §2. `version: 3` dropped the old `motion` field (Animations setting removed; the motion set is always on). |
| Games page | ✅ | `/#/games` — the Game Library design, inside AppShell, with its own look (`testGame.css`): a collapsible navigation panel (sections + every platform grouped by maker, no Others fold), vertical bookcase, overlay detail panel, Analytics with four tabs, phone grid; `?section=&platform=` in the address. The old page was deleted 2026-09-26 once every feature was ported or recorded as deliberately not ported (`docs/games/test-game-known-issues.md`); its addresses redirect. Code lives in `src/features/games/test-game/`. See Games Feature Detail → Test-Game page. |
| Football | ⛔ | Deferred — no route wired, and the `football-api` edge function is no longer in the repo. API-Football free tier stops at 2024. Future: fixtures from Google Calendar. |

### Daily Feature Detail

`DailyPage.tsx` (`src/features/daily/pages/`) — `PageContainer width="wide"` + `PageHeader`: the date arrows sit in the title with the greeting/time as subtitle, and ONE `SegmentedControl` switches periods. **Four modes** (`type Mode = 'day' | 'week' | 'month' | 'tasks'`); the Yesterday/Today/Tomorrow highlight is DERIVED from `viewDate` (the three old near-identical day views are one `DaySection`). Desktop tabs: Yesterday · Today · Tomorrow · Week · Month · Tasks; mobile collapses to a 4-cell segmented control (Today · Week · Month · Tasks).

- **Day mode is a PageBoard** (`dailyBoards.ts` → `DAY_BOARD`): phones/tablets and 1280/1469 stack the schedule card then the glance board; 1920 puts the card across main + one side with **`DayQuickRail`** (quick actions, up to 3 next blocks, an entry into Tasks) beside it and the glance board below; 2450 puts the glance board beside the schedule (3 columns) and the rail under it as one strip. The schedule card = `WeekStrip` (7 fixed day cells for the viewed week, chevrons browse without changing viewDate, cell click = view that day; the full `WeekWidget` lives under the Week tab) → `DayAgenda bare` | `DayView`, side by side once the card is 54rem wide (a landscape phone stacks); from 72rem the panes are capped at 40rem and 36rem.
- **`TodaySummary` (glance board)** — shown for EVERY viewed day, not just today. Columns follow the board's own width (`@container`): 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 at 36 / 55 / 90rem; each module is its own `Card`. **Nutrition is a double slot**; from three columns it is two rows tall (three rows at four), so the small cards stack beside it instead of stretching (3 columns: N N T / N N W / H G S; 4 columns: N N T W / N N H G / N N S ·). Where the board sits beside the schedule (`DAY_BOARD` step 4, `glanceInColumn`) its heading is visually hidden. **NO auto-fill anywhere and explicit column counts only** — the user requires boxes never to change position based on content/size (verified by E2E: cell x/w identical between a full and an empty day). Cells share `summary/cellKit.tsx` anatomy (Cell / CellHeader icon-chip / CellLink). An empty module collapses to a compact row IN PLACE; it never leaves its slot. Cards: **NutritionCard** (double slot, see below), **TrainingCard** (planned/done; on a rest day it lists Hevy routines INLINE and plans one through the `time-block` popup with its `training_session` source, prefilled for the viewed date), **WatchNextCard** (chip-switch between watching/paused series, real next-unwatched episode from `useNextEpisode`, SxxEyy + title + watched count + future-air-date badge, Watched (`useMarkEpisodeWatched`) auto-advances, Plan opens the `time-block` popup with the episode on its source), **HealthCard** (swipeable sleep/steps/energy/heart/weight strip reusing the Health aggregation), **GamesCard**, **ShopCard**. Work + Projects cards were removed (they have their own pages).
- **`NutritionCard` = Nutrition + Meals merged into one card** (the separate `summary/MealsCard.tsx` was deleted). It carries: the meal timeline (slots Breakfast · Lunch · Dinner · Snack · Suppl., each with its emoji as content, current slot highlighted on today; planned rows muted/italic) with per-row edit (`food-log-edit` / `meal-plan` popup) / confirm-planned / delete icon buttons, per-slot **free-text quick-add** (live data showed usage is ~100% custom titles; an exact/start-of-name library match auto-logs with real macros, a loose match opens the `food-log` popup with `query` prefilled), recent-food chips (`useRecentFoods`, diary-sourced, re-log their own snapshot), copy-yesterday into empty slots, the calorie ring + protein/macros, `GoalSummary` (Edit goal opens the `day-targets` popup), and **`WaterTracker`**.
- **`DayAgenda`** replaced the old 24h×52px pixel-grid `DayTimeline` (deleted): dense agenda rows (time · duration · title · lucide recurring / calendar / overlap / spillover badges, block colour tags mapped to tones; +30m/+1d/rename/delete quick actions behind a ⋯ toggle), a Night group for blocks before 06:00, a now-marker (the "Nh free · +" gap rows were removed on request), NULL-`start_time` blocks in a "No time set" group (real data has them), empty-day quick-add chips (Morning/Afternoon/Evening). Every editor opens through `useEntityModal` (`time-block` to add or edit a one-off row, `task` for a task-linked row, `schedule-block` for recurring, `EditCalendarEventModal` — edit-only, on `ModalShell` — for Google events); linked-task notes come from `useTasksByIds`. Drag-to-reschedule was dropped with the grid.
- **Week and Month** share `PICKER_SECTIONS`: picker (40rem — `WeekWidget` / `MonthWidget big`) + the picked day in place — one pane (picked day or Upcoming) at 1280/1469, the picked day's schedule + Upcoming at 1920, plus that day's tasks ("Tasks · <day>") at 2450; on phones/tablets the week strip opens the day, on wider pages it picks in place. The picked-day card shortens its date when it has two buttons; Daily's header is capped to the mode's board width (`boardWidthRem`). **Tasks mode** = `TasksPanel` (all tasks): one card on phones, columns on wider pages — overdue · open now · today | upcoming | no date | recently done; an empty column keeps its heading with a 0 badge.
- **Cancel** (⊘, `ToDoItem`/`WorkTaskCard`) sets `status='cancelled'` — `TaskStatus` always had the value but no UI ever set it; distinct from Delete (keeps a record instead of destroying the row). `DayView` has a "Cancelled" section (same 24h window as "Done") so a cancelled task stays visible; the Work board has no cancelled column, so there it simply drops off (matches the "cancelled excluded from active counts" convention used everywhere — WeekWidget/MonthWidget/HomePage/…).
- **`ToDoItem`'s desktop action row (✎/↳/📋/⊘/✕) and the checkbox both silently no-opped into opening the edit modal instead — a real bug in `useSwipeToReveal.ts`, not in `ToDoItem` itself.** `onPointerDown` called `e.currentTarget.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId)` **unconditionally**, on every pointerdown anywhere inside the row — including one that started on a nested interactive child (pointerdown bubbles up regardless of which descendant it began on). Once a pointer is captured, the browser retargets that pointer's subsequent `pointerup` **and the `click` it synthesizes** to the CAPTURING element (the row), never wherever the pointer physically is — so a plain tap on the checkbox or any of the five action buttons fired the ROW's own `onClick` (open edit) instead of the button's own handler: the checkbox never toggled, Delete never deleted, Cancel/Reopen never updated status, and every action looked identical from the outside ("everything opens edit"). The mobile `⋯` menu was unaffected because Headless UI's `MenuItems` portals outside the row's DOM subtree, so its clicks never bubble through this handler at all — this made the bug desktop-only, exactly where it was reported. Fixed by deferring `setPointerCapture` out of `onPointerDown` and into `onPointerMove`, firing only once a genuine horizontal swipe is confirmed (`axisLocked.current === 'x'`) — a plain tap never moves enough to reach that branch, so its click reaches its real target untouched; only an actual swipe gesture (which still needs capture to survive the pointer leaving the row's bounds mid-gesture) ever captures the pointer. `ToDoItem` opens its editor as the `task` popup (rows no longer each mount a hidden editor), keeps its action menus in `ToDoItemActions.tsx`, and toasts nothing itself — the migrated task hooks own error feedback (Delete adds "Deleted" via `withProgress`).
- **The `section` day-leak (real bug, fixed on the READ side only).** `useDayData` maps a whole RANGE of day-offsets onto ONE `section` value (+2…+7 → `this_week`, everything else → `backlog`), and `fetchTasksForDay`'s non-today arm carried `due_date.is.null` — so **one** undated `this_week` row rendered on all six of those days, and **one** undated `backlog` row claimed yesterday *and* every far-future day. The entire fix is one clause: only `section === 'today'` keeps `due_date.is.null,due_date.lte.X` (an undated row parked in Today IS today's work, and an open overdue task must still surface there); every other day view is now `due_date.eq.X` exactly. **No row was rewritten and `section` was not dropped** — a drop is a 4-unit break (`037`'s audit list, `046`'s policy list, `DB_CATALOG`) for zero gain, and **both backfills are verifiably worse than nothing**: `→'backlog'` relocates rows onto an unbounded date range (the same bug, wider), `→due_date=today` manufactures deadlines that go **red tomorrow** out of rows that were never deadlines. Nothing lost its home: undated rows still reach `fetchAllTasks` → `TasksPanel`'s "No date" group, and undated `this_week` rows still reach `fetchTasksByWeek`'s untouched section arm → `DayView`'s "This week — no date" band. `TaskTab`'s `SECTIONS` also dropped the `Tomorrow`/`This Week` pills (neither has a home of its own — picking one only made a task float like Backlog under a date-shaped name; a row already holding one keeps a "(legacy)" pill so the value stays visible and one tap moves it off). **`planForm.ts`'s `section: 'today'` default must NOT be changed** — four server-side `section.eq.today` predicates (`phone-gateway` ×2, `push-send`, `ai-proxy`) drain silently if it becomes `'inbox'`, quietening the brief and the lock-screen push forever.

### Wishes Feature Detail

`/#/wishes` → `WishesPage` (`src/features/wishes/`). A **wish** is something you want to do, or a place you want to go — "go to the hytte this winter", "things to do in Polatlı" — carrying a **reminder period**. The period decides only when the app brings the wish up first; **it is never a deadline and never a visibility rule, so a wish is never late** (see Coding Rules → `NEVER_HIDES`).

- **Its own table — `wish_items`** (migration `069`: owner-only RLS, `trg_audit`, `trg_wish_items_updated_at → update_updated_at()`, `(user_id, period_start)` index): title, notes, `kind` `thing|place`, `period_start`/`period_end`, `period_label` (the user's own word for the window), `city`/`country`/`url` (meaningful when `kind='place'` — the same one-table variant shape `shop_items` uses for its own), `priority`, `status` `idea|planned|done|dropped`, `promoted_task_id`, `sort_order`. Periods are **concrete dates**: no MM-DD, no season enum (a 5-value CHECK can't say "*this* spring, Italy", its `'anytime'` default IS a junk drawer, and CHECK values are duplicated into `ai-proxy`'s catalog string → a redeploy per new period word), and **no generated `is_open` column ever** — `CURRENT_DATE` is STABLE, not IMMUTABLE, so Postgres rejects it in a generation expression. Migration 069 **updates not one existing row**.
- **Not `tasks`:** every one of the five `section` values leaks. An undated row matches `fetchTasksForDay`'s `due_date.is.null` arm and `fetchTasksByWeek`'s unbounded `.eq('section','this_week')` arm, and four server-side `section.eq.today` predicates would put "hytte this winter" on the **lock screen in July**. The obvious repair — a `kind` discriminator filtered OUT of those queries — is forbidden twice: it removes rows (`NEVER_HIDES`), and `ai-proxy`'s `db_query` composes **model-supplied** filters over `select('*')`, so no server-side code can enforce it anyway. **Not `shop_items`:** `category_id` is `NOT NULL` and Daily's `ShopCard` filters `status === 'wishlist'` with **no** category filter, so a place-wish would advertise itself in the Shopping glance cell. Buy vs do: "buy ski gear this winter" is a `shop_item`, "go skiing this winter" is a `wish_item`.
- **Promotion leaves the wish alive.** `+ Plan it` opens the `task` popup with `defaults={{ title }}`; `onSaved` always sets `status='planned'` and adds `promoted_task_id` **only when the save produced a `result.taskId`** (a schedule-only save with no linked task leaves the column null rather than storing a fake id), then refreshes the wish list (the task hook already refreshes tasks). The task is the commitment, the wish is the memory — the card then reads "→ scheduled". No trigger and no auto-close: a second completion signal beside `tasks.status` is the documented `user_tv_entries.current_season` mistake. `promoted_task_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL` because `deleteTask` hard-deletes. A wish never passes through `useCreateTask`, so it never reaches Google Tasks.
- **"Passed" is grey, never red.** Page groups, in reading order: Open now · Coming up · Anytime · **Passed**. A wish whose `period_end` went by stays `'idea'` in the grey group under an explicit "nothing here is late" note; nothing auto-closes, nothing resets, and it leaves only when the user ticks or deletes it. `resolveWishWindow` (`wishRules.ts`) returns `open|upcoming|passed|anytime` from lexicographic `yyyy-MM-dd` compares — no `Date`, no timezone. `wishPeriodLabel` prefers `period_label` over the derived range.
- **Capture is the feature.** `WishQuickAdd` is always visible (never behind a "+ New" modal): title → optionally one season chip → Add, and **only the title clears** so a whole season's wishes go in one after another. Notes/place/priority/kind/status live in `WishSheet` (on `ModalShell`, opened as the `wish` popup via `useWish(id)`, seeded once so a background refetch can't overwrite typing) → `WishForm`. Window-state and priority tones live in `wishTones.ts` (open → success, passed → neutral, scheduled → info — never the accent). `WishFilters` (kind + status, default Active) only narrows what is **displayed** — "Everything" always brings the list back.
- **`shared/components/windowChips.tsx`** — the one-tap period picker, shared with the task-window control. `seasonWindows(today)` returns Northern-hemisphere/Oslo windows soonest-first (winter 1 Dec → end of Feb · spring 1 Mar–31 May · summer 1 Jun–31 Aug · autumn 1 Sep–30 Nov); `this <season>` = the season you are currently inside, otherwise the **upcoming** one, so tapping `❄️ This winter` in July stores 1 Dec this year → end of Feb next year (three anchor years are probed to cover a season that began last year). End-of-February is `endOfMonth`, **never a hardcoded `'02-28'`** — a leap year yields 29 Feb. `windowRangeLabel` is en-GB day-first ("1 Dec – 28 Feb"), adding the year to **both** ends only when a bound falls past next year (never a mixed "1 Dec 2029 – 28 Feb", which reads as a typo). `📅 Pick dates…` falls back to two `DateInput`s; `✕ No period` clears.
- **Layout** (`WISH_BOARD`): intro on top; quick add + filters in a sticky left rail; the groups span the rest (cards `minmax(15rem,1fr)`) and pair two side by side once the area is ≥ 100rem.
- **Resurfacing — two places, both purely additive.** (1) `DayView` renders one always-visible link when a period is open (`❄️ This winter · 3 things →`, from `useOpenWishes` — the label comes straight from `wishPeriodLabel`, emoji included; several open periods render `<first> +N more`). It belongs **there**: Daily's day band stacks `WeekStrip → DayAgenda → DayView`, so at 393px it is about one screenful down, while the glance board is 2–3 screenfuls down and `DayQuickRail` is `hidden xl:flex` — **neither is a phone surface, so don't "tidy" the row into them.** (2) `push-send`'s `buildMorning` appends a segment **only on the day `period_start` equals today** — once per season, never a daily nag; it adds a segment and adds no predicate to the tasks query. A missing table there surfaces as `{data:null,error}` (the null-check keeps the brief alive, **not** the `catch`). **`period_label` is stored WITH its season emoji** (`WindowChips` writes `❄️ This winter` verbatim), so anything that prepends its own emoji must strip the leading one first — `push-send`'s `labelText()` does, and a label-less row falls back to `A new period starts today (1 Dec – 28 Feb)` because a bare date range doesn't fit the `<name> starts today` sentence. The rest of that brief is still Turkish (`🌅 Günaydın`, `açık iş`) — sanctioned by the on-phone notification-text exception, so the body currently mixes languages until someone decides to translate the older segments.
- **Pre-migration safe.** `wishesApi.ts` returns `[]` on `42P01`/`PGRST205`/"Could not find the table" for **reads** (the `waterApi.ts` precedent), but a **write** throws a named "migration 069 not applied" error — a silent no-op would look exactly like data loss. `useWishes` and `useOpenWishes` share ONE query key `qk.wishes.all` (`['wish-items']`) and one `queryFn`, narrowed with `select`, so mounting both surfaces costs one request. Every mutation uses `useMutationWithFeedback`.
- **AI:** `wish_items` is `rw` in `ai-proxy`'s `DB_CATALOG` for the same reason `dev_requests` is — the user dictates wishes in chat, and the Siri `AI'a Sor` path runs `surface:'general'` with the **full** tool set, so one flat `db_insert` is the app's best capture channel — and it is in `EMBED_SOURCES` for `semantic_search` (needs one Developer → "Reindex AI search" click after the redeploy — embeddings are not written on insert). Its `rules` carry the "a period is a reminder window, never a deadline; never compute lateness, never auto-close on `period_end`" guardrail plus the **four-way** tie-break between the "remember this" targets: `dev_requests` (about this app) · `ai_memory` (a durable fact about the user) · `shop_items` (to buy) · `wish_items` (to do / a place to go). `ai_memory`, `shop_items` and `save_memory` gained the symmetric pointer back. Client-side, `aiApi.buildContext` adds a **`WISHES`** block (open windows then upcoming, each with its period/place/`(already planned)` flag, plus a count of undated ones). It selects only what a line needs, and a passed wish is left out of that **summary** while the block explicitly tells the model to `db_query wish_items` for the rest — a shorter summary, **not** a hidden row (`NEVER_HIDES` is about queries that decide what exists, not about a digest's length).
- **Verified:** `scripts/verify-wish-windows.cjs` (sucrase against the real modules, no test framework — the `verify-health-source-resolver.cjs` precedent) — **42 assertions**, all passing: season-chip math across a year boundary, leap-safe end of February (29 Feb 2028 vs 28 Feb 2027), both inclusive window boundary days, one-sided windows, and en-GB label formatting.

### Dev Requests Feature Detail

**2026-09-29 round:** the floating composer is the ONLY editor (tapping a card opens it; the inline forms and `DevRequestForm.tsx` are gone; status is a select in the composer). The card's circle is a checkbox feeding one selection → action bar (Mark done · Delete · Build prompt). Cards show "Added … · Done …" (`completed_at`, trigger-stamped, migration `114`) and a warn "Prompted" pill once a prompt was built/copied (`prompted_at`). **Description = body + checkpoints + marks** (`devRequestMarks.ts`, all plain text in `description`, no migration): the text box edits only the user's words; **checkpoints** are `- [ ] …` / `- [x] …` lines (`checkpoints.ts`) added with "Add checkpoint" (Enter adds the next, Backspace on an empty one removes it) and ticked in the composer or right on the card (a saved request's tick is written at once, optimistic, and mirrored into its unsaved edit — `useTickCheckpoint`); the card shows "2/5 checkpoints done". **Picks are links in the text (2026-09-29, owner's spec):** Pick on page (or Alt-click / Quote selection) puts a blue underlined link where the caret was ("I want **Water card** to be red") — `LinkedTextEditor` (a contenteditable box; value = plain text with `[[@id]]` tokens, rendered as atomic `<span data-ref>` links; Enter/paste go through the value, DOM helpers in `linkedTextDom.ts`). The link's data is one hidden line `[[pick {id, label, capture}]]` (`insertPickLink`, `linkLabel`: the card a heading names → "Water card", else the control's label, else the humanized component); deleting the link drops its pick on save (`descriptionForSave`). Clicking a link (in the composer or on the card, which shows the text with its links) runs **Go there** (`pick/goToMark.ts`: navigates to the stored route + query, closes the drawer, finds the element by its `data-src` stamps + label, reopens the popups it sat in, outer → inner — an entity popup from its saved request, a local one by clicking what opened it (`shared/modals/popupTrail.ts`: every open `ModalShell` registers its request or its trigger; `readPopupTrail` stores them in the capture's `popups`) — then looks only inside the top popup, scrolls to the spot and gives it a red glowing border that pulses twice, `.goto-blink` in index.css; a popup that can't be reopened is named in a toast and nothing behind it is outlined). The saved page context `[[page {json}]]` stays out of sight. The prompt builder turns each link into `“Water card” [1]` with footnote [1] carrying the full detail (`markPromptText`: component + file, route with query, element, text, value, page tabs, popup, where, screen) and lists checkpoints as numbered sub-points (`1.2 [done] …`). Old requests still render: id-less picks show as rows with Go there, `[Picked …]`/`[Page context]` blocks read as legacy marks (kept verbatim for the prompt), old `1- ` points stay body text (still numbered in the prompt). A Vite plugin (`pick/componentSourceTransform.ts`, `DEV_SRC_STAMP=0` disables) stamps `data-src="src/…/File.tsx#Name"` on every app component's root element (+1.7 % bundle). Older notes below that mention inline forms or status cycling are superseded.

`src/features/devRequests/`. **Nothing typed is ever lost:** `SideDrawer` unmounts its content on close, and the old form kept its text in component state, so a click outside, Esc or Back threw a half-written request away. Every draft now lives in `devRequestDraftStore.ts` — Zustand persisted to localStorage key `lasci.devRequests` (this device only, owner decision; writes debounced 300 ms and flushed on `pagehide`/hidden, every access guarded, read back field by field through `sanitizeDraftState`). It holds the new draft (with the page it was started on), unsaved edits per request (a card shows "Unsaved edits"; Close keeps them, Discard drops them with an Undo toast), the composer, the drawer's filters/sort/"Completed" expansion/open form/pick selection, and the prompt.
- **Drawer:** multi-select category filter + manual/priority sort, inline edit, status cycles open → in_progress → done on click; done items collapse into "Completed (N)" with **Delete all closed** (done + dismissed, confirmed).
- **Floating composer** (`composer/`, mounted by `DevRequestComposerHost` in `router.tsx` beside `<Routes>`, so it survives every navigation; its code loads only once opened). One request (new, or an edit popped out of the drawer) plus the prompt, in **Request | Prompt** tabs. Tablet/desktop: a draggable window (`useFloatingWindow` — title bar drags, kept on screen, re-clamped on resize, Alt+Arrow nudges, double-click resets to the corner, position persisted), minimisable to a pill bottom-right. Phones: docked above the tab bar (lifted above the keyboard), collapsing to a pill. "New" opens it on tablet/desktop; phones get the inline form + a Pop-out button (owner decision). A right-edge drawer (Requests, Ask AI) tucks the window away until it closes; Esc inside it minimises. **Never a Dialog:** it is a direct child of `<body>`, which Headless UI does not make inert, so it stays usable over popups (`z-float` 150) and clicks in it are not "outside" clicks for an open popup.
- **Pick on page** (`pick/usePickMode.ts`, `pickDom.ts`): capture-phase listeners on `window` swallow the picking click before any page handler, drawer backdrop or Headless UI outside-click runs (no overlay element, so hover and scrolling keep working). Desktop: click picks, ↑/↓ widen/narrow, Esc stops, **Alt-click** picks any time the composer is open. Phones: tap selects, then Wider / Use this. **Quote selection** adds the selected text. On save, a new request also gets a page mark (page + query, heading, selected tabs, open popup, screen/breakpoint/theme, bundle name, start time) unless "Attach page context" is unticked. No migration: context is text in `description` (owner decision).
- **Prompt for Claude:** pick requests in the drawer (two-line preview of each description) → Build prompt → the composer's Prompt tab: an editable textarea (edits persist), Copy, Reset to generated, the included requests and Change selection. Building again over an edited prompt asks first.
- **Drag reorder** renumbers the whole list (`planReorder`) and writes only changed rows (the old optimistic update replaced the cache with just the dragged, filtered rows).
- Pure modules `devRequestContext.ts` / `devRequestRules.ts` / `devRequestPrompt.ts` and `clampPosition` (+ `devRequestMarks.ts`, `checkpoints.ts`) are verified by `scripts/verify-dev-request-context.cjs` (218 assertions).

### Games Feature Detail

**RP6 widgets and source preservation (2026-09-16):** [Permanent guide](docs/termux-widgets.md), [10-game audit](docs/games/esde-completeness-audit.md). `sync-esde-content.py` extends the existing game/stat/cover/deletion coordinator with complete parsed game XML and system/folder context, plus original PNG/JPEG/WebP/GIF/BMP assets across media categories. PDFs and videos are excluded. Migration `100` adds owner-scoped `game_platforms.esde_source`, `esde_source_hash`, and `esde_assets`; the service-role-only `esde_save_content` RPC merges assets atomically and prunes references with expected-hash checks. `esde-content-sync` authenticates the device secret before inventory or writes. Curated game metadata and ScreenScraper `media`/`provider_data` remain separate. Original media lives in the existing `game-media` Storage bucket; no new gallery UI is included. Migration and endpoints are deployed; initial bulk original-image upload remains a user-run RP6 widget step. Five widgets cover full sync, all images, source metadata, optimized covers, and a read-only check.

`/#/games` → `test-game/TestGamePage.tsx` (`src/features/games/`; the page itself is described in the Test-Game section below — this section covers the data, the APIs and the provider integrations). A personal retro-game library + backlog tracker (originally built for a Retroid Pocket handheld). **Migration `089` moved `games`/`game_platforms` into this app's own database** — the separate RP5 Supabase project (`bniqmxbtvgwkaoswugds.supabase.co`, repo `Lasciviens/retroid-pocket-5-`) is retired. **The RP6 ES-DE library and optimized covers have been imported.** Termux installation, widget maintenance, source-data coverage and recovery are documented in [the RP6 widget guide](docs/termux-widgets.md).

- **Schema — 2 tables, deliberately down from RP5's 11 tables + 5 views.** `games` (title, release_year, publisher, developer, description, storyline, `genres text[]`, `series_name text`, play_status/tier/rating/play_order/is_coop/is_iconic/play_notes/game_log, `primary_cover_url`, ScreenScraper-shaped fields `age_rating`/`players`/`modes`/`screenshot_url`/`fanart_url`, provider-neutral sync bookkeeping `external_ref`/`external_source` (CHECK: `screenscraper`/`esde`/`manual`)/`synced_at`/`needs_review`, and ES-DE play-stats targets `esde_playcount`/`esde_last_played`/`esde_playtime_seconds`, updated by the ES-DE device sync) + `game_platforms` (one row per game×system×emulator variant — kept as a REAL child table, not a `jsonb` array, specifically so `is_primary_variant` can be DB-enforced via a partial unique index — `game_platforms(game_id) WHERE is_primary_variant`, mirroring `time_blocks.task_id`'s own "at most one" shape — and so a future sync script can `UPSERT` a single row instead of a read-modify-write of an array). RP5's `systems`/`emulators`/`genres`/`series` lookup tables and `emulator_systems`/`game_genres` junctions all collapsed into plain `text`/`text[]` columns (single user, no multi-tenant reuse pressure — the same reasoning behind `athlete_limitations.movement_pattern` staying free text). RP5's 5 SQL views (list/detail/cleanup/audit) are **not** recreated — `gamesApi.ts` fetches `games` + `game_platforms` with two plain `select('*')` calls and joins them client-side (`attachPlatforms`), matching this app's own aggregation-module convention (`progressAggregate.ts` et al.) over RP5's view-heavy approach. Full rationale, RP5's original full schema (recovered from its git history, including files its own repo had since deleted), and the researched prior-art survey (Playnite/RomM/Backloggd/Grouvee) live in this session's chat record — not duplicated here.
- **RP5's 18-rule audit-scoring system was NOT ported** — it was one-time cataloguing QA (getting a raw import to a clean, matched library), not an ongoing personal-use feature. Replaced by **`NeedsReviewTab`** — a plain filter (missing cover/genres/release-year/platform/primary-variant, or an explicit `needs_review` flag) over already-fetched data, no SQL view.
- **New capabilities RP5 never had reachable from this app:** `AddGameModal` (manual add — RP5's own add-game tooling was its own separate `admin.html`/IGDB Bridge, both retired) and `GameDetailModal`'s edit form both cover every field the schema has (title/year/publisher/developer/series/description/storyline/genres/modes/players/age-rating/cover+screenshot+fanart URLs), not just a handful — a real gap fixed the same day it shipped, when the first pass only exposed status/tier/rating/iconic/coop/notes; full `game_platforms` CRUD from `GameDetailModal` (add/edit/delete a platform, `setPrimaryVariant` clears every sibling row for the game before flipping the new one — the partial unique index would otherwise reject two `true` rows mid-transition), and a `needs_review` toggle. (The old page's Stats panel is gone; the page's Analytics replaces it.)
- **Quick status switch (`StatusQuickBar`, `gamesApi.ts::setPlayStatus`)** — a real gap fixed the same day: changing play_status used to require opening the full Edit panel, picking a dropdown value and hitting Save. Now a row of tap-to-switch status pills sits directly under the title, always visible, one tap = one write. `setPlayStatus` is a dedicated action (not routed through the generic edit-form patch) specifically so it can auto-stamp **`started_at`/`finished_at`** (migration `090`) the first time a game moves into `playing`/`completed` — it reads the row's current dates first and only ever fills whichever is still NULL, so it can never clobber an already-set or manually-corrected date. Both dates are also plain editable fields in the full Edit panel (a `type="date"` input, truncated/expanded between the column's full timestamp and the input's `yyyy-MM-dd`) for setting or correcting them directly, mirroring Media's own `started_at`/`finished_at` precedent on `user_tv_entries`. Deliberately two plain columns, not a `game_play_sessions` child table — this tracks one playthrough's start/finish, not a replay history, which would be a genuinely new feature if ever wanted.
- **Desktop filter toolbar — real layout bug, fixed.** The Tier/Genre/System dropdowns, the Co-op/Iconic toggles, and the Sort+view-mode controls used to render as three separate full-width stacked rows on desktop — same-looking small rounded buttons with zero grouping cues, reading as one undifferentiated wall of controls (reported with a screenshot). Fixed for `sm:`+ only (the mobile filter Sheet renders the identical JSX unchanged, since it's only ever opened at phone widths regardless of breakpoint classes): the three groups now sit in ONE bordered toolbar row, separated by vertical dividers and small "Filter / Only show / Display" labels, so they read as one control cluster with visibly distinct sections instead of a pile of buttons.
- **Every mutation uses `useMutationWithFeedback`** (`useGames.ts`) — RP5-era `useUpdateGame`/`useReorderQueue`/etc. had **zero** error feedback at all (a bare `useMutation`, no toast, no `logError`); this was a real, silent gap fixed in the same pass as the data-source migration.
- **Full-width page layout is a deliberate exception** to PageBoard (see Coding Rules → Layout width): a `fullHeight` page inside the shell with its own navigation panel and no `max-w-*` cap.
- **ScreenScraper search and matching lives on the new page's Scrape section** — see "ScreenScraper — the Scrape page" below. The old studio (`components/studio/*`, `ScrapeGameButton`, `api/screenscraperApi.ts`/`screenscraperRules.ts`, `screenscraperStudio.ts`) was deleted in the rewrite; `GameDetailModal`'s Scrape button only points to the Scrape section.
- **Pre-migration-safe** (`gamesApi.ts`, the `wishesApi.ts` convention): every read degrades to `[]`/empty stats on a missing-table error rather than crashing the page; every write throws a named "migration 089 not applied" error. This is more load-bearing here than for most tables this pattern covers — Games previously read a fully-populated separate project, so without this guard every read would 42P01 the whole page the moment this PR deploys, before the migration is applied.
- **Tables this schema deliberately does NOT carry over:** RP5's `game_ss` (a ScreenScraper-matching staging table — already-matched data folds into `game_platforms`' own fields, the staging table itself was always meant to be temporary), `game_media_assets` (created in RP5's history, referenced by zero surviving RP5 code — orphaned, unconfirmed whether it still holds live data), `roms` (dropped by RP5 itself years ago, empty). RP5's Tips/Request-Tracker `notes` table isn't ported either — a request-tracker-shaped backlog already exists here as `dev_requests` (`page: 'games'`).
- **Steam and PlayStation (2026-09-15; on the old page a tab row, now shelves plus Advanced → Steam / PlayStation).** Their games sit on their own shelves in the Library (imported through `ImportProviderButton`/the shelf's Sync button), and `SteamTab`/`PlayStationTab` live under Advanced. **Research findings, so a future session doesn't redo this** (full agent reports live in this session's chat record, not duplicated here):
  - **Steam** is the far more stable of the two: a **personal Web API key** (steamcommunity.com/dev/apikey) + your own SteamID64 is all that's needed for read access — no OAuth ("Sign in through Steam" OpenID only proves identity, grants zero extra data access). Real endpoints confirmed: `IPlayerService/GetOwnedGames` (playtime per game), `GetRecentlyPlayedGames`, `GetSteamLevel`/`GetBadges`, `ISteamUserStats/GetPlayerAchievements` (per-achievement, % computed client-side) + `GetSchemaForGame` (achievement names/icons), `ISteamUser/GetPlayerSummaries` (currently-playing status, online state). **No CORS headers at all on `api.steampowered.com`** — confirmed mandatory server-side proxy. Wishlist has NO official endpoint — deliberately NOT built (would need the reverse-engineered `IWishlistService/GetWishlist`).
  - **PlayStation has NO official public API at all** — the only working approach is the community-reverse-engineered `npsso` cookie flow. **Real, current blocker as of 2026: Sony added a reCAPTCHA to the login flow that blocks scripted npsso refresh** — a human must periodically (roughly every 1-2 months) re-login in a real browser and re-paste a fresh npsso via the Connect form; this cannot be fully automated, unlike every other OAuth integration in this app. Confirmed available once authenticated: owned/purchased games, trophy list per game (bronze/silver/gold/platinum + rarity %), online/presence status. **Confirmed NOT available**: PS Store wishlist, clean PS Plus subscription status — neither built. **Explicitly best-effort, low-priority** (like this repo's own Football entry) — never load-bearing, given the manual-reconnect chore and coverage gaps.
  - **Implementation (both wired up, 2026-09-15):** `steam-api` (edge function, Vault `STEAM_API_KEY`/`STEAM_ID64`, normal `verify_jwt` ON) proxies Steam; `steamApi.ts`/`useSteam.ts` → `SteamTab.tsx`.
  - **Steam phase 1 — game detail modal + library enrichment (2026-09-15), built from an explicit decision pass** (the user worked through a published decision worksheet covering every capability both APIs expose; their per-item choices are the scope source): `SteamGameModal.tsx` (tap any game → store metadata, per-platform playtime split incl. **Steam Deck**, last-played, Metacritic, review score, screenshots, achievements) + `SteamAchievementGrid.tsx` (unlock state + schema icons + **global rarity %**, three Steam endpoints joined server-side, unlocked-then-rarest sort, locked-only toggle). `SteamTab` gained a "Son 2 hafta" strip, sort (playtime / last-played / name, using `sort_as` so "The Witcher" files under W), search, a library-total hours readout, and profile extras (country, account-creation year, profile link). `owned_games` now sends `include_extended_appinfo=true` (free, same request — adds `sort_as`/`has_dlc`/`content_descriptorids`). Live concurrent-player count is a **deliberate explicit-tap action inside the modal, never fetched on open** (the user's own instruction).
  - **Loading priority is a deliberate design, not an accident** (the user's own instruction: "her şeyi aynı anda çekmeye gerek yok… görseller, oyun isimleri ve temel şeyler hızlı yüklensin, gerisi detay işi"). The tab's first paint costs exactly **two** requests — profile + library. Everything else is tiered: the **"Son 2 hafta" strip is DERIVED, not fetched** (`GetRecentlyPlayedGames` is a strict subset of `GetOwnedGames`, and `playtime_2weeks` is already in the owned-games payload — the `recent_games` action and its client hook were deleted, not just left unused); **level/badges** is `enabled`-gated behind the library landing (two Steam round trips for one line of header text); the modal's hero art and title are painted from the appid and the owned-games row so the popup is legible on its **first frame with zero network**, and never swap when metadata arrives; **store metadata + reviews** load per opened game; **achievements load only when scrolled into view** (an `IntersectionObserver` inside `SteamAchievementGrid`, 150px root margin) since they are three uncacheable Steam round trips rendering below the fold; the **player count** waits for an explicit tap. Keep this tiering when adding to either tab — a new eager call on mount is a regression, not a feature.
  - **PSN phase 2 — the playtime library (2026-09-15).** `PlayStationTab` is now **two views over two genuinely different Sony datasets, deliberately not merged**: **📚 Kütüphane** (`getUserPlayedGames` — real lifetime playtime, play count, first/last played, PS4/PS5/PC category, cover art; sort by playtime/recency/name + search + a total-hours readout) and **🏆 Kupalar** (the pre-existing `getUserTitles` trophy-set grid). **They cannot be joined across a library**: PSN keys playtime by store SKU (`npTitleId`, CUSA…/PPSA…) and trophies by trophy-set id (`npCommunicationId`, NPWR…), and Sony's only bridge (`getUserTrophiesForSpecificTitle`) takes **5 titles per request** — 300 games would be 60 calls. So `PsnGameModal` bridges **one** game's id when it opens, and the trophy view (which already holds NPWR ids) needs no bridge at all. `PsnTrophyPanel` is shared by both entry points: per-trophy name/icon/hidden-flag joined server-side with the user's earned state on `trophyId`, carrying **Sony's own rarity tier** (Ultra Rare→Common) and earn-rate %, DLC group tabs (a second call, made only when the title row says `hasTrophyGroups`), earned-then-rarest sort, and the PS5 partial-progress fields that are real in Sony's response but **absent from psn-api's own TypeScript types**. `getPurchasedGames` supplies **PS Plus badges** (the only source of "came with the subscription" and of never-launched games) and is the most fragile call in the library — a Sony-side GraphQL hash rotation breaks it alone, so it degrades to an empty list with a note rather than taking the tab down. **No `getRecentlyPlayedGames`**: it is a strict subset of `getUserPlayedGames` (which already carries `lastPlayedDateTime`) *and* the other fragile GraphQL call — dropping it removes a round trip and a failure mode at once, same reasoning as Steam's `recent_games`. `parsePlayDurationMinutes` (`psnApi.ts`) parses Sony's ISO-8601 duration (`"PT228H56M33S"`) — the ONLY playtime figure PSN exposes anywhere, and a cumulative lifetime total with no per-session records; **verified:** `scripts/verify-psn-playtime.cjs`, 14 assertions (real observed values, each component alone, a day component, and every degenerate input returning 0 rather than a `NaN` that would poison the sort comparator and the hours total).
  - **`steam_apps` (migration `092`) is the ONE Steam table, and only because Steam forces it.** Everything about the USER (owned games, playtime, achievements) stays a live passthrough with no local row — Steam is the source of truth and nothing else in this app joins against it. Store METADATA is different: `store.steampowered.com/api/appdetails` is rate-limited to ~200 requests / 5 min per IP and returns ~36 KB per app, so a 400-game library cannot be enriched on every page load. So `steam_apps` mirrors the **`movies` shared-catalog shape** (no `user_id`, `SELECT` for `authenticated`, written only by the edge function's service-role client, **no `trg_audit`** per the bulk-synced exemption): promoted columns only for what the library filters/sorts on (`name`/`type`/`genres`/`metacritic_score`/`is_free`), the whole `appdetails` payload in a `details` jsonb (the `health_workouts.raw` precedent — a later UI addition needs no migration), and reviews on their own `reviews_fetched_at` cadence since a review score moves daily while a store page rarely does. TTLs: details 14 days, reviews 24h. `app_details` caps live store fetches at **20 per request** and returns the rest in `missing` so the client can page through without tripping the rate limit; a delisted app caches a `details: null` tombstone rather than being retried forever. Missing table (092 unapplied) degrades to an uncached live fetch, never a crash. `psn-api` (edge function, uses the `psn-api` npm package via a Deno `npm:` specifier rather than hand-rolled fetch calls — the auth exchange/GraphQL-backed calls are too finicky to safely guess at) resolves the caller via `supabase.auth.getUser` (the `calendar-oauth` pattern) and reads/writes **`psn_tokens`** (migration `091`, singleton per user, **deliberately no `trg_audit`** — it holds live secrets, and this table must never enter `ai-proxy`'s `DB_CATALOG`); `psnApi.ts`/`usePlayStation.ts` → `PlayStationTab.tsx`. **Genuinely unverified against a real account** — this authoring session has no PSN/Steam credentials to test against; the response-shape reads (`profile?.profile?.onlineId` etc.) are defensive with fallbacks, not confirmed live. Deploy `steam-api`/`psn-api` and report back what the first real connect actually returns.
  - Neither table/function touches `ai-proxy`'s `DB_CATALOG`.
  - **Provider-library behaviour (2026-09-25, migration `102`).** Five rules, all deliberate:
    - **`finished_at` is the day you finished, not the day you pressed the button.** `setPlayStatus` stamps it from the row's own `last_played_at` (the provider's last session) and falls back to `now()` only for a game no provider ever reported a session for. The old `now()` collapsed an imported library's years of history onto one afternoon when many old games were marked completed at once. Migration 102 backfills existing `completed` rows the same way — only rows with no `finished_at`, only where a session exists; a manually corrected date is never overwritten. **That left the real bug in place:** rows the old code had already stamped with `now()` weren't NULL, so 102 skipped them. Migration `103` repairs them: a `finished_at` more than a day after the game's own last session (the later of `last_played_at`/`esde_last_played`, matching `playStatsOf`) can only be a button-press stamp, and is replaced by that session.
    - **`hidden` is a `play_status`, not a separate flag** — the user's explicit call ("status içine kaydolsun; statüsü okuyup karar versin UI"). A row therefore holds a play status OR `'hidden'`, never both: hiding replaces the status and un-hiding resets it to `backlog` (the previous one isn't recorded anywhere). `STATUSES` (the picker) deliberately omits it; `LibraryControls` sets it with its own 🙈 button so it can't read as a kind of progress.
    - **`isHiddenEntry(kind, playStatus)`** (`providerEntries.ts`) is the one predicate both tabs use: an explicit `'hidden'` always hides, ANY real status always shows (a deliberately-tracked app stays visible even if the provider calls it a non-game), and only with NO status does the provider's type decide — `unknown` stays visible, so an unclassified Steam library never empties itself. The toggle is **Show hidden (N)**, off by default.
    - **PlayStation paints from the saved library first.** `psnGamesFromLibrary` (`psnLibraryFallback.ts`) projects `games` rows back into Sony's own `PsnPlayedGame` shape — round-tripping `play_seconds` through `secondsToIsoDuration` so the SAME `parsePlayDurationMinutes` reads saved and live rows identically, one shape for every consumer. Once anything is saved, the connection state only decides which banner sits above the grid (`ExpiredBanner` / `ExpiringSoonBanner`), never whether the grid renders. The PSN **Last 2 weeks** strip is "played in the last 14 days" by `lastPlayedDateTime`, never hours: Sony exposes only a lifetime total and the last session's timestamp, so claiming Steam's per-period hours here would invent a number.
    - Both libraries default to **last-played** order. `formatPlaytime` (`playtimeFormat.ts`) is the single days/hours/minutes formatter for every playtime figure AND the PSN token countdown (`npssoLifetimeLabel` → "PSN token expires in 64d 5h 41m"); library totals read "… played" so a duration next to "total" can't be mistaken for that countdown.

### Test-Game page — now the main Games page (`/#/games`, 2026-09-25)

The Games page rebuilt on a user-supplied "Game Library" design (desktop dark/light + phone). **Promoted to `/#/games` on the owner's call**; the old `GamesPage`, its two demo pages, `PlayQueueTab`, `StatsPanel` and `CheckboxFilterGroup` were deleted on 2026-09-26 (their addresses redirect here, as does `/#/test-game`). What was deliberately not carried over is listed in `docs/games/test-game-known-issues.md`. The code keeps its `src/features/games/test-game/` home and `Tg*` names. Registered inside the AppShell route group (lazy, own `ErrorBoundary` + `Suspense`), flagged `fullHeight` + `collapseSidebar`; it uses the shell's `ModalHost` and `Toaster`. `useTgUrlSync` mirrors section/platform to `?section=&platform=` in both directions (the store stays the source of truth; replace-only history; a plain /games opens the last place). The store→address write goes through `whenHistorySettled` (`useHistoryDismiss.ts`): it waits until history is back on the page's own entry (no overlay entry current, no own pop pending) and runs inside that popstate before the router reads the address — replacing the address while a popup's throwaway entry was current left the page entry stale, and the popup's Back then showed (or re-applied) the old section/platform. `/` focuses the Games search; ⌘K is the app's command bar. Default sort is **Last played** (a persisted `'title'` — the old default — migrates once via the store's `version: 1`); the list view's Rating / Playtime / Last played headings toggle direction like Title (`rating-asc`, `playtime-asc`, `recent-asc` — the reverse sorts keep games with no value last); there is no Backlog section any more (2026-09-30, the Backlog status filter covers it; store `version: 5` sends a saved Backlog view to the Library); **Library** in the nav resets a platform/genre picked elsewhere (e.g. from an Analytics row). Open issues: `docs/games/test-game-known-issues.md`.

- **Real data, no mocks.** `useTestGameLibrary` merges the SAME queries `/games` uses — `['games','all']` (retro) + `['games','library','steam'|'playstation']` — so every existing `useMutationWithFeedback` hook's `invalidateQueries(['games'])` refreshes both pages. Steam store types come from `steam_apps` under `['steam','app-types','library',<hash>]` — deliberately OUTSIDE `['games']` (a status edit must not refetch them) with `keepPreviousData` (hidden apps don't flash back while the id list changes).
- **`testGameModel.ts` owns every decision** (pure, type-only imports, `scripts/verify-test-game-model.cjs`, **227 assertions**): ONE platform per game (the primary variant, via a `resolveSystemKey` alias table so "GameCube"/"gc" file together; no platform → "No platform"), every platform grouped by maker (`platformGroups`: Nintendo/Sony/Sega/Microsoft/PC/Arcade/Android/Other, biggest first, Other last; `tgStoreMigrate.ts` holds the persisted-state upgrades — version 4: a saved Others shelf → All), hidden rows (explicit `hidden`, or a Steam non-game with an undecided status), scoping/status/genre/search, sorts, queue ranks, rating ↔ stars (`rating/2`, proportional, never rounded up), image candidate chains, en-GB dates (`formatDay` builds "15 Sep 2026" by hand — CLDR renders "Sept").
