- **Bookcase = a vertical grid of full shelves**, never horizontal scrolling (owner feedback): `TgShelf` + `TgShelfSlot`, CSS-variable sizing, as many covers per shelf as fit the width, the whole case scrolls down; `content-visibility` on slots keeps ~1,500 games at zero idle CPU.
- **Detail = a non-modal overlay, not a permanent column** (owner feedback, tablet + desktop): the games get the full width; clicking a game slides the panel over the shelf (the shelf stays usable), it collapses to a slim tab at the right edge (`detailCollapsed`, persisted) or closes (Esc). `activateGame` in `testGameStore.ts` decides opened/swapped/tab; arrow keys only move the selection, and walking onto a card hidden under the expanded panel tucks it into its tab so the card is visible; details close by themselves if the open game leaves the library; nothing is selected on load and a reload never reopens it. Analytics filters live in the same store (not persisted) so a tablet↔phone switch keeps them. The phone keeps a full-screen sheet.
- **Analytics** is a first-class screen with four tabs — **Overview · Play · Collection · Data health** (`TgAnalyticsTabs`, the tab is persisted; the phone label reads "Health"). One window (all time / 12 months / this year / 90 days / 30 days / 7 days) × library (all / retro / Steam / PlayStation) filter row scopes every tab except Data health, which always describes the whole library. `useTgAnalyticsBase` computes the window's games once and each tab calls its own hook (`useOverviewData` / `usePlayData` / `useCollectionData` / `useHealthData` in `tgAnalyticsData.ts`), so only the open tab derives. All figures come from pure, verified modules: `tgAnalyticsModel.ts` (windows, KPIs, drill lists, `isCompletion`, `isStarted`, `isStalePlaying`), `tgAnalyticsSeries.ts` (`buildTimeline` — days for 7/30, Monday weeks for 90, months otherwise — and `activitySeries`), `tgAnalyticsMore.ts` (play coverage, community score = the variant's 0–100 rating, worth playing next, libraries compared, platforms by metric, idle Playing, launches/session length, decades, studios, play-time buckets and concentration, players, series, fun facts) and `tgAnalyticsHealth.ts` (field coverage, review reasons, freshness, ES-DE asset inventory, hidden counts). Every row that opens a list hands the Library **exactly** the games behind its number (`useAnalyticsHandoff` in `tgAnalyticsHandoff.ts` → `openLibraryWith`, selectors in `tgAnalyticsLists.ts` that use each figure's own rule): the store's `libraryScope` ({ids, label}) narrows the Library and its header reads "12 of 310 games · from Analytics: Completed · Last 30 days · Retro" with Clear filters; any navigation clears it. A plain status/genre/studio filter used to open a longer list than the number (it dropped the window, Retro opened every library, and Studios matched developer OR publisher). Focus then moves to the page heading (or into the opened game's overlay), since the tapped row unmounts.
  - **Overview:** KPI tiles (a **Played** tile replaces Average rating while fewer than 3 games are rated; Completed's all-time rate is of games you've *started*; Playing says how many had no session in 60+ days), a hidden-titles note, **Activity** (every played game once, at its latest session — the only session date any provider reports — stacked by library, with the window's completions; all time names the completions it can't place: no finish date, finished before its 24 months, dated in the future), status mix, Playing-but-idle, recently played (only sessions inside the window), fun facts. **Idle Playing and the queue are whole-library states** (`computeKpis`' `whole` argument, `playingBreakdown(inLibrary)`): a game idle for 60 days has no session in a 30-day window by definition, so windowing them made the idle card empty and the tiles claim false facts. The idle card hides when nothing is idle and its "Show all N idle" opens exactly those; in a window the Backlog tile says "played here, still marked Backlog".
  - **Play:** play coverage (played vs no recorded play, per platform — ES-DE only records games it launched), libraries compared, Most played with a Launches view and average session length, how play time spreads (top-5 concentration + buckets by library), PlayStation trophies (fetched only once the card scrolls into view, only while PSN is connected).
  - **Collection:** ratings (Community | Yours), worth playing next (no recorded play, community score ≥ 80, reads the whole library), platforms ranked by games / play time / played share / completed, top genres and studios (rows filter the Library), release decades (owned vs played), players, series progress.
  - **Data health:** metadata coverage per field (retro fields over retro games; cover over every library) with a hand-off to a ScreenScraper batch, needs-review reasons, source freshness per library, ES-DE original images by category and size, hidden titles.
  - Source freshness counts calendar days (a sync at 23:00 yesterday is "yesterday", and 8 days is stale) and ignores a stamp a ScreenScraper save made (`synced_at` equal to `ss_scraped_at`); `screenscraper-sync` no longer writes `synced_at` at all — it belongs to ES-DE/Steam/PlayStation. Shares never round away their extremes (`fmtPct`: "<1%", ">99%"); bar lists scale without the folded "N more" row (it draws no bar) and draw a zero as nothing; the "rest of the bar" tint has a 1px inner edge (`TGA_TINT`). Collection pairs short cards with short ones per breakpoint (`collectionLayout`, checked by a grid simulation in `-collection`); Play and a no-retro Data health stop at three columns (`TGA_GRID_3`). On the phone layout (a landscape phone too) the window and library are always two dropdowns.
  - Verified: `scripts/verify-tg-analytics.cjs` (151 assertions — every hand-off list equals its row, idle/queued are whole-library, freshness, formats, unplaced completions) plus one script per tab — `-overview` (31), `-play` (30), `-collection` (50), `-health` (63).
- **Owner feedback round 2 (2026-09-25):**
  - **Detail = the whole record.** `TgDetailFields` (+ pure `tgDetailFields.ts`) lists every non-empty field the list query already loads — About · Your progress · Platforms (one card per `game_platforms` variant with emulator/region/ROM/file/performance/per-variant ES-DE stats) · Source — so nothing needs the legacy `GameDetailModal`. The phone sheet shows the full description; the overlay clamps to 8 lines (14 on tall screens) with Read more. The footer's second button is **Scrape** for retro games (`TgScrapeButton` → `openScrape(gameId)`, the Scrape section) and **Edit** for Steam/PlayStation; Edit also lives in the ⋯ menu for every game, next to "Manage platforms…" (retro only — platform CRUD still lives in the classic modal) and Delete (`TgConfirmDialog`).
  - **Filters are multi-select.** The store holds `statuses: PlayStatus[]` / `genres: string[]` (empty = all) with `toggleStatus`/`toggleGenre`/`clearFilters`; `setStatus(s)`/`setGenre(g|null)` remain as set-exactly-one setters. Desktop dropdowns are checklists (`TgMultiDropdown`), header tabs light every picked status, phone chips toggle. Sort is not a filter: its own pill on the phone, `DEFAULT_SORT = 'recent'` in the model.
  - **Every phone sheet drags down to close** (`useSheetDrag` — now shared, `src/shared/hooks/` — in `TgMobileSheet`: handle/title, or the body when scrolled to the top; distance or flick closes, otherwise it snaps back; reduced-motion respected).
  - **No blurred letterbox behind covers** — the surface shows through and the phone grid's frame hugs the art.
  - **Analytics tiles drill down.** Each KPI tile opens the exact games behind its number for the current window × library (`tileGames(kind, scoped, start)` in `tgAnalyticsModel.ts`; `computeKpis` counts from the same lists, `scripts/verify-tg-analytics.cjs`). Playtime is **lifetime** play time of games played in the window, and says so — no per-period hours exist in the data. A row (and Most played / Recently played) opens that game in Library.
- **Images walk a fallback chain** (`coverCandidates` / `heroCandidates` / `sceneImages`): Steam portrait capsule (`library_600x900`, never the Steam header banner) → PSN resized copy → saved cover → variant cover/box → ScreenScraper `games.media` (migration 099) → ES-DE originals in `game_platforms.esde_assets` (migration 100) → a drawn placeholder case. Failed URLs are retried once, then remembered in memory for the session (`coverCache.ts`).
- **Platform icons** (`platformArtData.ts`): real brand logos from Simple Icons (CC0; Nintendo/Wii/GameCube/3DS/Xbox from v10, since removed upstream at the brands' request; the Android robot is CC BY 3.0, Google) for PlayStation, Xbox, Switch, Wii, GameCube, DS, Steam, Sega and Android; hand-drawn line glyphs for PSP (wordmark unreadable at 20px), the generic Nintendo controller, Game Boy, arcade and other. All draw in `currentColor`.
- **Console photos** (`TgConsolePhoto`, beside a platform page's heading from tablet width): Evan-Amos photos from Wikimedia Commons, all **public domain** (console + controller where one exists; Dreamcast, Wii U and the handhelds are console-only because the set photos are CC BY-SA or by other authors), bundled as ~12–43 KB WebP in `test-game/assets/consoles/` with sources in its `manifest.json`; loaded only when that page opens (not precached), on a white mat (`.tg-console-photo`) since most are shot on white. `snesna`/`megadrive` reuse the SNES/Genesis photo.
- **Styling is self-contained** in `testGame.css` (tokens on `.tg-root` / `.tg-portal`, swapped by the app's `.dark` class; plain CSS outside `@layer`, since several selectors only match at runtime; it overrides the app-wide orange focus ring). **Anything Headless UI portals to `<body>` needs the `tg-portal` class** or it loses every token. Tailwind gotcha hit here: `shadow-[var(--x)]` compiles as a shadow COLOUR — write `shadow-[shadow:var(--x)]`.
- **Layouts:** phone (< 768px or a landscape phone): the app header + tab bar frame the page; `TgMobileSections` (section pills — the row fades whichever edge has more beyond it — + search, random dice, ⋯) and a scope row (platform pill, never under 4.5rem, with a sheet grouped by maker; an icon-only Sync on the Steam/PlayStation shelves; sort, filters on the right; status sections show only their platform dropdown). From 640px wide the scope and tools of a game list join the section row (the queue's "Remove N finished" sits beside its title), and on a short screen (a phone held sideways) the Games rows slide away with the app header while the list scrolls down (`useTgMatch`). Tablet/desktop: `TgNavPanel` (folds to a rail, `navCollapsed` persisted) + `TgToolbar`, three widths measured on the toolbar itself — full ≥ 1100px, compact (icon-only pills, Studio included), tight < 700px (44px square pills, the view switch moves into ⋯) — so ⋯ never leaves the row; hidden on Analytics/Scrape/Advanced, where ⋯ sits in the heading. The overlay host reserves room for the queue when its own row is ≥ 768px (measured).
- **Dialogs:** the four classic dialogs (`AddGameModal`, `GameDetailModal`, `SteamGameModal`, `PsnGameModal`) render `ModalShell`; the Games page passes `tg-portal tg-legacy` as their panel class. Games' own dialogs sit on the shell layers: `sheet` for its phone sheets, Analytics drill and scrape dialog; `modal` for renew, lightbox and video; `confirm` for `TgConfirmDialog`; `toast` for the chunk-failed alert.
- **Advanced** (restyled in the page theme; `.tg-legacy` remaps the app's cream/ink/accent tokens so embedded legacy components fit): **Needs review · Duplicates · Steam · PlayStation** (Duplicates, 30.09.2026: games sharing a normalized title or a cover URL, same-platform groups first — pure `tgDuplicates.ts`, nothing merged automatically). Classic library, Queue editor, Tiers and "Add & random" were removed on the owner's call (persist `version: 2` maps a saved removed tab to `review`); ScreenScraper left for its own Scrape section (persist `version: 3` sends a saved ScreenScraper tab there). **Tiers are gone from every UI** (the page, `GameDetailModal`'s form + patch, `AddGameModal`; `TierEditorTab`/`TierBadge` deleted) — the `tier` column stays. `GameDetailModal`'s optional `initialEditing` prop opens the form directly and closes on Save/Cancel.
- **Add game** lives in the ⋯ menu (phone) and the panel footer (`tgAddGame.ts` opens `AddGameModal` via `TgModals`). **Random pick** is a dice icon next to search (`TgRandomButton`/`tgRandom.ts`), picking from the currently visible games and skipping the one already open.
- **Library settings menu** (`TgLibraryMenu`, a gear since 30.09.2026): **Show hidden games (N)** (persisted `showHidden`; puts hidden rows back among all in the Library), Find duplicates, Refresh library; Add game on phones. The PSN/Steam status rows were removed (owner: they already show on the shelves, Advanced and Settings → Subscriptions; `TgConnections` stays for the renew flow). Theme lives in the app's ⚙ menu. Empty bookcase slots have no lamps. A review opened from Many games returns there on every Back (`scrapeFromBatch` in the store); the review's storage estimate uses the measured average of the copies already stored (≥ 5, `measuredCopyAverage`), and Keep on a picture field puts its copy online.
- **The detail's primary button is "Add to queue"** ("In queue · #N" once queued; tapping again removes it, optimistic with rollback). Launching a Steam game from the web moved to ⋯ → **Launch on Steam** (`useSteamLaunch`, promotes backlog/wishlist to Playing first). **The Play Queue reorders by drag** (`TgQueueViewDrag.ts`: 44px grip, pointer events so touch works, auto-scroll at the edges, optimistic order saved through `useReorderQueue`, filtered-out entries keep their slots; Alt+Arrow / arrow keys on the grip for keyboard).
- **Covers** are scaled to fit a fixed box and centred in the phone grid and grid view (titles line up across a row); the bookcase keeps them standing on the plank.
- **Audit round (2026-09-26) — correctness:** the edit form (`GameDetailModal`) sends only changed fields (`diffPatch`, `api/gameEdit.ts`) and waits for a fresh read when opened from the page; play dates round-trip at local noon (no day drift east of UTC) — `scripts/verify-game-edit.cjs` (22 assertions × 4 time zones, incl. `providerUpdateFields`). A Steam/PSN re-import refreshes play figures but only **fills** empty title/cover/genres/year (`api/providerImportRules.ts`) — it used to reset corrected titles and blank Steam genres. Analytics has ONE completion rule (`isCompletion`: status Completed, and in a window a finish date inside `[start, end of today)`), shared by tiles, drill-downs and the chart (future dates footnoted). "Last played" sort and Recently played share `isRealSession` (a sub-5-minute launch ranks below real sessions; nothing is dropped). Genres fold case-insensitively (`foldGenres`/`genreKey`). A retro system spelled like a page key or a provider ("All", "Steam") gets its own `sys-…` shelf. Needs review is computed from the loaded rows (`needsReviewReasons`, a cover on screen counts). PlayStation apps hide like Steam tools via `games.provider_kind` (migration `105`, Sony's `category`; `first_played_at` too). Deleting a synced game offers Hide first (the sync re-adds deleted rows). An auto-promoted Playing can be confirmed (stamps `started_at`). `/games` and the detail's record blocks and Analytics sit in `ErrorBoundary`s.
- **Audit round — performance:** status, rating and queue edits patch every cached `['games', …]` read optimistically (`api/gameCachePatch.ts`: `patchGameData`, the shared `statusPatch` date rule, `nextQueuePosition`; `scripts/verify-game-cache-patch.cjs`, 18 assertions) and only the small reads refetch — the big lists are marked stale instead of re-downloaded per tap (reads interrupted by an edit are re-run); per-game mutation `scope` keeps rapid taps in order. Library freshness is 10 minutes (`LIBRARY_STALE_MS`) with an explicit **Refresh library** in the ⋯ menu. The phone grid mounts cards in pages as it scrolls; each section keeps its scroll depth (`tgScrollMemory.ts`); a new filter/sort/search starts every view at the top (`useScrollReset`). Filters are deferred, the library is sorted once per change, cover/hero chains are memoised per row, and covers move to the next candidate immediately on an error while the failed URL is re-probed in the background (per-URL subscriptions, recovery on `online`). The bookcase measures before first paint; Steam rows wait for their store types; Analytics and the queue badge reuse the page's rows (`TgRanksContext`).
- **Audit round — ported from the old page (before it was deleted):** ⋯ → **Plan a session…** (and a calendar button on queue rows) opens the app's `UnifiedPlanModal` as a `games` time block; queue rows get ▶ **Mark as playing** (through `useSetPlayStatus`, so `started_at` is stamped) and the header reads "4 games queued · 2 playing · 1 up next · 1 finished" (up next = still to play and not playing; Completed/Dropped still queued are "finished"). The header shows "12 of 310 games · Clear" while anything narrows the list (the phone filter sheet's button says "Show N games"). Cards carry one sort-aware meta line (`cardMeta`: play time, last-played day, year, series…), the list view has xl-only Platforms / Genres / Launches columns and sortable headings, a **Studio** filter (developer OR publisher, case-folded, `studioOptions`), a **By series** sort plus a "More in this series" strip (`seriesSiblings`), inline **My notes** in the detail (saves on blur), a **Sync** button on the Steam / PlayStation shelves (`TgProviderSync`, explicit tap, "last 12 Sep 2026"), the Needs-review count in the navigation, a numeric filter badge on the phone, and the dice's count and `r` shortcut. Queue rows lay their controls out by the ROW's width (container queries), and on screens ≥ 992px the queue makes room for the open details (`TgDetailOverlayHost`'s `reserve`, `--tg-ov` = the overlay width) instead of hiding its controls under them. Every dialog on the page closes on native Back, only the top one at a time (`useHistoryDismiss` in the classic modals, the confirm, lightbox, drill, renew and scrape dialogs). Arrow keys, Home and End walk the cover grid and the list like the bookcase, with one tab stop per list (`tgGridNav.ts`: `gridStep`, `stepOrigin`; assertions in `verify-test-game-model.cjs`).
- **Queue insights and profile facts:** the queue header offers **Remove N finished** as a text action beside the count (Completed/Dropped games still queued — one write, `useRemoveManyFromQueue`; on the phone on its own line, since beside the title it pushed Filters off the screen) and, on a line of its own (`TgHeaderConfig.note`), a rough play-through estimate in whole hours (still-to-play × the median play time of your completed games, shown only with 3+ of those — `queueInsights`). The header row wraps before its title truncates. On a monitor the queue list is capped at 72rem so a title stays near its own buttons. A game with no status files as Backlog everywhere (`effectiveStatus`: counts, the tab, the section, cards), and a hidden row's card says "Hidden" or "Hidden · not a game" (`cardStatus`). The ⋯ menu's connections show the PSN trophy level, PlayStation Plus and earned trophies, and Steam presence/current game/account year from the calls it already makes (`tgConnectionFacts.ts`).
- **Code loading:** Analytics, Scrape and Advanced load on first visit, and the classic edit form, Add game and the Steam/PlayStation modals only once opened — all through the shared `lazyWithReload` (`src/shared/utils/lazyWithReload.ts`, moved out of the router; a missing chunk after a deploy reloads once, then shows `TgChunkFailed` inside the page instead of a blank screen).
- **Shared-code changes this page made (they affect `/games` too):** `gamesApi.ts` reads explicit list columns (not `provider_data`/`esde_source`) with a missing-column retry, fetches games and platforms in parallel and paginates ordered by id; `playStatsOf` prefers the live `esde_*` columns over migration 096's frozen neutral columns for retro rows (`scripts/verify-game-stats.cjs`, 63 assertions); `useReorderQueue` invalidates all of `['games']`; the service worker's `supabase-api` cache is limited to `/rest/v1/`.

### ScreenScraper — the Scrape page (rewrite, 2026-09-25)

Rewritten from scratch on the owner's call ("this version is f up"). The measured starting point: of 1,030 retro games only 45 had ever been matched, `provider_data` was filled on 1 and `games.media` on 0 — the old per-game path (`apply_match`) never wrote either, and only ~11 of the ~25 fields their API returns were mapped. Full API map, storage research and design: `docs/games/screenscraper-integration.md` §16.

- **Where:** a first-class section `scrape` — phone More → "Scrape (ScreenScraper)", desktop sidebar "Scrape". The detail's **Scrape** button (retro games) is `openScrape(gameId)`: the SAME page with that game picked and its search already running — there is no second flow anywhere. Header tabs switch **One game / Many games**. The last search (game + exact form + results) and the batch session live in `testGameStore`, so switching modes or leaving the page never throws away lookups that cost requests. Phone: search → review are steps; **Back to results** and the **What to save** gear sit in the phone header (always on screen); tablet/desktop show results and review side by side.
- **Owner only.** `screenscraper-sync` refuses every caller but `HEVY_USER_ID` (403) — the ScreenScraper account is one person's paid membership.
- **Search** (`planSearch`): name (jeuRecherche, ≤30 results, ≥4 letters with "the" ignored), ROM identity (hash → jeuInfos with every hash + filename + size, no system needed; filename alone needs the system — "systemeid obligatoire si aucun CRC"), serial, ScreenScraper id — any mix, run in parallel and merged (`mergeCandidates`: exact matches first, one row per entry with every way it was found). Name and ROM info each switch off with one tap. A game's previous match id is sent as `previous_id` and labelled "previous match", never "exact". A lookup that cannot run says why. A filename match is **verified** (`verifyFilenameMatch`) before dump-specific values (ROM region, release date of this version) may be saved; a hash match marks the variant `rom_status='verified'`.
- **Review** (`TgScrapeReview`): every field yours-vs-theirs with Keep / Fill / Replace (Fill only on an empty field, Replace only on a filled one; identical and absent fields fold into one line each); pickable title region and description language (sent as `overrides`); every media file ScreenScraper has, one card per type with preview, version (region/disc) and **Copy / Online / Skip** (+ group "All:" shortcuts); "Everything else" (every regional title/date, every rating board, series/themes/styles, tips, hacks without download links, control mappings, matched dump + hashes, every known dump, and their raw answer). One sticky Save with a live summary; the result screen lists each field old → new, every file copied/online/failed (with the reason, e.g. "storage budget reached — shown online instead"), with **Undo**. **Recent saves** (from the journal) keeps Undo available later.
- **Many games** (`TgScrapeBatch`): filters Not scraped / Old matches / No cover / No description / All + system; "Find matches for the next 10" (`find_batch`: previous id → filename (verified) → name), tick, "Save N ticked" (`apply_batch`, ≤5 per call, deadline-bounded, remaining ones reported as not started) using the saved "What to save".
- **What to save** (`screenscraper_prefs`, migration `104`, a jsonb doc normalized by `normalizePrefs`): per-field default policy, per-media-type mode, image size (0.5×/1×/1.5×/original), region and language order, the full-record switch, and the **storage budget** (default 800 MB, **never above 950 MB** server-side). The sheet shows the bucket's real usage by category (ES-DE originals / ES-DE covers / ScreenScraper / old previews) against the budget and the 1 GB plan, today's ScreenScraper allowance (requests + misses), and **Find unused ScreenScraper copies** (`cleanup`, dry run first, then delete).
- **Storage strategy — the 1 GB wall is the constraint** (Free plan: over it the project goes read-only and then answers every request with 402). **Copy** = a copy resized by ScreenScraper itself (`maxwidth` + `outputformat`, JPEG for big opaque art, PNG for transparency and small pixel-art screens — `outputFormatFor`), written only while `game_media_usage()` + a reservation for the whole save stays under the budget (`decideMediaModes`); otherwise it is shown online and the result says so. **Online** = nothing stored; the `screenscraper-media` proxy streams it when viewed (`ssImageQueue` loads 3 at a time with retries because the account has 7 threads). **Skip** = ignored. Defaults: box front/back, screenshot, title screen, fan art and HD logo copied (≈ 285 MB worst case for 1,100 games); 3D box, spine, texture, cartridge, Steam grid, logos, marquees and the manual online; composites, bezels, themes, pictograms and video skipped. Manuals and videos can never be copied. **The ES-DE pushes obey the same wall:** `esde-content-sync` and `esde-media-sync` refuse an upload that would cross 950 MB with 413 `storage_full`.
- **Credentials never leave the server.** Every ScreenScraper media URL carries devid/devpassword/ssid/sspassword, so none is ever stored, logged or returned: copies are downloaded server-side, and online images go through `screenscraper-media` (JWT verification OFF — an `<img>` sends no header), which only serves a request signed with HMAC-SHA256 over `jeu|system|expiry` (key `SCREENSCRAPER_MEDIA_KEY`, else derived from the service-role key), limited to fixed widths (120/200/360/640/1280), a whitelisted image/PDF/video content type, `nosniff` + a sandbox CSP, and one bare redirect hop. **Signatures expire** (end of next week, one value all week so browser caches stay stable) and are **never stored** — search results carry one, and the detail asks for a fresh one per visit (`sign` action).
- **What is stored:** columns per field policy (`games` + the primary `game_platforms` row: release date, ROM region, their /20 score ×5); image columns (`primary_cover_url`/`screenshot_url`/`fanart_url`) only ever take a STORED copy (a proxied cover in the grid would be a request per card); `games.media` only the list-read types (`LIST_MEDIA_TYPES`: box-2D, box-3D, fanart, ss, sstitle). The match marker is **`games.ss_jeu_id`** (+ `ss_scraped_at`) — **never `external_source`/`external_ref`**, which belong to the ES-DE sync (the old scraper overwrote them and broke `esde-sync`'s upsert key; migration 104 repairs those rows). `provider_data` holds only a small v2 pointer (`{v:2, fetched_at, run_id, jeu_id, rom_id, system, matched_by, verified_rom, saved, linked (type → chosen version), media_count}`); the heavy record — normalized summary incl. the full media inventory, and their raw answer when "Save their full record" is on — lives in **`game_scrape_records`** (one row per game, owner read-only, service-role written, no audit trigger), so the library list never carries it. The detail's **ScreenScraper** section (`TgDetailScreenScraper`) reads it for the open game only: every file (copies from Storage, the rest online, every version under "Show all"), the full record, and **Undo last scrape**.
- **Journal:** every apply writes `scrape_decisions` (browser: **read-only**; written by the function only, no audit trigger) with `written_values`, `prior_values` and `replaced_paths` (migration 104); a save whose journal row is refused writes nothing. Undo is **newest-apply-only per game** — once undone, the older apply never becomes undoable (its copies were deleted when the newer one landed) — and "undone" is tracked **per journal row** (the undone row's `written_values.undid` names the apply it reverted; older undone rows fall back to their run). It restores a prior value only where the live value is still what was written (a later edit is kept), goes through a column allow-list, deletes the heavy record, and removes stored copies nothing references any more. **A game's own "Undo last scrape" (detail, Saved screen) undoes only that game** (`undo` with `game_ids`) — a batch shares one run id, and undoing the run from one game used to revert the whole batch; run-wide undo lives only in Recent saves and "Undo this batch". Every undo asks first; the detail and Recent saves offer Undo only when the server would do it (`ssJournal.ts`, the same newest-only rule). Pre-104 rows (no prior values) undo to empty, which was the old fill-only inverse.
- **Re-matching** (a different entry than the game's current match) reverts the previous apply only **after** the new entry has been fetched and checked (a failed lookup never throws the old match away), then clears every column still showing one of that game's copies (`scrubCopies`), so an entry applied twice by mistake leaves no art behind. A copy that cannot be (re)made — quota, unknown usage, budget — **keeps** the copy the game already has; a copy one of the game's columns still shows is never released.
- **Match proof:** a hash lookup is exact only when the returned dump carries a hash that was asked for (`verifyHashMatch` — ScreenScraper answers an unknown hash with its filename guess); a filename answer is verified (`verifyFilenameMatch`). Without proof, the dump's region and version are not written and the review does not offer them; "ROM verified" only comes from a hash.
- **The handheld's pictures count:** an image column that is empty while ES-DE already uploaded that picture (screenshot, fan art…) is not "empty" — Fill skips it on the server, and the review shows the handheld picture as yours (Replace, never a silent Fill). The review's starting copy decisions are the same `decideMediaModes` Many games uses (a copy only when the game will use it).
- **Quota:** `requeststoday` and the miss counter are ACCOUNT-wide (the handheld's ES-DE scraper spends them too), so search/apply/batch refuse below a floor (300 requests / 50 misses) rather than assuming a fresh budget; ScreenScraper's plain-text errors (often HTTP 200) are classified (`classifyText`: closed, busy, quota, no match).
- **Cleanup** pages everything past PostgREST's 1,000-row cap (`game_media_scrape_objects` is ordered and returns `created_at`), aborts before deleting anything if any read fails, and never touches a copy younger than 30 minutes or one a save made in the last hour (it may still be writing its game). The storage split identifies ES-DE covers by what a variant's `cover_url` points at (originals can be WebP too).
- **Proxy details:** the upstream timeout covers only the wait for response headers (a long manual or video is never cut off mid-file); manuals are served without the sandbox CSP (PDF viewers refuse to render under it) — type allow-list and `nosniff` still apply.
- **Layout:** results and review side by side only at desktop widths; phones and tablets use the step flow (Back to results in the phone header, an inline Results link on tablets, the results' scroll position restored); the media grid follows its own width (container queries). Many games keeps lookups and saves in the page store (`scrapeBatch.inFlight`) so leaving mid-run neither loses results nor sends a game twice, and refreshes the library once per save.
- **Shared code:** the pure modules in `src/features/games/scraper/` (`ssTypes`, `ssMediaCatalog`, `ssRules`, `ssProxy`, `ssPlan`) are GENERATED into both functions by `scripts/sync-screenscraper-shared.mjs` (`--check` in CI-less review); `scripts/verify-screenscraper-v2.cjs` (142 assertions, incl. credential stripping, token parsing, mapping, planning, merging, filename and hash verification, media-mode decisions against the budget, policies, prefs, proxy parsing/signing/expiry, and the generated regions being current) and `scripts/verify-tg-scrape-model.cjs` (59 assertions, incl. the journal's newest-only undo rule) cover them.
- **Video player:** a ScreenScraper video (detail gallery and the review's media grid) plays in place in `TgVideoPlayer` — a full-screen `<video controls>` over the signed `screenscraper-media` proxy, which passes Range requests through so seeking works; nothing is stored. A failed load says why instead of a black box. Manuals still open in a new tab.
- **Per-game storage (migration `108`, `game_storage_usage(game_id)`):** the detail panel's **Storage** block (`TgDetailStorage`) shows what one game keeps — ScreenScraper copies (`<game id>/…`), handheld originals and cover (`<user>/esde/<variant id>/…`, the cover being what a variant's `cover_url` points at) and the on-disk size of its own rows (`games`, `game_platforms`, `game_scrape_records`, `scrape_decisions`). SECURITY DEFINER because the browser cannot read `storage.objects`, and it answers only for the caller's own game. The review has a **Storage for this game** banner (`TgScrapeStorageBanner`, math in `tgScrapeModel.storageChange`, verified in `verify-tg-scrape-model.cjs`): this game now → after the save, the ScreenScraper part (copies + text record) on its own, the change (−/+), and one plain line per effect — copies added (estimated), earlier ScreenScraper copies removed, the text record saved or replaced, pictures kept online (free), handheld pictures untouched. The Save bar repeats it in one line. Migration `109` splits ScreenScraper's record (`scrape_record`) out of `database` so the replaced size is known. A new record is estimated as its JSON ÷ 3 (Postgres compresses it); a re-scrape assumes the same size as the saved one. Before 108 both simply don't render.
- **Attribution:** ScreenScraper media is CC BY-NC-SA — the page and the detail section credit ScreenScraper.fr.
- **Not built (deliberately):** WebP re-encoding in the function (2 s CPU budget, and WASM bundles need a CLI deploy), external object storage (R2 needs a Cloudflare-hosted domain to serve properly), system/genre/company pictograms (their inventory is kept as `extra_media`), and reclaiming the ~537 MB of ES-DE originals (that is the RP6 pipeline's call — the originals are on the device's SD card anyway; see the doc).

### IGDB — game length, scores and a link (01.10.2026, migration `121`)

Its own source, deliberately **separate from ScreenScraper** (owner: "separate from screenscraper"): match marker `games.igdb_id` (never `ss_jeu_id`/`external_*`), its own columns (`igdb_slug`/`igdb_url`, `igdb_match` steam|exact|picked, member/critic/total scores 0–100 + counts, `ttb_main/extra/full_seconds` + `ttb_count` from IGDB's `game_time_to_beats`, and a small `igdb_data` jsonb: themes, modes, perspectives, franchises, engines, studios, platforms, release date, summary, ≤ 10 similar games), and it never writes a title, cover, description or any other column. Works for **every library** (retro, Steam, PlayStation).
- **`igdb-api`** (JWT ON, writes scoped to `getUser`): Twitch app Client ID + Secret (Edge Function secrets `IGDB_CLIENT_ID`/`IGDB_CLIENT_SECRET`) → app token by client credentials, kept in memory; calls run one at a time ≥ 260 ms apart (IGDB: 4/s, 8 open, 500 rows, 10 per multiquery), one retry on 429/401. Actions `status` · `match` (≤ 50: Steam rows by app id through `external_games` with the Steam `external_game_source` looked up by name, the rest by `search` in multiqueries of 10, `version_parent = null`) · `search` · `apply` (≤ 50, fetches `games` + `game_time_to_beats` and writes) · `refresh` (200 matched rows per call) · `unlink`. Field names from api-docs.igdb.com (v4; the deprecated `category`/`collection` are not used) — **not yet run against a live account**.
- **Matching** (`igdb/igdbMatch.ts`, pure; `verify-igdb-match`, 56): titles normalised (region/dump tags, accents, ™, punctuation, leading/trailing "The", roman numerals → digits **except X** — "Mega Man X" is not "Mega Man 10"), compared with IGDB's alternative names too; platform (this app's key → IGDB platform ids, IGDB's platform names as fallback; a signal, never a filter) and year (±1) add or subtract. **Exact** = same title + nothing disagreeing + platform or year agreeing + not a DLC/bundle/pack; two exact rivals the year can't separate are both demoted to "likely". Steam app-id matches are exact whatever the title says.
- **Games → IGDB** (a tool section next to Scrape; `components/igdb/`): filters Not matched / To review / Matched / No length / All × library; **Match N games** runs 25 per request with a progress bar and Stop (the run lives in `igdb/igdbBatchStore.ts`, so leaving the page doesn't stop it), saves exact matches by itself, leaves the rest with their best result to tick (**Tick all likely**, **Save N ticked**), each row has Results / Search by hand / Change; **Refresh scores and lengths**. Not configured → the Twitch setup steps. The library refreshes once per run.
- **Detail → IGDB block** (`TgDetailIgdb`): How long to beat (Main story · + Extras · 100 %, members count, "You've played X of about Y"), Scores (Members · Critics · Overall with counts), facts from `igdb_data`, IGDB's summary only when the game has no description, Similar games ("In your library" opens it), **Open on IGDB**, Change match, Remove match (confirmed). Unmatched → Find on IGDB.
- **Uses:** sorts **Shortest first** (`gameLengthSeconds` = extras, else main story; unknown last) and **IGDB score**; the Play Queue's estimate uses each game's IGDB length minus what you've played, the median of your completed games only for games without one (`queueInsights.fromIgdb`). Lists read the promoted columns (one missing-column group drops all IGDB columns at once before 121, `COLUMN_GROUPS` in `gamesApi.ts`); `igdb_data` is read per game (`fetchIgdbData`).
- Settings → Subscriptions has a read-only **IGDB** card (server-configured) and Integrations and APIs an `igdb` entry.
- **Round 2 (02.10.2026, owner: "retro bulk matching did basically nothing; review is the same as not matched"):** titles compare by the best of word overlap and letter-pair overlap after normalising (so spacing, apostrophes, accents and colons don't matter), different numbers always keep two titles apart (a sequel is never the game), years agree within ±2 (regional releases), and two exact rivals are separated by the closer year or a clearly better score instead of both dropping. ES-DE/No-Intro names are rewritten for IGDB's search ("Legend of Zelda, The - X (USA)" → "The Legend of Zelda: X") and a second, shorter search (the part before the subtitle) runs on the server when the first finds nothing. Filters are Not looked up · To review · No match · Matched · No length · All, with counts; the scope picker lists All, each library and **every retro system** separately. Each result shows a comparison (title ✓/≈/✗ with its similarity and the alternative name it matched, platform, year vs yours). The review session (lookups with their top 5 results, ticks, filters) is saved on this device (`lasci.igdbBatch`), so a reload keeps it.

### Home Feature Detail

`HomePage.tsx` renders ONE layout per breakpoint (`useBreakpoint()`, so no hidden copy keeps fetching). Phone: the rule-based **DailyBrief** (see Key Patterns; its greeting is the page `<h1>`, no `PageHeader`) → `HomeHero` (now/next + a 48px-cell week strip) → `TodayTasksCard` (reuses Daily's `ToDoItem`) → `TransitCard` (next departures from the default stop; the full planner `TransitPanel` opens in a popup) → 2-column `GlanceTile`s (weather, NOK→TRY, training, recently watched, projects, games — weather/currency open a detail popup, watched opens `modal.open({kind:'media'})`) → news. Wider pages use PageBoard (`homeBoard.ts`): 1280/1469 — brief, now/next and tasks in main; transit, the glance tiles (open their popups) and news beside them; 1920 — full Weather/Transit/Currency and Training/Media/Projects/Games/News columns (Games under Currency); 2450 — News gets its own column and lists 16 headlines (`newsRows`; `useNews` fetches 16). Only placed sections mount. On a wide Home (board step ≥ 2) the Training, Projects, Games and Watched tiles open their widget's content in a popup (`TileDetail`, a local ModalShell like Weather's, whose "Open …" link replaces the popup's history entry); phones still go to the page. Home no longer mounts Daily's `TodaySummary`; `NAV_CARDS`, `TodayScheduleWidget`, `RuterWidget` and the per-widget sync controls are gone. Widgets use `WidgetShell` (on `Card`) + `useWidgetState` (shared store, same localStorage keys) and **disable their queries when collapsed** (Training stays enabled — the brief reads the same data). Data comes only from hooks: `useWeather` (one query keyed by the real coordinates), `useCurrencyRates` (`STALE.hour`, no focus refetch), `useNews`, `useWeekTrainingStats` (dated by `start_time`), `useTodayOverview` (task counts without cancelled; next-up/next-training include recurring blocks, calendar events and what's in progress), `useRecentlyWatched`; `ProjectsHomeWidget` passes `enabled` to `useProjects`/`useProjectStats`.

- **Geolocation** (`useGeolocation.ts`): requests the browser's location once per session (`qk.external.geolocation()`, `STALE.never`; pull-to-refresh skips it), falls back to Oslo if denied/unsupported. `HomePage` calls it unconditionally so the prompt fires on load regardless of which widgets are mounted. Weather uses it for a real local forecast; Transit uses it for "📍 Nearby" real stops (only when the fix is real, not the Oslo fallback).
- **Weather** (`WeatherWidget.tsx` / phone tile → `WeatherDetails` popup, `useWeather` + `weatherApi.ts`): current conditions + detail row (pressure, cloud cover) + 12h hourly strip + 5-day forecast (`computeDailyForecast`, MET Norway compact product, midday-representative symbol per day).
- **Currency** (`CurrencyWidget.tsx` / phone tile → `CurrencyDetails` popup, `useCurrencyRates` + `currencyApi.ts`): **primary** NOK⇄TRY (direct — never routed through a third currency), **secondary** EUR⇄USD (their own parity), plus **gold** (XAU, confirmed available on the app's OXR plan via `/api/currencies.json`; shown as $/oz + NOK/gram with day-change badges). Replaced 5 confusing cross-rates that read as a wall of numbers.
- **Transit (`TransitPanel`, opened as a popup from Home's `TransitCard`)** — tabs **Routes** (default) · Departures · Settings. Routes = the planner: To home / To work (`ruter/PlaceButtons.tsx`, from Settings → Places), saved routes, "From here to" other saved stops, recent searches, From/To and an optional collapsed **Add a via stop** field (EnTur's `via` argument through `useTrips`; a via trip is neither recorded as a recent search nor saveable as a route — neither table has a via column). The separate Via tab is gone. Departures = the saved-stop board (saved + nearby stops, search, `QuaySavePanel`, directions). Supabase access lives in `api/transitStoreApi.ts`; reads in `hooks/useTransitQueries.ts` (keys from `qk.transit`/`qk.external`); every write (save/update/remove a stop, set default, add/remove a route) is a `useMutationWithFeedback` hook in `useTransitStops`/`useTransitRoutes`, and removals ask via `modal.confirm`. Line/brand colours are identity constants in `transitUtils`. Current behaviour + the verified-live gotchas:
  - Saving a stop: `SettingsTab` has its own **"Add a Stop"** section and Departures shares the same `QuaySavePanel.tsx` (extracted so the quay/direction/label flow isn't duplicated), with one-tap **🏠 Home / 💼 Work** presets and small bordered cards in a responsive grid. Both tabs show a **"📍 Nearby"** row from `ruterApi.fetchNearestStops` (EnTur's `nearest` root query — **verified live via GraphQL introspection before use**, per the no-speculative-schema rule).
  - **Quay identity (verified live at Visperud):** roadside stops have `publicCode` `""` (empty string, **not null**) AND a null description on every quay, so any code/description-based key merges distinct directions into one bucket. `DeparturesTab` groups by the quay's **raw NSR id**; `fetchDepartures` normalizes `""` → undefined at the boundary. `fetchStopDirections` collects ALL distinct destinations per quay (not just the first seen) and a shared `quayLabel()` renders them consistently in `QuaySavePanel`, `DeparturesTab` and `RoutesTab`'s `PlaceDisplay`.
  - **Filter at the DEPARTURE level, not the group level** — scoping a saved favourite's `quay_id` at group level meant the merged single group either matched wholesale or fell through to show-all. There's a "Show all directions" toggle and an honest "saved direction not found — showing all" state instead of a silent fallback.
  - **Migration `049`** widened `user_transit_stops`' unique index to include `quay_id` (saving two directions of the same physical stop used to hit a raw Postgres duplicate-key error); `useTransitStops.addStop` throws a typed `DuplicateStopError` the UI catches to offer `updateStop()` instead. Same migration added `lat`/`lon` so **address favourites** are saveable, gated behind an "Include addresses" checkbox (`StopSearchInput`'s `stopsOnly` prop).
  - `useTransitRecentSearches` wires up the previously-orphaned `transit_recent_searches` table (last 6 stop→stop searches, chips in `RoutesTab`). Geolocation auto-fills `RoutesTab`'s "From" once (never fights a manual clear). Favourite Routes in `SettingsTab` are clickable (`onSelectRoute` → `TransitPanel` switches to Routes and applies it once via `pendingRouteId`). `TripCard`'s summary shows an explicit "Leave in X min" / "Leave at HH:MM" instead of one ambiguous bold time; live line disruptions (EnTur `situations` on `line`) render as severity-coloured badges in `TripCard` + `DeparturesTab`. **Travel Profile** (`useTravelProfile`, localStorage like `useDayTargets`) persists walking pace / max transfers / wheelchair-accessible and is applied to every `fetchTrips()` via `TripPreferences`. The **via stop** (Routes) uses EnTur's verified `via: [TripViaLocationInput!]` argument (`gqlViaLocation()` builds `{visit: {stopLocationIds/coordinate}}`) and reuses `TripCard` unmodified — EnTur weaves the via stop into ONE coherent `TripPattern`.
  - **Real bug fixed:** `RoutesTab`'s "use current location" set an `'error'` state on GPS timeout/unavailable but rendered no message (only `'denied'` had visible text) — a silent failure violating the mandatory-toast rule; both paths now `toast.error(...)` on any geolocation failure.

### Media Feature Detail
Standard page frame; the header's Movies/TV `SegmentedControl` also drives search. Search + library share one card (Continue watching sits in it when Trakt has paused plays); layout is a PageBoard (`mediaBoard.ts`): phones and tablets stack the card, Discover, then the three tools as one grid (two columns once it is 36rem wide); 1280/1469 put the tools in a sticky side column; from 1920 the card and Discover span main + one side track; at 2450 the tools get two columns and Coming soon / Your stats open by default (`MEDIA_TOOLS_OPEN_FROM`). Coming soon and Your stats show placeholders while the library loads. Titles open as the `media` entity popup (`MediaDetailModal`); TMDB reads use `qk.media.tmdbQuery` + `STALE`. Key components:
- **Search (03.10.2026, owner: "results hide behind the next card; search should replace the overview")** — `MediaSearch` only edits the query (no dropdown). `useMediaSearchSession` keeps the box in LOCAL state and writes `?q=` once typing settles (300 ms; a router update per keystroke could drop letters on iOS). The first letter pushes ONE history entry tagged `{mediaSearch:true}`; ✕, Esc, the Overview tab and Back pop exactly that entry (`whenHistorySettled`); emptying the box keeps the session ("Keep typing"); leaving scrolls the overview to the top. While searching, `MediaSearchResults` takes Discover's slot (Continue watching and, on a phone, the tools step aside; wider pages keep their side columns): both types searched (`useSearchTitles`, infinite, 20 a page, `keepPreviousData` so refining never flashes a skeleton), "Movies 312 · TV 8" toggles switch the page's type, library belts on every cover, "20 of 312" + Show more. A title opens as the popup over the results, so closing it or Back returns to the same results and scroll. Phone step 1 has no Your library card (the Library tab is the same list).
- **Overview | Library | Lists** (header control, `?view=library&status=` / `?view=lists` so Back returns to the overview; 2026-09-30, owner: "too crowded"): the overview card holds search + `LibrarySummary` (a count chip per status — each opens the Library on that status — one row of posters: Watching, Paused, Coming soon, Wishlist, newest first — and Open library); `LibraryView` is the whole library (status chips, title search that folds accents, sort Recently added / Title / Release year / Your rating, a poster grid `minmax(6.5rem,1fr)`). One pure model, `libraryModel.ts` (`verify-media-library`). **Groups: Coming soon / Wishlist / Watching / Paused (TV) / Completed / Dropped.** **"Coming soon" is DATE-derived, not a manual status** (release/first_air date in the future) — the fix for "Wishlist ≠ Coming soon": Wishlist = want-to-watch AND already out; Coming soon = not out yet. Deriving it also auto-resolves the legacy manual `upcoming` movie status (nothing ever migrated it, so a released film stayed pinned under a stale "Upcoming" label forever). `CompactLibraryStrip` was deleted.
- `DiscoveryTabs` — Today/This Week/Popular/Upcoming/Norway tabs (scrollable row, refresh button, error state; the poster grid follows its own width: 6.5–9rem posters on a phone, from 36rem the columns share the row (`minmax(8rem,1fr)`)); manual sync only (24h stale)
- `EpisodesPanel` — season selector, per-episode watched checkbox (saves `watched_on`), plan checkbox → time blocks, Select All season. **Watched state is Netflix-style obvious**: season tabs show `n/total` + a green progress fill (✓ when complete); watched rows get a green tint, dimmed title and a filled green ✓ badge. Planning a **single** episode stamps `season_number`/`episode_number` on the created `time_blocks` row (migration `043`) — `source_id` alone identifies the show, not the episode, so this is what lets a DB trigger match "this episode got marked watched" back to exactly the one planned block and delete it. A multi-episode batch plan is intentionally NOT stamped and NOT auto-cleaned (deleting a 3-episode reminder because one was watched would be wrong); see AGENTS.md's "Linked-entity sync".
- `MediaDetailBody` = `MediaDetailInfo` (TMDB facts, error + Try again) + `MediaLibraryControls` (add/remove — remove confirms — status, rating, note, next episode): personal 1-10 rating, **private note** (`personal_note`, saves on blur), status buttons (movie completion stamps `watched_at`; TV completion/first-watch stamps `finished_at`/`started_at` — none of these columns were ever written before). Status pills: Wishlist/Watching/(Paused TV)/Completed/**Dropped**; the manual "Upcoming" pill was removed (coming-soon is date-derived). **"Advance to next episode"** uses `useNextEpisode` (real season rollover), fixing a bug where the last episode of a season recorded a nonexistent `S?E(n+1)` instead of `S(n+1)E1`. Star fill is `accent-500`.
- `TonightPicker` (random from My List / Trending / Popular) · `ReleaseCalendar` (upcoming from wishlist) · `MediaStats` (both on the shared `CollapsibleCard`; counts the real rows — films watched = completed entries, episodes = watched `user_tv_episodes` via `fetchEpisodeTally`, plus plays = 1 + `repeat_count` when rewatches differ, hours = runtime × plays — so it matches Trakt; it used to ESTIMATE episodes from each series' position, which is why it disagreed) · `MediaHeroArt` (a still drawn film strip behind the search card; replaced the rotating backdrops)
- **Episode watched has ONE write path:** `useMarkEpisodeWatched` (single or batch, optimistic with rollback, refreshes the `episodeWatched` group) — used by `EpisodesPanel`, `MediaLibraryControls` and Daily's `WatchNextCard`. Marking an already-watched episode never overwrites its date (`markEpisodeWatched` returns the existing row — it used to stamp today over a Trakt date). `EpisodesPanel`'s action bar splits the selection: unwatched → Mark watched; watched → **Watched again** (`rewatchEpisode`: `repeat_count` + 1, last watched = today; rows show "· N plays") or **Not watched** (confirmed). A watched movie shows the same pair in `MovieWatchedControls` (Watched again = `repeat_count` + 1; Not watched confirms, then Wishlist with no date or plays). Every one of these reaches Trakt through the outbox (below). A watch date Trakt doesn't know shows as "date unknown" (`traktDates.ts`).

DB tables: `movies`, `user_movie_entries` (watching/wishlist/completed/dropped/upcoming — `upcoming` is movie-only by design), `tv_series`, `user_tv_entries` (watching/wishlist/completed/dropped/paused), `user_tv_episodes` (season_number, episode_number, watched_at — **the ONE source of truth for TV progress**). Both entry types have `rating int (1-10)`, `genres jsonb`, `personal_note`, `priority`.

**TV progress architecture (migration `050`, root-cause fix)**: THREE disjoint progress systems used to coexist — a legacy `watched_episodes` table (bulk-marked data incl. The Office's 169 rows lived ONLY there; no current code read or wrote it), `user_tv_episodes` (what the UI writes), and `user_tv_entries.current_season/current_episode` cache columns that migration 011 promised "application logic" would maintain but never did — so Daily Watch-next, the AI briefing, Ask-AI context, `get_media`, the detail chip and MediaStats all showed S1·E0 forever despite hundreds of real watched rows. Migration 050 backfills `user_tv_episodes` from the legacy table (union — each had rows the other lacked), DROPS the legacy table, installs a trigger keeping the cache synced to max-watched, one-time recomputes it, and adds the missing UPDATE RLS on `movies`/`tv_series` (metadata upserts silently couldn't refresh — rows were frozen at first insert). `markEpisodeWatched`/`unmarkEpisodeWatched` also sync the cache app-side (instant UI, works pre-migration). Anything needing "next unwatched" uses `useNextEpisode` (max watched + TMDB season data → SxxEyy/title/air-date, rolls into the next season, `caughtUp` when none). **The cache columns are for cheap reads only — never write them directly.**

TMDB images: `posterUrl(path, size)` from `src/integrations/tmdb/client.ts`.

**Trakt (phase 1 of `docs/trakt/PLAN.md`, 30.09.2026).** Trakt becomes the source of truth for what it holds (watched + play counts, movie/show ratings, watchlist + order, favorites, dropped shows, paused progress, notes, the episode calendar); app-only data (paused status, dropped movies, priority, plans) stays local. One title = one row by `tmdb_id`; plays live in the existing `repeat_count` (= plays − 1, now on `user_tv_episodes` too) — no per-play history, no season/episode ratings, and `personal_note` IS the Trakt note. Built so far: migration `116` (ids + RT/Metacritic columns with partial unique indexes, the user-table columns, `trakt_tokens`/`trakt_sync_state`/`trakt_unmatched`/`trakt_outbox`), the `trakt-api` edge function (connect/status/disconnect/snapshot), and the Trakt card on Settings → Subscriptions with **Preview import** — a dry run (`traktPreview.ts`, pure, `verify-trakt-preview`) that writes nothing. The OAuth return (`?code=&state=` before the hash) is parked by `captureTraktCallback()` in `main.tsx` before the router starts, then finished by the card. `trakt_tokens` is service-role only and never enters `DB_CATALOG`. **Trakt API change (live since 03.07.2026, trakt-api discussion #775):** `/sync/watched/*` is paginated (100 items without a limit) and leaves out seasons unless `extended=progress` (≤ 100 per page) — the first snapshot read shows with no episodes and stopped at 100 movies; every list is now read through `getAll`. The preview's **Copy report** puts every count and the titles behind it on the clipboard (`previewReport`). **Phase 2 — the first import (30.09.2026):** the preview's **Import now** (confirmed once) runs `traktImport.ts` over a plan from the pure `traktImportPlan.ts` (`verify-trakt-import-plan`, 30): library first (catalogue rows from TMDB for new titles, entries, episodes, trakt/imdb/tvdb ids backfilled — a clashing id is skipped), then `trakt-api`'s `push` action sends the app-only facts (history — one entry per play, a minute apart — ratings, watchlist add/remove, dropped shows via `POST /users/hidden/dropped` (API blueprint, Add Hidden Items), one write per second) and stamps `trakt_sync_state`. Rules: Trakt wins where both know a fact; **watched or watching beats wishlist on both sides** (a watched title leaves the Trakt watchlist, a wishlist show with watched episodes becomes Watching); a show with episodes is Completed once every aired regular episode (TMDB `last_episode_to_air`) is watched, else Watching (Paused/Dropped kept; no TMDB details → Watching). Every write is an upsert, so a second run plans no pushes and no episode writes. Not yet: playback/favorites/notes (phase 5). **Phases 3–4 — automatic two-way sync (30.09.2026, migration `117`):** (1) **App → Trakt:** triggers on `user_movie_entries`/`user_tv_entries`/`user_tv_episodes` queue every change to a Trakt-held fact in `trakt_outbox` (watched, extra plays, rating, watchlist, dropped; a movie moved to Wishlist or removed, and an episode unmarked, remove their plays) — from ANY writer (web, AI, SQL), only while Trakt is connected. A write that carries the `x-trakt-sync: 1` request header (the sync's own client; PostgREST exposes it as `request.headers`) is skipped, so nothing loops. (2) `trakt-api`'s **`sync`** sends the outbox oldest-first (same-op runs batched, 1 write/s, a failure keeps its order and backs off), then reads `last_activities` and, only when it changed, mirrors Trakt into the library with the pure `traktSyncPlan.ts` (`verify-trakt-sync-plan`, 39): additions AND removals, leaving alone items still waiting in the outbox or just sent this run; app-only states (Paused, a Watching/Dropped movie, a show marked Watching/Completed without episode rows) stay; an entry is deleted only when Trakt holds none of its facts and it has no note; more than max(10, 10 %) removals at once are **held back** until the owner confirms on the card. Runs from the pg_cron job `lascis-trakt-sync` (:07/:37, `x-sync-secret`), 4 s after a media edit here (`scheduleTraktSync`, silent — errors land on the card), and **Sync now**. One run at a time (`trakt_sync_state.sync_started_at`, stale after 5 min). The first **import** also runs on the server now (`import` action; `traktImport.ts` is gone); both apply through one server path, and the pure plans are GENERATED into the function by `scripts/sync-trakt-shared.mjs` (`--check`). **Unknown watch dates:** Trakt's "unknown date" is the epoch — movies store NULL (117 fixed existing rows), episodes keep the epoch (NULL there means not watched to every reader) and every screen shows "date unknown". **No TMDB data = no guessing:** without TMDB's aired count the import and the sync keep a show's own Completed/Watching (a failed or rejected `TMDB_API_KEY` once demoted finished shows to Watching); a TMDB 401 stops the server run with a named error, the app's `tmdbFetch` says the key was rejected, and **Sync now** (a full sync) re-checks every show's status, which repairs earlier demotions. **No duplicates, by the database:** `movies`/`tv_series.tmdb_id` UNIQUE, the trakt/imdb/tvdb ids partial-unique, `user_movie_entries (user_id, movie_id)`, `user_tv_entries (user_id, tv_series_id)` and `user_tv_episodes (user_id, tv_series_id, season, episode)` unique — every import write is an upsert on those keys (a second preview after the import read 55/32/2153 all "Already the same"). **Phases 5–6 + lists (30.09.2026, migration `118`):** **favorites** two-way (a heart on the title page; `is_favorite` changes queue `favorite_add/remove` under the key `fav:<type>:<tmdb>` so they never hold back the title's mirror; the sync mirrors `/sync/favorites` when `last_activities.favorites` moved — an empty answer while > 5 are favorited is taken as an outage); **Continue watching** (Trakt `/sync/playback`, read live, a strip in the search card, hidden when empty); **my calendar** (`/calendars/my/shows`, next 33 days, merged into Coming soon); **Lists** = Trakt personal lists read live (Media → **Lists** view `?view=lists`, "Add to list" menu + New list dialog on every title page, **Save as list** on a film's franchise strip from TMDB `belongs_to_collection`); **scores** from MDBList (`ratings` action for an opened title; the sync refreshes library titles older than a week, ≤ 200 per type) → Rotten Tomatoes critics/audience, Metacritic, IMDb, Letterboxd chips on the title page as small tiles (brand mark, value, Fresh/Upright/out of 10 — no vote counts are stored), 🍅 on Library tiles and a Rotten Tomatoes sort. **Notes are not synced yet** (`personal_note` stays app-only). API facts: `docs/trakt/PLAN.md` §12. **Media UI round (same day):** the title popup is `xl` with a left rail (poster, status, watched/plays, rating + favorite, note, actions) and the TMDB facts, episodes and similar titles on the right (phones: facts → controls → episodes); **Watched up to here** (per-episode icon + "Up to here" in the selection bar) marks every earlier unmarked episode, earlier seasons by TMDB's episode count, in ONE upsert (`markEpisodesWatched`); the rotating trending backdrop became a still drawn film strip (`MediaHeroArt`, no network); posters are compact (5.5–7.5rem, `w185` images); Discover fetches only the tab on screen and What-to-watch only its picked source. **Media ideas round (30.09.2026):** (1) **When watched?** — completing a movie, completing a series, Mark watched / Watched again / Up to here on episodes and Next episode ask like Trakt: Just now · Release date · Other date · Unknown date (`WatchedWhenSheet` via `useWatchedWhenPrompt`, pure `watchedWhen.ts` `resolveWatchedAt`, `verify-watched-when`); Release date gives each episode its own air date (`episodeAirDates`), Unknown stores NULL for movies and the epoch for episodes; completing a series marks every aired unwatched episode in one upsert (≤ 500 rows). (2) **Airing this week** (`AiringThisWeek`/`useAiringThisWeek`: Trakt calendar when connected, else TMDB `next_episode_to_air` of Watching/Paused shows) on Daily's Watch next card and Home's media widget. (3) **Year** view (`?view=year`, pure `yearReview.ts`, `verify-year-review`): time watched, movies, episodes, rewatches, a month chart and genre/show chips — every tile, bar and chip opens the titles behind it (`YearDrillList`); a play counts on the one date kept per title/episode. (4) Discover **My services** (Norway watch providers picked once, `mediaPrefsStore` on this device, TMDB `/discover` with `with_watch_providers`). (5) What to watch: a **Trakt list** source (e.g. "Watch together") and a length limit (≤ 90 min / ≤ 2 h). (6) **Follows** (migration `119`, `media_follows` + `media_follow_events`): see `docs/trakt/PLAN.md` §11 — no push notification yet. (7) `ModalShell` dropped its backdrop blur (measured: p95 frame 82 → 17 ms while scrolling a popup), and the episode selection bar wraps onto two rows. **Media round 2 (30.09.2026, migration `120`):** (1) **Covers** — one `PosterTile` (Library, Lists, Discover, the overview row; `compact` on the franchise strip and More like this): bigger (`POSTER_GRID` 7.5–9rem), the status as a **belt across the top-left corner** (`BUCKET_TONE` → `.poster-belt`, fixed dark colours so white 11px text passes contrast on artwork in both themes; sized in `cqw` of the cover so it centres at every width), the Rotten Tomatoes score as a banner along the bottom (`RT 87%`, fresh/rotten dot; library titles only), your own rating never on a cover, and a ticket mark for cinema visits. (2) **Cinema visits** — `movie_cinema_visits` (one row per visit: date, cinema, where, with whom, cost + currency NOK/TRY/EUR/USD, note; owner RLS, `trg_audit`, FK RESTRICT to the shared `movies` catalogue): a "Watched at a cinema?" toggle on a Completed/Watching movie, then Details / Another visit (`CinemaVisits`, `CinemaVisitSheet`). (3) **The popup navigates in place** — `MediaEntityModal` keeps a trail; a title opened inside it (franchise strip, More like this) replaces the content, each step pushes its own history entry (`BackStep` → `useHistoryDismiss`), so mouse/browser Back steps back title by title, then closes; closing with steps jumps back past all of them (`history.go(-n)`, a 600 ms fallback) so no dead entries stay behind; adding a title reached inside keeps the popup open. Known gap: a route change still leaves the popup's (now n+1) history entries. (4) **Lists** — a rail (Trakt lists + **smart lists**) with the picked list beside it; a picker on phones. A smart list = a `media_follows` row (franchise / studio / **keyword** / person) whose films are read live from TMDB (`useSmartListTitles`: collection parts, `/discover/movie` by company or keyword newest-first up to 200 with a "newest N of M" note, person credits without "Self" cameos; "Hide documentaries and TV movies" on by default for studio/keyword). `ListGrid` + pure `listModel.ts` give every list the same filters (All · Watched · In progress · Not watched · In my library · Not in library · Coming soon), sort and a watched-progress bar. What's new (`FollowingPanel`) is the events feed under it. (5) **Dates** — the when-watched sheet takes a day only (`DateInput`, stored as midday); movies get **Change date**, completed series **Change date** on `finished_at` (`SeriesFinishedControls`), episodes **Change date** on the selection and **Dates** for every watched episode (`setEpisodesWatchedAt`, one upsert per 500; Release date skips episodes TMDB has no air date for). A title/episode watched more than once asks first: the app keeps one date, so every play gets it. The outbox triggers (120) resend a changed date to Trakt (remove plays, re-add at the new date); `trakt_outbox.seq` orders the drain (rows of one trigger call share `created_at`) and `pickBatch` sends interleaved remove/add runs as one request each while keeping every item's own order. (6) **Discover** (`discoverModel.ts`, pure): Trending (Today / This week), Popular (= `/discover` of the last three years with a vote floor — TMDB's `/movie/popular` ranks old and odd titles by page views), Top rated, In cinemas (Norwegian theatrical, six weeks) / On the air (TV), Upcoming, Norway, My services (flatrate + free + ads); Filters: genre (TMDB's own list), from year, TMDB score, order, hide titles in my library; Show more pages (`useDiscover`, infinite). Params are verified against TMDB's docs, not a live sample (no key in the authoring session). `verify-media-lists` covers lists and Discover. **Add to library (follow-up):** adding a title as Completed asks when it was watched like switching to Completed does (movie: the date goes in with the new row; series: every aired episode is marked with the answer), and adding keeps the popup open on the title — only removing the opened title closes it (`onRemoved`). Airing this week without Trakt also checks Completed shows, so a finished show's new season shows up (Trakt's calendar already includes them); there is still no push notification. Discover's **Show more** adds 40 (two TMDB pages per step, `useDiscover`), and Filters gained **Hide genres** / **Hide languages** checklists (several each; saved on this device in `mediaPrefsStore` v2, genres per Movies/TV): genres go to TMDB as `without_genres` and are re-checked on the client (trending), languages are filtered on the client by `original_language` (TMDB has no language exclusion). `verify-media-lists` (42). **Round 3 (01.10.2026):** a title outside the library reads **Unwatched** (not stored): picking any other status adds it in that status (toast "Added to library · Completed"), picking Unwatched on a library title removes it (confirmed) — the Add to library button is gone. Lists are one kind: one rail and one **New list** ("I add titles" = a Trakt list, "Fill automatically" = the franchise/studio/keyword/person source, marked ✦; `AutoListPicker` replaced `SmartListDialog`). `.scroll-x` shows a thin scrollbar with a mouse/trackpad (`pointer: fine`), still hidden on touch. The title page labels its numbers (Released …, Runtime …, TMDB users ★ x/10). The half star in `StarRating` clips a full-width star (centring it in the clip drew a shifted second star). **Lists are one, in the data too (01.10.2026, supersedes the "smart lists" and Round 3 list notes above):** every list is a private **Trakt** list, so Trakt shows exactly what the Lists page shows. "Fill automatically" (New list), **Follow** on a title page and the franchise strip's **Save as list** all go through `useCreateAutoList` (`hooks/useAutoLists.ts`): create the Trakt list (or reuse an unlinked one with the same name, so nothing is doubled), fill it from TMDB (`fetchSmart`; documentaries and TV movies left out except for franchises; ≤ 100 films per request, one request per second), and keep only the **rule** in `media_follows` (`kind`, `tmdb_id`, `trakt_list_id`) — the daily `follows_check` adds new films to that same list. The rail lists only Trakt lists (✦ = has a rule); a rule without a list on Trakt (older ones, made without Trakt, or its list deleted on Trakt) shows under **Not on Trakt yet** with **Put on Trakt** / **Put all on Trakt**. **Stop filling** (and unfollowing) keeps the list on Trakt as a normal list; **Delete list** on a ✦ list deletes the Trakt list and its rule. Without Trakt only the rule is saved. No migration (the `trakt_list_id` column is from 119). Known gap: `follows_check` ignores a failed add (e.g. Trakt's 420 account limit), so a new film can miss the list until added by hand. **Round 4 (02.10.2026):** (1) **Covers:** a non-English title gets its original language as a belt across the bottom-right corner (`PosterTile` `language`; library rows read it from the stored TMDB record `movies/tv_series.metadata_json.original_language` — no migration), and a Coming soon belt prints the release date under "Soon". (2) **Discover Upcoming** asks TMDB for Norwegian dates (`region: 'NO'`) and drops anything whose own release date is before yesterday (`applyClientFilters(…, today)`): without the region TMDB matched a film released here whose release elsewhere was still ahead, so it showed in both In cinemas and Upcoming (owner report, "Runner"). Upcoming tiles show the full date. (3) **Minimum votes** filter (IMDb's "Number of votes" steps: 100 / 1,000 / 10,000 / 100,000 → TMDB `vote_count.gte`, never below the tab's own floor; applied on the client for trending), and tiles show the vote count beside the TMDB score ("TMDB 8.3 (1.2k)"). IMDb's own vote counts are not stored (MDBList gives only the rating). (4) **Queue** = a private Trakt list named "Queue" (owner's call: Trakt sees what the app shows): **Add to Queue** / "In Queue · #3" on every title page (`QueueButton`, creates the list on first use), the Queue leads the Lists rail and opens as an ordered list (`QueuePanel`: move up/down/top, Plan, remove, Remove N watched) whose order is saved on Trakt through `trakt-api`'s new `list_reorder` (`POST /users/me/lists/{id}/items/reorder`, rank = every list item id). Pure `queue/queueModel.ts` (`verify-media-queue`). (5) **Release reminders** (migration `122`, `media_release_reminders`: offsets of 30/7/1/0 days and the ones sent): a bell on any title not out yet (`ReleaseReminderButton`); `push-send`'s morning run sends one push per due title (only the nearest when mornings were missed, never a burst) — `reminders/reminderRules.ts` is hand-mirrored there (`verify-media-reminders`). A reminder whose release date TMDB moved is refreshed when its page opens. **Round 5 (02.10.2026):** (1) A cover's original language is its **full name** (`languageName` — TMDB's `cn` = Cantonese, `xx` = none, the rest via `Intl.DisplayNames`) in the bottom band, beside the Rotten Tomatoes score (either alone is centred); still non-English only (`coverLanguage`). The title popup shows "Original language Japanese · also English" (TMDB `original_language` + `spoken_languages`). (2) **Discover filters are a draft** (`FilterRow`): picking changes nothing until **Search** (Undo changes, Clear all), so a run of changes is one request. (3) **Real bug:** the list's query key left out the vote floor and hidden genres, so changing them showed the list fetched for the previous filters ("wrong films" / "nothing"); the key is now the exact request (`discoverKey`, page 1's params minus the page). When client-side filters (hidden languages, trending's genre/score…) leave fewer than 12 titles the list fetches up to 6 steps by itself before saying nothing matches, and Upcoming says which filters it ignores (`ignoredFilters`). `verify-media-lists` (64). **Round 6 (02.10.2026, supersedes the older Year / Your stats notes):** (1) Discover's filter row is compact — the field name is each select's first option (Genre / Year / Score / Votes / Order), "Not in library", Genres / Languages, Search · Undo · Clear on the same row. (2) The title popup's picture is taller (`h-36 sm:h-56`) and loses its light from top to bottom; the left rail is sticky (`md:top-5`) and as tall as the scroll area at most, scrolling inside itself when longer. (3) **Status colours are shared stages** (`shared/theme/stage.ts`, THEME §2.4; Games' Playing is now cyan and Completed green); the popup's status picker is an aligned two-column grid of `.stage-option`s. (4) **Previous / next in the popup**: `open({ kind: 'media', tmdbId, mediaType, sequence })` carries the list it was opened from in its on-screen order (Library after its filter/sort/search, Discover, Lists, the overview's rows, search results, Coming soon, Continue watching, Queue, Stats lists) — arrows on the picture, ←/→ keys, a horizontal swipe on the picture, and "3 / 12"; titles reached inside the popup (More like this) don't step. (5) Your library sits in the right-hand column from the laptop (`summary` section, `MEDIA_BOARD`). (6) **Stats** replaces Year (`?view=stats`; `?view=year` still opens it) and the overview's Your stats card is gone: All time or a year — time watched, movies, episodes, rewatches, watch time by year (tap → that year) or by month, genres and shows by time, when you watch (weekdays, active days, busiest day, longest run), your ratings vs TMDB users (spread, 1.5-point disagreements) and the library by status now; every tile, bar and row opens its titles (`components/stats/`, pure `yearReview.ts` with `year: null` = all time, which also counts completed films with no watch date). `verify-year-review` (30), `verify-status-stages` (28).

### Shop Feature Detail
Wishlist/shopping-planner under `src/features/shop/`. Strict **2-level** category tree — `shop_categories.parent_id` null = top category, set = subcategory; items always attach to a subcategory (`shop_items.category_id`), never to a top category. `ShopPage` is the one `fullHeight` route (sized by `<main>`, not a viewport calc): `ShopAIBox` (internal scroll + pinned input — never grows the page) is a permanent side column from 1024px, opens from a header button at 768–1023px and is a docked bottom bar on phones; the other pane = category pills + items in 17–21rem columns (own scroll).
- `ShopAIBox` — chat panel (separate from the app-wide ✦ Ask AI panel) using `sendShopMessage` with a shopping-companion system prompt: converses about plans (not just add-item commands), handles a pasted multi-item basket, never guesses a new category silently.
- Clarifying questions use a real Gemini function call — `ask_clarifying_question(question, options[])` — not text-parsed conventions. `ai-proxy`'s loop **short-circuits** on this call (the answer must come from the human as a real next turn, not a synthesized tool response) and returns `{ text, quickReplies }`; `ShopAIBox` renders `quickReplies` as tappable buttons.
- `AddShopItemModal` (on `ModalShell`) — manual add: top category / subcategory cascading selects, each with "+ New…" to create on the fly. `ShopItemCard` — title, notes, price (+ `price_source` `manual`|`ai_estimate`), platform, link, priority dot, region flag (🇹🇷/🇳🇴), planned date, mark bought/delete.
- No live price-lookup API (Prisjakt/Akakçe have no public free API) — price is manual entry; the AI can only give a rough text estimate on request, never auto-writes a price.
- DB: `shop_categories` (id, user_id, name, parent_id), `shop_items` (category_id, title, notes, price, price_source, platform, url, priority, region `TR`/`NO`, planned_date, status `wishlist`/`bought`/`dropped`, source_type `manual`/`ai`). Migrations `029_shop.sql` + `030_shop_seed_categories.sql` (partial-unique indexes + a starter taxonomy, seeded idempotently for every existing user).
- AI tools (shared with the main Ask AI panel): `get_shop_categories`, `create_shop_category`, `create_shop_item`, `ask_clarifying_question`.

### Food Feature Detail

User-visible name is **Food** (nav tab, CommandBar, page h1); route stays `/recipes` for deep links. `RecipesPage` has 4 tabs: **Today** (default) · **Library** · **Ingredients** · **Meal Plan**, plus `FoodTabs` (Food | Shop) and "Log food" in the header row. No banner photo (`RecipeBackdrop` was deleted as decoration). Every Food popup opens by id through `useEntityModal` (`food-log`, `food-log-edit`, `meal-plan`, `recipe`, `recipe-view`, `day-targets`); deletes confirm via `modal.confirm`.

**Data model — 4 tables** (after migration `061` unified plan + diary):
- `recipes` — title, description, `servings` (base count), instructions, `macro_mode` `manual`|`from_ingredients`, per-serving `calories`/`protein_g`/`carbs_g`/`fat_g`/`sugar_g`/`fiber_g`, `category` (breakfast/lunch/dinner/snack/supplement), `image_url`, `source_url`, `times_cooked`, `is_temp`.
- `recipe_ingredients` — name, `quantity` (nullable = to-taste), unit, note, sort_order, `library_ingredient_id` (nullable FK). Quantities are stored for the base `servings`; recipe macros are stored **per serving**.
- `recipe_ingredient_library` — reusable ingredient catalog, macros **always per 100g**, unique per `(user_id, name)`, + `fiber_g`, portion presets (`serving_label`/`serving_grams`, e.g. "1 scoop"=30g), `food_group`, `image_url`, and `source`/`source_ref` provenance (`off`, `dsld`, `matvaretabellen`, user's own; user's own wins on name conflict, attribution travels on the row, idempotent refresh).
- **`food_log_entries` — plan AND diary in one table**, discriminated by `status`: `'planned'` (intent; macros computed live on read) vs `'eaten'` (a real at-log-time **snapshot**). Planning → eating is a status flip. Slots: breakfast/lunch/dinner/snack/**supplement**; unlimited rows per slot; exactly one of `recipe_id` / `library_ingredient_id`+qty+unit / `custom_title`.
- **`water_log_entries`** (migration `067`) — one row per logged amount; the day's total = `SUM(amount_ml)`. **Deliberately its own table, never a food slot** — water carries no macros and must never enter the calorie ring. Owner-only RLS, `user_id DEFAULT auth.uid()`, no audit trigger (high-frequency, low audit value — same reasoning as the bulk-sync exemption).

**Load-bearing contracts (do not break):**
