- **Totals are DERIVED, never frozen (user decision 2026-09-26 — reverses the old "snapshot at log time" rule).** An eaten row's macros always equal what its source adds up to now: a library item (per-100g × grams) or a recipe (per-serving × servings); a `'from_ingredients'` recipe's totals always equal its ingredients. Any add/edit/delete recalculates — past days included — because the user eats more or less based on these numbers. Migration `106` makes the database enforce it for every writer (web, `phone-gateway`, `ai-proxy`): triggers on `recipe_ingredients`, `recipes`, `recipe_ingredient_library` and a BEFORE trigger on `food_log_entries`. Custom rows (no source) and manual recipes keep their typed values, planned rows stay NULL, and a deleted source (FK `SET NULL`) leaves the last computed values. Until 106 is applied the web app does the same itself (`refreshEatenEntries` in `foodLogApi.ts`, called by `useUpdateRecipe` and `useUpdateIngredientLibraryItem`). Live data was repaired once on 2026-09-26 (4 saved meals, 25 eaten rows — e.g. Sushi 546 → 820 kcal).
- The original `UNIQUE (user_id, date, meal_slot)` on the old plan table **had to die** — it physically forbade "lunch = chicken + rice + broccoli".
- **Planned rows render muted "planned" and are NEVER added to the eaten total** (the ring sums `eaten` only) — counting them was visually contradictory with the ring.
- `dayNutritionApi`'s plan-side query deliberately does **NOT** select `recipes.fiber_g` (a missing column would error the whole plan query on old schema; plan rows don't feed the ring anyway).
- **Pre-migration fallback pattern (reused across the feature):** on a column-missing error (`42703` / `PGRST204`) retry once with the new column dropped — `createRecipe`/`updateRecipe` for `fiber_g` and `is_temp`, `create/updateIngredientLibraryItem` for `image_url`, and `foodLogApi`/`mealPlanApi`/`dayNutritionApi` for `status` (they fall back to the old two-table behaviour). This is what let the frontend deploy before migration 061 was applied.
- **Cache-key bug (real):** food-log hooks invalidated `['day-nutrition']`, which does NOT prefix-match the card's `['meal-plan','day-nutrition',date]` key — the card showed stale totals. Invalidate the actual key.
- **Recipe macros must follow their ingredients (real bug, fixed 2026-09-26).** Two disconnects froze calories: (1) the logger's "Save meal" stored `macro_mode: 'manual'` with a total computed once, so editing that meal's ingredients later never changed its calories — it now saves `'from_ingredients'`, and `RecipeModal` opens any manual recipe whose ingredients are all library-linked by weight (`canComputeFromIngredients`; links are only editable in from-ingredients mode, so only old saved meals look like this) as `'from_ingredients'`; (2) editing a library ingredient never touched the recipes built from it — `useUpdateIngredientLibraryItem` now calls `recomputeRecipesUsingIngredient` (recomputes every `'from_ingredients'` recipe using it, and switches those old manual saved meals over). Eaten diary rows follow too (see the derived-totals rule above). `replaceIngredients` also checks its delete error now (a failed delete + insert duplicated every ingredient).
- `macro_mode: 'from_ingredients'` is computed on save (`computeMacrosFromIngredients` in `recipesApi.ts`): sum each linked library ingredient's per-100g macros × `quantity/100`, ÷ servings. Only rows with a `library_ingredient_id` AND a weight/volume unit contribute; the rest are skipped and surfaced in the UI. `RecipeModal` previews the same math client-side (`previewMacros`). `dayNutritionApi` imports recipesApi's `WEIGHT_UNITS` (a narrower local copy once dropped 'milliliter(s)').

**Components / flows:**
- **`FoodLogModal`** — the build-a-meal logger (`sm:max-w-xl lg:max-w-3xl`, 2-column on `lg`: left = recents / saved meals / search / new ingredient, right = the basket). **Recents-only suggestions** — the alphabetical library dump was removed; ingredient match chips render only while searching. Grams or portion-preset tap; live kcal/protein total; footer shows remaining protein/kcal **after** this meal. "Save meal" is **save-only** (no auto-log) with a "Makes N portions" field, so a batch is stored at correct per-serving macros and you log your portion later.
- **Ingredients** (`INGREDIENT_BOARD`): the add/edit form is a sticky left rail; the food list spans the rest and splits its rows into 1/2/3 columns by its own width (68/100rem).
- **Favourites + hidden Recents (migration `087`, `food_favorites` + `food_recent_hidden`)** — Recent (`useRecentFoods`) is derived purely from eaten history and can't hold something you haven't logged in a while or want to guarantee stays reachable, so Favourites is a SEPARATE, pinned shortcut list, always rendered first. Both the Recents and Favourites grids are collapsible (chevron header, open by default) and share `FoodTile` (`foodLogKit.tsx`) — a photo tile whose main body is the "add this" tap target, with a ★ favourite-toggle and (Recent only) a ✕ "remove from Recent" corner button as SIBLINGS of the inner button, never nested inside it (a `<button>` inside a `<button>` is invalid HTML and gets silently hoisted out by the parser). Corner buttons are a deliberate 24px exception to the mobile-first 44px rule, matching the pre-existing `onEditRecipe` ✎ button's own precedent on the saved-meals strip — a secondary, occasional action layered on a dense grid, not the tile's primary ≥44px tap target. `FoodThumb` gained an optional `sizeClass` prop (Tailwind width/height utilities) for when a call site needs the thumbnail to shrink on mobile — an inline `size` px value always wins over a class, so the two can't be mixed at one call site; the Recents/Favourites grids use `sizeClass="w-9 h-9 sm:w-11 sm:h-11"` (was a fixed 44px at every breakpoint). Both tables are pre-migration-safe (`fetchHiddenRecentKeys`/`fetchFoodFavorites` degrade to empty; a write throws a named "migration 087 not applied" error).
- **"As meal" — several individually-logged items collapse into ONE compact diary line** (migration `087`, `food_log_entries.meal_group_id`). The real complaint: logging a lunch item-by-item (bread, chicken, onion, rice) turned the day view into a wall of separate rows. A checkbox in the basket header — squeezed into the existing "This meal · N" row, never its own row, default ON, only shown once the basket has 2+ items — tags every item in `handleSave`'s batch insert with one shared `crypto.randomUUID()` `meal_group_id` (a group of one has nothing to compact, so it's only assigned above 1 item). `dayNutritionApi.ts`'s `groupDayMeals()` (pure, order-preserving) collapses same-`meal_group_id` rows back into a `MealGroupRow` (summed macros, "`first item title` +N more") for display; `FoodTodayTab`'s slot list renders that as one row with a chevron — tap to expand into each item's own real row (still individually editable/deletable) via a per-slot-card `expandedGroups` set, collapsed by default. Pre-migration-safe: the query retries without `meal_group_id` on a `42703`/`PGRST204`, and `addFoodLogEntries` does the same on insert — every row then reads as its own ungrouped item, today's behaviour.
- **Meal-slot rows — all macros, aligned columns (real complaint, fixed).** Every logged/planned row used to show only calories + protein (`{protein_g}p`), and the amount was baked straight into the title string ("Chicken · 150g" — "gramaj yapışık" per the report). `FoodTodayTab`'s `mealLine()` now splits `title` into name + quantity via `splitTitleQty()` (splits on the same " · " separator both `unifiedToMeal` branches already use, only treating the tail as a quantity if it starts with a digit — a name that legitimately contains " · " itself is never garbled) and renders TWO markups: a `hidden sm:grid` desktop row with the amount, kcal, protein, carbs, fat and fiber each in their own aligned `grid-cols-[...]` column (never crammed into the name), and a `sm:hidden` mobile version — the name/kcal/actions row, then a small `text-[11px]` macro-summary line underneath. Sugar is deliberately left out of this dense per-row listing (still visible via ✎ → `EditFoodLogModal`) — a documented simplification, not an oversight.
- **Temp meals (migration `066`, `recipes.is_temp`)** — a saved meal is **temp by default**: hidden from the Library grid (`RecipesPage` filters `!is_temp`) but fully usable — it appears in the logger's "Saved meals" strip, and hover (desktop) / the ✎ editor (mobile, `onEditRecipe` → RecipeModal) reveal its ingredients. An inline "Library" checkbox on the save row promotes it to a permanent Library recipe.
- **`MealPortionPicker`** — tapping a saved meal picks a free **%** of the WHOLE batch (25/50/75/100 chips + free entry, + grams when total weight is computable) → `logRecipe(recipe, servingsEaten)` where `servingsEaten = pct/100 × recipe.servings`. `RecipeDetail`'s "I ate this" is a free-entry portions input with a "% of the batch · logs N kcal" hint.
- **`EditFoodLogModal`** — edit a logged row (was delete-only): kind inferred from the row (library → edit grams + re-snapshot · recipe → edit servings + re-snapshot · custom → edit title+macros), and any of them can move meal slot. Wired to ✎ on every diary row (Daily NutritionCard, FoodTodayTab, MealPlanWeek's eaten chips). `DayMeal` carries a `logEntry` sub-object (raw ids/qty/unit) so the modal knows the row kind.
- **`MealPlanWeek`** — 7-day × slot grid; each cell shows 📋 planned (tap → `AssignMealModal`) + green ✓ eaten chips (tap → edit), because a Today-logged day used to leave the plan grid empty. Empty cell = the whole area is one big centered `+`; filled cell gets a full-width bottom "＋ add" row. Every planned row has a **✓ confirm** (`mealPlanApi.eatPlannedEntry` / `useEatPlannedEntry`) that computes a fresh snapshot and flips planned→eaten so it starts counting in the ring. `AssignMealModal` modes: 🍲 Recipe / 🥚 Ingredient / **✏️ Type it** (free-text one-off).
- **`FoodTodayTab`** — laid out by `FOOD_TODAY_BOARD` (`foodBoards.ts`): Nutrition and Water as a rail on the left (with Last 7 days and Coach at 1280/1469), the meal slots in main, Last 7 days and Coach in their own columns from 1920: calorie (accent) + protein rings (the shared `ProgressRing`), macro chips (one chart colour per macro in `recipes/macroColors.ts` — calories series 2, protein 1, carbs 3 (plum), fat 4, fiber 5; over target = danger), sugar, `WaterTracker`, `GoalSummary` (Edit goal opens the `day-targets` popup), and the coach card. Aligned per-macro columns switch on by the slot card's OWN width (container query ≥40rem). Its own `DateNav` drives `foodDate`.
- **`useNutritionCoach` → THE goal decision (`health/goal/cutDecision.ts`, 2026-09-29, owner: "same data → one confident answer")** — one pure module every coaching surface renders: the goal editor's coach (`GoalCoachBlock`) and Food · Today's coach card (both via `CoachDecisionView`), Health → Goal progress (`goalPath.applyDecision` sets the path headline + calorie/protein steps; the Pace pill is `paceLabel`). The hook reads the goal report's 28-day window (`useGoalReport(28)`: fitted trend, paired intake/Apple burn, `observedTdee` = the scale burn, latest scale body fat %), Muscle watch's level and the last 7 days' steps. **Protein:** with body fat, 2.8 g/kg lean mass clamped to 1.6–2.2 g/kg BW, range max(1.6 BW, 2.3 FFM)–min(2.4 BW, 3.1 FFM) (83 kg / 24.6 % → "Aim ~175 g (145–195)"); without body fat 2.0 (cut) / 1.8 g/kg (Morton 2018, Helms 2014, Jäger 2017). **Floor (heuristic):** max(1500, 500 + 22 × FFM (Cunningham; 24 × BW without body fat), 75 % of the scale burn); it only limits cutting and never triggers a diet break — that is named only after 12+ weeks of cutting or Muscle watch `likely_loss`. **Precedence (first wins, one headline + ≤ 2 lines):** 1 not enough data (< 4 of the last 7 complete days fully logged, < 10 weigh-ins over 14 days) → what's missing · 2 losing > 1 %/wk or `likely_loss` → eat more · 3 cut < 0.5 %/wk and the needed cut would cross the floor or the diary likely misses food (logged < 85 % of Apple burn − scale deficit) → check logging + add steps toward 10k (~0.0006 kcal/step/kg), no cut · 4 cut < 0.5 %/wk with room → the smallest DAILY cut to 0.5 %/wk (≥ 50, rounded to 50, clamped at the floor, 14-day cooldown) · 5 0.5–1 %/wk → on track; maintain/gain keep `paceAdvice` behind gates 1–2. On a cut 0.35–0.5 %/wk reads "Slow but fine" (neutral), never danger. `verify-cut-decision` (65, incl. the owner's exact gate-3 text). Daily's Nutrition card only takes the per-meal hint from the week's weight (no coach there). **`useDayTargets` (migration `086`, `day_targets` — a singleton row per user, same PK-is-user_id pattern as `athlete_profile`)**: calories/protein/water/goal/`last_calorie_adjust` used to live ONLY in localStorage (`'lasci.dayTargets'`) — a second device or a cleared browser silently reset them to the hardcoded defaults with no way to recover the real numbers. `dayTargetsApi.ts` follows the same pre-migration-safe convention as `athleteProfileApi.ts` (a missing-table READ degrades to the same `DAY_TARGETS_DEFAULTS` the old localStorage version shipped; a missing-table WRITE throws a named "migration 086 not applied" error). The hook keeps its old `{ targets, update }` shape exactly — every one of its 4 consumers (`NutritionCard`, `FoodTodayTab`, `WaterTracker`, `useNutritionCoach`) needed zero changes — but is now a react-query hook with an optimistic `onMutate` (a goal pill tap or a stepper click still feels instant, not gated on a round trip) and `placeholderData: DAY_TARGETS_DEFAULTS` (renders synchronously on first paint, same as before). **Real bug, fixed same day it shipped:** the first version used `initialData` instead of `placeholderData` — `initialData` stamps `dataUpdatedAt` as "now" the instant the hook mounts, so combined with `staleTime: 5min` the query read as already-fresh from the very first render and `fetchDayTargets` never actually ran on a normal page load. Every WRITE still landed in the DB fine, but a plain reload silently kept showing the hardcoded defaults instead of the real saved goal — reading exactly like "saving to the DB doesn't work" (a save's own `onSettled` invalidate was the only thing that ever forced a real fetch, so editing a goal right after loading could look fine while a fresh reload reverted it). `placeholderData` shows the same instant fallback without marking the query as already-fetched, so the real fetch always runs on mount. **Per-goal saved targets + explicit Save (migration `088`, `day_target_profiles` — the user's own explicit correction to the autosave design):** the Goals editor used to write on every tap (goal pill, every stepper click) straight to the single active `day_targets` row — switching Cut→Maintain→Cut lost Cut's own numbers (Maintain's overwrote them), and there was no way to compose several changes before committing. Fixed on two axes: (1) `day_target_profiles` (PK `(user_id, goal)`) keeps one saved `{calories, protein_g, water_ml}` set PER GOAL; `upsertDayTargets` mirrors every write into that goal's own profile row, and tapping a goal pill in the editor recalls that goal's saved profile instead of carrying over whatever the previous goal had. **Real bug, fixed same day it shipped:** a missing `day_target_profiles` table (migration 088 not yet applied) used to degrade SILENTLY — the active row still saved fine, so "Saved ✓" showed every time, but the per-goal memory the whole feature is about never actually persisted, with zero indication why. Now throws a visible `toast.warning(...)` naming migration 088 explicitly instead of swallowing it — a genuine (non-missing-table) error still throws through the mutation's own error toast as before. The mutation's `onMutate` also now optimistically writes the just-saved numbers into the profiles cache (not just the active-row cache) so switching goals immediately after Save can never read stale pre-save data during the brief window before the background refetch lands. **Real bug, fixed same day it shipped:** with NO profile saved yet for a goal (first use, or a goal never tapped before), switching pills only changed the `goal` label — calories/protein sat frozen, reading as "picking Cut/Gain does nothing." `selectGoal()` now falls back to a sensible DIFFERENT starting point per goal when no profile exists: protein scales with bodyweight via `coach.proteinByGoal` (`useNutritionCoach` already computes this for all three goals, not just the active one — cut's higher g/kg was sitting unused), calories step off Maintain's own saved number by the standard ~500 kcal deficit / ~300 kcal surplus a cut/gain implies (clamped to `coach.calorieFloor`). Saving a goal replaces this fallback with its own real profile from then on. (2) **One goal (migration `113`):** `day_targets` holds the phase + `phase_start_date`, daily calories/protein/water and one set of body targets (`goal_weight_kg`, `goal_body_fat_pct`, `goal_muscle_mass_kg`); 111's `athlete_profile` columns are kept but no longer read. ONE editor, **Your goal** (`day-targets` popup, `DayTargetsEditor`: phase with a Since date that becomes today on a phase change, daily targets, body targets, coach; one Save), opened from `GoalSummary` — the shared "Your goal · Cut since 1 Sep · … · Edit goal" line on Food Today, Daily's Nutrition card and Health → Goal progress (Health's phase control is read-only). It is a **local draft** seeded once — pills and steppers only touch the draft, Save writes, Cancel closes without saving; Coach "Apply" inside it feeds the draft, while the Coach card's own "Apply" on FoodTodayTab writes immediately. Pre-113: reads fall back to `athlete_profile` then the device copy; `upsertDayTargets(next, loaded)` puts the daily targets on `day_targets` and writes the body goals to their old place only when they (or the phase start) changed or a device-only copy exists, else `'unchanged'` with no warning (`verify-body-goal`, 184). `day_targets` is `rw` in `DB_CATALOG`: the AI may `db_update` it after an explicit yes (setting `phase_start_date` on a phase change) or point to Your goal → Edit goal.
- **Ingredient sources** (all reviewed before save, per-100g): the user's **library** (search-first) · **barcode → Open Food Facts** (`openFoodFactsApi.lookupBarcode`, free/no-key/CORS-open → no edge function) via `BarcodeScanner` (native `BarcodeDetector` + dynamically-imported `@zxing/browser` fallback for iOS, code-split; manual entry always available) · **online name search** (`openFoodFactsApi.searchFoodsByName`, OFF v2, retries on 503 with an honest "busy" state) merged behind **Kassalapp** results (Norwegian branded groceries + price + nutrition) via the `food-search` edge function; `OnlineFoodSearch` is wired into FoodLogModal's new-ingredient (🔎) and the Ingredients manager (📷 + 🔎). (The NIH DSLD supplement lookup lost its screen with the supplement launcher and its orphaned `supplementDsldApi.ts` was deleted 2026-09-30; old `source='dsld'` library rows stay valid.) **Kassalapp caveat:** the response STRUCTURE is verified from the `kassalappy` client's Pydantic models (`Product{name,brand,image,ean,current_price,nutrition:NutritionItem[]}`, `NutritionItem{code,display_name,amount,unit}`) and `normalize()` disambiguates kcal vs kJ by unit — but the exact Norwegian `display_name`/`code` VALUES (assumed Energi/Protein/Karbohydrater/Fett/Sukker/Kostfiber) and the per-100g basis are **still unconfirmed against a live sample**; unknown labels fall back to null, never crash.
- **Atwater consistency warning (`macroSanity.ts` + `MacroWarningBadge.tsx`)** — a small orange `!` badge (tap/hover, same `Popover` pattern as the ℹ️ `InfoBubble`) appears next to any calorie/macro entry whose declared kcal doesn't match what its own protein/carbs/fat imply (`4P + 4C + 9F`), flagged when the gap exceeds the LARGER of 50 kcal or 15% of declared calories. Pure, import-free, sucrase-verified (`scripts/verify-macro-sanity.cjs`, 15 assertions) — never auto-corrects anything, since a legitimate product can genuinely deviate (fibre/sugar-alcohol calorie adjustments) and the declared kcal is sometimes the MORE trustworthy number, not the macros. Wired into every place nutrition numbers are entered or reviewed: `IngredientManager`'s add/edit form and "Your foods" list rows, `FoodLogModal`'s new-ingredient form AND its library search-result rows (catches a bad EXISTING library entry at logging time too), `OnlineFoodSearch`'s result rows (catches a bad barcode/Kassalapp/OFF result **before** it's ever saved — the earliest possible point), `RecipeModal`'s manual-macro fields and its `from_ingredients` live preview, `RecipeModal`'s inline `NewIngredientInline`, and `EditFoodLogModal`'s custom-macro fields. Real motivating case: a Kassalapp-sourced "TINE Proteinrik Lettost" declared 157.6 kcal/100g alongside 30g protein/16g fat/1.5g carbs — its own macros imply ~270 kcal, a 42% gap traced to third-party source data, invisible without doing the arithmetic by hand. Several call sites needed a `<div>`+inner-`<button>` restructure (the same fix `FoodTile`'s ★/✕ corner buttons already established) since the badge is itself a Popover button and can't nest inside another `<button>`.
- **A same-session bug sweep across the whole feature found and fixed 7 more real issues** (a dedicated read-only pass, not incidental to the Atwater feature above):
  - **`eatPlannedEntry` (`mealPlanApi.ts`) ignored the weight-unit gate** — every other reader of a planned row (`sumMacros`, `unifiedToMeal`) zeroes an ingredient's contribution when its unit isn't a weight/volume unit (a non-gram quantity like "2 piece" can't be scaled against a per-100g basis), but confirming a planned entry as eaten skipped that check entirely — tapping ✓ on a "2 piece" egg snapshotted `ingredientSnapshot(data, 2)`, i.e. **2 grams**, permanently logging near-zero macros. Now gated identically to the other readers.
  - **"Add missing to Shop" (`RecipeDetail.tsx`) ignored the servings-scale factor** — every ON-SCREEN quantity already scales by `factor = servings / recipe.servings`, but the pushed shopping-list quantities used the recipe's raw, unscaled amounts. Doubling a batch before shopping still pushed the original (half) quantities. Now scales each ingredient's quantity by the same `factor` before building the Shop payload.
  - **`FoodLogModal`'s new-ingredient form had no sugar field at all** — unlike `IngredientManager`'s equivalent form, so any food added via Log Food (including barcode scans and online search — the far more common path) permanently lost its sugar value even when the source API supplied a real one. Added the missing field/state, threaded through `prefillFromProduct`/`handleNewIngredient`.
  - **`RecipeDetail`'s "Eating N of M portions / X% of the batch" text divided by `recipe.servings`** (the base/original yield) instead of the currently-scaled `servings` state — doubling a batch before logging kept showing the un-scaled portion count and roughly doubled the reported "% of the batch". The actual logged calories were unaffected (they're computed from per-serving `recipe.calories`, always correct) — only this descriptive line was wrong.
  - **`NewIngredientInline` (inside `RecipeModal.tsx`) had no error handling** — a `try { await onCreate(...) } finally { ... }` with no `catch`, so a rejected create (RLS, network, duplicate name) left the button silently stopping its spinner with zero feedback, violating the mandatory toast-on-every-async-action rule every sibling save path in this feature already follows.
  - **`EditFoodLogModal` hardcoded `unit: 'g'`** on every save regardless of the library ingredient's own unit (some are `'ml'`) — editing such an entry silently relabelled its stored unit (the macro math itself is unit-agnostic and unaffected, but any UI/export trusting `unit` for display was now wrong). Now uses `lib.unit || 'g'`, both in the saved patch and the displayed suffix.
  - **Two food-logging save paths (`FoodLogModal`/`EditFoodLogModal` `handleSave`) had an unhandled-rejection gap** — `await mutateAsync(...)` with no surrounding try/catch; the mutation's own `useMutationWithFeedback` still toasted the error, but the rejected promise was unhandled at the call site (console noise), unlike `AssignMealModal`'s explicit catch-and-swallow precedent elsewhere in this feature. Wrapped to match.
  - **One finding deliberately left as-is, flagged rather than fixed:** day/group nutrition totals sum already-`Math.round()`-ed per-meal numbers rather than rounding a single raw sum once, which can compound a few kcal/g of rounding drift across many small entries — very likely an intentional WYSIWYG trade-off (so every visible per-item number always adds up to the visible total) rather than a defect; left for a deliberate future call rather than guessed at.
- **Supplements** log into the `supplement` slot through the normal logger (and `phone-gateway`'s `log_supplement`). There is no separate supplement launcher.
- **Recipes core:** `RecipeModal` (create/edit — title, base servings, dynamic ingredient rows, inline `NewIngredientInline` in from-ingredients mode, full macro labels Calories/Protein/Carbs/Fat/Sugar/Fiber — never P/C/F abbreviations; save replaces all ingredient rows) · `RecipeDetail` (serving stepper rescales quantities × target/base and totals macros per-serving × target) · `RecipeCard`/Library grid (`repeat(auto-fill,minmax(10rem,12rem))`, client-side search over title/description/ingredient name, category filter pills) · `MacroBar` (proportional protein/carbs/fat stacked bar, 4/4/9 kcal-per-gram) · `CookMode` (full-screen step-by-step: large text, progress dots, persistent ingredient checklist) · `times_cooked` + a 🔥 "I made this" button (organic favourites signal, no extra table) · **pantry → Shop** (`recipes/api/shopIntegration.ts`, deliberately crossing into `shop/api/shopApi.ts`): a per-ingredient "I already have this" checkbox (session state) and "Add missing to Shop" pushes unchecked ingredients into a `Groceries → Recipe Ingredients` category with quantity + recipe title in the notes.
- **AI recipe extraction (editor-only)** — `parseRecipeText(text)`, `parseRecipeFromUrl(url)` (fetchUrl → parse), `estimateRecipeMacros(ingredients, servings)` in `aiApi.ts`, on top of `ai-proxy`'s `responseSchema` + `fetchUrl` modes. The parse prompt always forces Turkish output (title/ingredients/instructions translated regardless of source language). In `RecipeModal`: "✨ Paste recipe" (Paste text / From URL toggle; URL mode also sets `source_url`) and "✨ Estimate with AI" (manual macro mode only). All are one-shot fills the user reviews — **never call these from the general Ask AI chat panel**, they're editor-only actions.
- **General Ask AI recipe tools** are separate: `get_recipes`/`create_recipe` in `ai-proxy`, for when the user pastes/describes a recipe in normal chat. The system prompt carries an explicit guardrail: **a shared recipe is never `create_shop_item`/`create_shop_category`** — this was a real bug (a pasted recipe got mis-filed as a Shop wishlist item under an AI-invented "Home & Living > Recipes" category, since no recipe tool existed for that panel yet).
- **Seed data:** migration `035_recipe_seed_turkish_classics.sql` seeds 10 classic Turkish dishes with ingredients + editorial per-serving macro estimates (`macro_mode='manual'`) for every existing user, idempotent (no Turkish recipe site has a public API and neither Spoonacular nor Edamam tags "Turkish" as a cuisine). Migration `054` seeds ~50 Norwegian staples — the full 2121-food **Matvaretabellen** import (`scripts/generate-matvaretabellen-seed.mjs`, re-run after the annual autumn update; sugar mapped to Mono+Di total, carbs to Karbo available) was **never applied to prod**; prod holds the ~50 staples + the user's own foods. **There is no migration `056`.** The AI prompt says "search the library first" without over-claiming its size.
- **Table naming standard:** every satellite table is `recipe_*` (matches `shop_*`/`hevy_*`); `recipes` itself is the feature's root entity (like `tasks`/`movies`/`projects`). Built fresh in Supabase — RecipeSage was evaluated and rejected (AGPL + a separate tRPC service needing its own server); no consumer recipe app has a usable public API.
- **Usability round (2026-09-26)** — from a bug + UX audit of the whole feature:
  - **Quick add** (`QuickAddCustom.tsx`): the logger's search offers a one-off line (title + kcal, optional protein/carbs/fat) that creates no library ingredient — real usage is mostly custom titles, and before this the only paths were "make a per-100g ingredient" or nothing. "kebab 700 kcal" fills kcal (`parseQuickAdd`; a bare "kebab 700" is only a guess the user confirms, see the 03.10.2026 round); Daily's inline slot box logs "… kcal" straight away. It leads the results when kcal was typed or nothing matched, otherwise it sits under the matches.
  - **Search** (`foodSearch.ts`, pure, `scripts/verify-food-search.cjs`): ranked exact → starts with → word start → contains, accent-insensitive (ø/æ/å/ş/ç/ı…); saved meals show alongside ingredient matches (they used to vanish as soon as one ingredient matched), past one-off lines are searchable ("Logged before · Log again"). Recents are slot-aware (`RecentFood.slotCounts`, `sortForSlot`), and a slot you fill the same way every day gets **Log usual (N)** (`usualForSlot`: ≥3 times, mostly in that slot — e.g. supplements).
  - **Undo, not confirm:** ✕ on a diary row (and on a whole "As meal" group) deletes at once and shows an Undo toast (`useRemoveFoodLogEntries`: `deleteFoodLogEntriesReturning` → `restoreFoodLogEntries` re-inserts the exact rows). `toast.undo`'s label is now "Undo" (was Turkish).
  - **Same as yesterday:** on Daily's empty state and per empty slot on Food · Today; groups stay groups (fresh shared id per source group); "nothing to copy" is a warning, not an error.
  - **Nutrition stats** card on Food · Today (`NutritionStatsCard` + pure `nutritionStats.ts`, `scripts/verify-nutrition-stats.cjs`, under Last 7 days in `FOOD_TODAY_BOARD`): the 7/28 days BEFORE the viewed day (that day is still being logged, so it's left out) — protein g/day + g/kg + days ≥ 1.6 g/kg, kcal vs target (reliable from 5/7 logged), logged vs burned on the goal report's paired days ("possible under-logging" by the coach's rule), meals ≥ 0.4 g/kg protein, fibre vs 25–30 g, fat vs 0.6 g/kg, weekend vs weekday gap (> 500 flagged); each row a tone dot, one sentence and a Measured/Evidence/Heuristic pill.
  - **Last 7 days** card on Food · Today (`WeeklyNutritionCard` + pure `weeklyNutrition.ts`, `scripts/verify-weekly-nutrition.cjs`): average kcal/protein over logged days only, protein-hit and ±10% calorie days, one bar per day against the target line. Meal Plan day headers show each day's eaten kcal.
  - Water: an **Other** amount and a behind-pace hint (today, goal spread 07:00–22:00). Filled Daily slots get **Add more**. Logger footer shows carbs/fat.
  - **Bugs fixed:** deleting a recipe/ingredient that was ever logged failed on the table CHECK (migration `107`); `phone-gateway` dated logs in UTC (00:00–02:00 Oslo landed on yesterday — now Europe/Oslo); the Meal Plan grid rendered only the first planned row per slot; `eatPlannedEntry` ignored read errors and could write a permanent 0 kcal row; rings read "0 over" when over target; decimals couldn't be typed in portions eaten / ingredient quantity (`DecimalInput`); the portion picker's grams snapped on batches over 1 kg; the Goals editor's coach followed the saved goal instead of the one being edited, and it (and Coach Apply) could save placeholder defaults over the real goals before they loaded; a Recent tile added the default portion instead of the amount it showed; a basket item could be logged at 0 g; the saved-meal ✎ threw away the basket; the logger kept a stale basket between opens; Edit stored a free-text library unit (freezing the row's totals); RecipeDetail's scaling went stale after its yield changed; rows inside a slot had no stable order.
- **Library, Foods, Insights round (03.10.2026, migration `123`).** Tabs are Today · Library · Ingredients ("Foods" on a phone) · Meal plan ("Plan") · **Insights**. Two pure modules carry the logic: `foodLibraryModel.ts` (usage from the last 90 eaten diary days via `useFoodUsage` — times eaten + last day per recipe/food; protein per 100 kcal; high-protein rules; recipe and food filters/sorts; recipe use counts; portion macros; `suggestForRemaining`) and `foodInsights.ts` (averages per logged day, macro split, top calorie/protein sources, most logged, by meal, streak, variety) — `verify-food-library` (38), `verify-food-insights` (14).
  - **Library** (`LibraryTab`, `RecipeCard`): each card shows kcal / serving, P·C·F (`MacroLine`) and the split (`MacroBar`), a High-protein pill and g protein / 100 kcal, "Last eaten DD.MM.YYYY · N×", and a **+** that logs one serving to today (supplement → supplement slot, else the slot for now). Sort (recently eaten default, most eaten, protein, protein per kcal, kcal ↑↓, most cooked, newest, A–Z) and flags (High protein, Under 500 kcal, No macros, **Saved meals (N)** — the temp meals from the logger, otherwise hidden). Search folds accents and reaches ingredient names.
  - **Recipe editor**: Delete and **Show in Library** (the `is_temp` switch) in edit mode — saved meals were undeletable before; servings are whole numbers (the column is an integer, "2.5" failed to save); linking an ingredient keeps a typed unit ("2 pcs" no longer became "2 g"); a manual Library recipe keeps its typed macros (only saved meals auto-switch to from-ingredients; a manual recipe whose ingredients can be counted shows "Calculate from ingredients"); the inline new-food form has fibre. Recipe detail says "Total for N servings · X kcal each".
  - **Units**: recipe ingredients in kg, l, dl, cl and "gr" count (`foodUnits.ts` ↔ migration 123's `food_unit_grams()` — change both). The portion picker offers grams only when every ingredient has a weight, labelled "g raw".
  - **Foods** (`IngredientList`): thumbnail, kcal / 100 g (or ml), P·C·F, protein per 100 kcal, the portion line ("1 scoop (30 g): 114 kcal · 24 g protein"), group · source (Your own / Open Food Facts / Kassalapp / NIH DSLD / Matvaretabellen) · eaten N× · in N recipes; sort + flags (High protein, Eaten lately, Not eaten lately, No macros, Macros don't add up); 120 rows at a time. The unit is a select (per 100 g / per 100 ml). Delete says how many recipes use the food; those recipes switch to typed macros and keep their totals (client + migration 123's trigger), so past days no longer drop. A category can be cleared on edit; a duplicate name is a plain message, and a scanned/searched product whose name exists reuses that food (barcode matched by `source_ref` first).
  - **Insights** (`FoodInsightsTab`, `INSIGHT_BOARD`): 7 / 30 / 90 days — your average day vs targets (kcal, protein, fibre, streak, variety), where calories come from, top calorie and protein sources and most logged (recipes open), by meal.
  - **Today → Fits what's left** (`FitsLeftCard`, in the totals rail): your own recipes and familiar foods (eaten lately or with a portion) whose one portion fits the calories left, ranked by protein per kcal; hidden under 10 g protein or 80 kcal left; + logs it.
  - **Bugs fixed**: quick add read any trailing number as kcal ("Chicken 150" logged 150 kcal) — only "kcal"/"cal" is calories now, "150g" is grams, a bare number opens the logger; Daily's slot box auto-logs only an exact (accent-folded) name; the logger searches on the name without the amount; recipe ingredient saves are one transaction (`replace_recipe_ingredients`, a failed insert no longer empties a recipe); a planned food with no amount used to confirm as 0 kcal (now its portion; "2 pcs" uses the portion preset); "Last 7 days" now leaves out today and 0 kcal days like Nutrition stats; the diary editor waits for the food/recipe before choosing its form; logger rows keep ml for drinks.
- **Known low-value:** `food_log_entries.notes` is effectively write-only. Water is **not** in `ai-proxy`'s `DB_CATALOG`, so the AI cannot currently read or write water logs (the phone gateway can).

### Training Feature Detail

**Structure (2026-09-27 overhaul).** `TrainingPage` has six tabs kept in `?tab=` (`pages/trainingTabs.ts`): **Next** (default — the next planned session from `useNextTrainingSession`, or the current program's least-recently-trained routine; every exercise with the progress engine's per-set target incl. backoff weights, last session and GIF, a recovery line from Health (last night's sleep, resting HR vs your own baseline — no score) and engine alerts; the warm-up ramp and the routine's exercise notes were removed on the owner's call — notes stay in Library → Routines, collapsed by default) · **Program** (current-program picker — pre-ticks routines actually trained in the last 28 days via `suggestCurrentProgramRoutineIds` —, weekly schedule from recurring training blocks, each routine's prescription (stacked under the schedule in main, 42rem; per-muscle and balance in the side tracks), an `exercise_target_overrides` editor, planned weekly sets per muscle graded against Pelland 2025 and the 10–20 sets range with MEV–MAV labelled as heuristic, push:pull / hamstring:quad balance; limitations and muscle preferences are applied) · **Progress** (4/12/26-week improvement card, the decision table, the weekly-volume body map open by default, a days-since-trained list per muscle, the older charts collapsed) · **Log** (`?view=hevy|strava`, switched at the right end of the page tab row; default Hevy: a month calendar — `calendar/TrainingMonthCalendar`, sticky left from `lg` — beside the workout cards; Strava: its activities. Hevy body measurements live on Health → Body, and an old `?tab=log&view=body` link redirects there) · **Library** (`?view=routines|exercises`, same place: routines, exercises + GIF fixer) · **Coach** (PT coach + profile with the Training focus, limitations, preferences). The progress engine runs ONCE per page (`TrainingProgressProvider` → `progressModel.ts`, a pure function; `useProgressData` is a thin wrapper). Pure plan logic lives in `src/features/training/plan/` (`verify-training-plan.cjs`, 104 assertions); every science citation with its URL is in `plan/sources.ts`, and the fact-checked research behind it is kept in `docs/training-health/research/` (a `verify-*.json` correction wins over its `research-*.json`). **One source per concept:** bodyweight = `useBodyweightSeries` (Health; Hevy > smart scale > Apple), next session = `useNextTrainingSession`/`pickNextTrainingSession` (Training, Daily, Home), sessions per week = `sessionsThisWeek`, est. 1RM = `est1RM` (`progressAggregate`), and ONE AI coach context (`training/coach/`: `coachData` → `coachFormat`, which holds the Turkish snapshot, the Coach JSON and `PT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` together) fed by the same engine run, so the coach cannot contradict Progress. Free-text limitations resolve through `muscleMap.resolveMovementPattern`; a 'monitor' limitation no longer paints a muscle. `WorkedMuscles`/`TrainingCalendar` are split into `components/muscles/` and `components/calendar/`. **Round 3 (2026-09-27):** **RPE** (`hevy_sets.rpe`, synced all along) shows wherever a logged set does — one formatter, `setFormat.ts::rpeSuffix` (" @ RPE 8/9/10"); the Next card adds an informational "near your limit" (avg ≥ 9.5) / "room to push" (≤ 7) note that never changes the target, and the coach snapshot carries it. **Missed sessions** (migration `112`, `training_skips`; `plan/skippedRoutines.ts`, `verify-skipped-routines`): a current-program routine not done for more than 7 days with no one-off plan ahead (recurring templates deliberately don't count) shows a warning on Program and Next — Plan it, or Skip with a required reason (covers the week it was due; undoable). **Session popup:** every session opens ONE popup, `training-session` (`{ workoutId?, plan?: { kind: 'block'|'recurring', id, date } }`, `components/session/TrainingSessionView`) — from the Log calendar rows and workout cards, Next's ⋯, Daily's Training card and agenda training blocks, Home's Next up/Later rows (`NextUpItem.category`/`planDate`), the Home Training tile and widget, and Health → Workouts. It is compact: a workout shows one stats row (duration · working sets · volume · avg HR from the matched Apple Watch workout, or the exercise count without one), an RPE line when a set was rated ("Avg RPE · N of M working sets rated"), one collapsed line per exercise (with `rpeSuffix`) that expands to its set table, and a collapsed "Heart rate & energy" section; a planned session shows the progress engine's targets, the same rows as Next (`useRoutineSessionPlan`, its own `TrainingProgressProvider`); a plan a workout covered opens as that workout ("Planned for 18:00" / "In place of ⟳ Upper B" — `sessionRef.resolveTrainingSession`, pairing via `calendarSessions.matchDaySessions`; the Log calendar rows use the same words via `sessionRef.coveredPlanNote`); changing or deleting a plan is behind the popup's ⋯ (`SessionPlanMenu`), which opens `time-block`/`schedule-block` by id. `training-session` is the only session popup kind (the `hevy-workout` alias was deleted). `training/sessionLinks.ts` records which workout a popup shows, keyed by the request object, so Health's `isTrainingSessionRequest` treats a popup opened from a covered plan as "back" from the Apple workout popup. Verified by `verify-training-log` (122). **Push:pull / quad:ham** come from ONE module, `plan/muscleBalance.ts` (push = chest+shoulders+triceps, pull = back+traps+biceps, shown "a : 1", heavy above 1.5×), used by the Program card (planned), the Muscles card (done, last 30 days — each shows the other's number and a "why they differ" line) and the coach; `muscleMap.templateMuscleCredit` credits each muscle once per set (Hevy's lats + upper_back used to double-count Back, which is why "balanced" showed against a planned 1.77). **Personal records are gone from every screen (owner's call, 2026-09-27):** the Library's Records tab (`HevyPRList`, `BestLiftsCard`, `fetchHevyPRs`, `personalRecords.ts`), the Improvement card's record timeline and the decision table's "6-month best" chips. The engine still emits its record events (they count as evidence for the program verdict); `progress-engine/copy.ts::visibleEvents` keeps them off screen. The notes below describe how the individual pieces were built; where they mention the old Hevy/Strava/Health/Coach tabs or records, read them against this structure.

Page layout: `PageContainer` + the tab row; each tab is a PageBoard (`trainingBoards.ts`): Next (session + context rail left, exercise cards `minmax(22rem,1fr)` across the rest) · Program (main 42rem; current program/schedule | planned sets | balance) · Progress (decisions + body map in main, improvement/verdict/recency rail, charts below in 1–3 columns) · Log (sticky calendar rail left) · Coach (main 42rem; coach | profile | history). The decision table switches table ↔ stacked cards by its own width (44rem). Status colours are tone maps next to their enums (`setTypeMeta.ts`, `progressMetricMeta.ts`, muscle bands, calendar dots); charts use `useChartColors`; no training component imports supabase (`hooks/useHevySync.ts`, `useMuscleVolume.ts`, `usePtCoach.ts`, `useExerciseImageDb.ts`, `useConnectStrava` in `useTrainingSessions.ts`). `HevyTab.tsx` now only exports the Library tab (Routines · Exercises).

- **AI PT Coach** (`PTCoachTab.tsx` + `api/ptCoachApi.ts`): user-initiated (NEVER auto-runs) daily assessment acting as a decisive personal trainer. **Token-minimal BY CONSTRUCTION** — `buildTrainingSnapshot()` pre-aggregates everything client-side (latest workout's per-exercise set summaries with previous-same-exercise comparison from up to 10 sessions back, weekly hard-sets-per-muscle vs `MUSCLE_LANDMARKS`, last-night + 7d-avg sleep, steps/active energy, 30d weight trend) into ONE compact text block → a single one-shot `invokeAI` call, zero tool loop (same pattern as the daily briefing). The coaching brain (recovery gate on <6h sleep, MRV deload cues, double-progression +2.5/+5kg engine, volume steering vs MEV) was distilled by the `strength-coach` agent and bounded by the `sports-scientist` agent's evidence guardrails — **the snapshot line formats are a CONTRACT with that prompt; change them together.** Input: feeling chips + optional free text. Every assessment is saved to `pt_assessments` (migration `051`: date, feeling, note, the exact snapshot the coach saw, the reply, which model served it); the next run feeds the previous one back in and the prompt's FOLLOW-UP rule makes the coach open with whether its last advice was applied. History is browsable in the tab; a localStorage fallback still displays results if the migration isn't applied.
- **Coach mode in the main chat** (`AIPanel`'s Coach toggle → `sendCoachMessage`/`buildCoachContext`): a SEPARATE surface from the daily assessment — open conversation over a prepared last-30-days compact JSON (workouts w/ sets, current routines incl. ids, sleep, steps, active kcal, weight/body-fat, the diary's logged nutrition, the coach's own past assessments, the athlete profile + active limitations below; ~7k chars measured live), blunt science-over-feelings persona, and REAL program edits via `update_hevy_routine` plus new-routine creation via `create_hevy_routine`. Both forward to `hevy-api` so every documented Hevy payload trap stays handled in one place and **Hevy stays the source of truth** (the local mirror is upserted by hevy-api, never written directly); both confirm first, and create requires resolving real `exercise_template_id`s via `db_query` on `hevy_exercise_templates`. The general Ask AI chat also carries a TRAINING QUESTIONS persona block (client-side in `aiApi.ts`'s `SYSTEM_PROMPT`, no edge deploy needed), which now tells the model to check the `get_athlete_profile` tool before giving programming advice.
- **Athlete profile** (migration `070`: `athlete_profile` + `athlete_limitations`) — a second strength-coach/sports-scientist design pairing, this time over durable facts about the person rather than volume math. **Two tables, not one `text[]` column**, because the repo's real convention for a one-to-many attribute list is a child table (`recipe_ingredients`, `hevy_exercise_template_muscles`), and a limitation needs its own identity/`created_at`/CHECK that an array element can't carry. `athlete_profile` is a true **singleton** — `user_id` is the PRIMARY KEY (no `id` column) — holding `goal` = the **Training focus** (strength/hypertrophy/general — a stored `fat_loss` reads as general via `trainingFocusOf` and migration 113 rewrites it, since losing fat is the Cut phase on `day_targets`; read-only metadata for the coach's tone that never forks the volume algorithm; the coach snapshot/JSON carry `Odak`/`focus` plus the phase `Faz`/`phase`, and `get_athlete_profile` maps it too and omits the superseded goal columns), `experience_level` (novice/intermediate/advanced), `training_age_years`, `training_days_per_week`, `equipment_access`, `notes`. `athlete_limitations` is a normal list: `movement_pattern` + `severity` (`avoid`/`limit`/`monitor`, **defaults to `monitor`** — escalating to avoid/limit is always an explicit, separate update, never automatic on log) + `note` + `active`.
  - **`movement_pattern`, never a muscle slug.** A physical limitation is almost always movement-shaped, not muscle-shaped — a shoulder issue rules out overhead pressing, but flat press/flyes are frequently still safe despite training the same chest muscle; flagging at the muscle level would suppress a still-safe stimulus. `muscleMap.ts`'s `MovementPattern` (9 values: squat/hinge/horizontal_press/vertical_press/horizontal_pull/vertical_pull/lunge/carry/isolation — a standard S&C taxonomy, not exotic) is free text on the DB side (no CHECK enum — a fixed value list would force an `ai-proxy` redeploy for every new phrasing a real conversation surfaces, the same reasoning `069`'s rejected season-CHECK already established).
  - **`PATTERN_AFFECTED_SLUGS`** (`muscleMap.ts`) is the derivation seam: which `Slug`s a pattern restriction realistically touches, and how hard (`'avoid'` = no comparable substitute at heavy load; `'limit'` = other patterns/isolation work still train it safely). This is what lets `WorkedMuscles.tsx` — a pipeline keyed entirely by muscle `Slug`, with zero pattern dimension of its own — render a per-muscle flag without the DB ever storing a muscle slug directly. A flagged muscle is a **third visual state** (violet, `FLAG_META`), deliberately never green or red: most flagged muscles are still safely trainable through a non-conflicting exercise, so a danger colour would overstate the restriction. Shown even at 0 logged sets (the flag is the point, not the volume).
  - **Experience-scaled landmarks** (`scaleLandmarksForExperience`, `EXPERIENCE_MULTIPLIER`): novice ×0.85 / intermediate ×1 / advanced ×1.15, applied to **MEV and MRV only** — MV and MAV are deliberately left unscaled, a cross-examined agreement (RP framework: training age shifts MEV/MRV; scaling the WHOLE landmark set would overclaim precision on a table that's already a heuristic, not a measurement). Every `InfoBubble` showing an adjusted landmark says so explicitly: *"Adjusted ±15% for your experience level — an unvalidated adjustment on top of an already-heuristic baseline, not a measured number."*
  - **`ptCoachApi.ts`'s snapshot** gets one new block — a `PROFİL:` line (goal/experience/equipment/days, only the fields actually set) + one `Kısıtlama:` line per active limitation — inserted before the workout lines, respecting the file's snapshot-line-format contract; `PT_SYSTEM_PROMPT`'s rule 1 (safety) was extended to ground `avoid`/`limit`/`monitor` into concrete behaviour. Absent entirely when the user has never touched the profile — zero snapshot-line change for anyone who doesn't use this feature.
  - **`ai-proxy`**: `athlete_profile`/`athlete_limitations` are `rw` `DB_CATALOG` entries (per the user's explicit instruction to give the AI authority here) plus a new single-shot tool, `get_athlete_profile` — returns both tables in ONE call (mirroring `get_day_summary`'s shape) instead of two separate `db_query` round trips, because token efficiency was an explicit requirement. `athlete_profile` being a singleton keyed by `user_id` (no `id` column) means `db_update` needs a non-empty `filters` object even though the function already scopes to `user_id` internally — the catalog's `rules` text spells out the exact `filters={"user_id":...}` shape to use. Not added to `EMBED_SOURCES` (semantic search) — this is structured settings data consulted deterministically, not free text worth fuzzy-recalling.
  - **Settings UI**: `PTCoachTab.tsx` gained a slim summary strip (`🎯 hypertrophy · intermediate · gym · 2 limitations` + a ⚙ button) opening `AthleteProfileSheet.tsx` (pills/stepper autosave on tap, `notes` autosaves on blur — matching Media's `personal_note` convention, no Save/Cancel footer) and `LimitationsList.tsx` (every limitation stays visible even when inactive — muted, one tap to reactivate; deleting is a separate confirmed action, never implied by "inactive"). Pre-migration-safe throughout: every new read degrades to `null`/`[]` on a missing table, every write throws a named "migration 070 not applied" error rather than failing silently.
- **Progress sub-tab** (`ProgressTab.tsx` + `progressAggregate.ts`) — three charts answering "how am I actually changing over time", added from an explicit `strength-coach` (what metric per exercise type) + `sports-scientist` (which other charts are evidence-worthy) agent consultation (2026-08-28), the same pairing behind Muscles/athlete-profile. **Data:** `hevyApi.fetchTrainingHistory(fromISO,toISO)` fetches the last 182 days of `hevy_workouts` → `hevy_workout_exercises` → PAGINATED `hevy_sets` (1000-row chunks) → `hevy_exercise_templates`, joined client-side (the `fetchMuscleVolume` precedent), via `useTrainingHistory()` (10-min `staleTime`). All three charts share this one fetch.
  - **Exercise Progress** (`ExerciseProgressChart.tsx`) — the user picks ONE exercise (mandatory `Combobox`/`ComboboxInput`/`ComboboxOptions`; **`immediate` goes on the root `<Combobox>`, not `<ComboboxOptions>`** — a real bug this session: `immediate` on the wrong element type-errored AND left the picker showing zero options on focus) and sees its own progression across every session it appears in. **The metric plotted is NOT one universal formula** — `metricKindForExerciseType(type)` dispatches on the exercise's own Hevy `CustomExerciseType`: `weight_reps`/`short_distance_weight` → **Est. 1RM** (Epley, the one `est1RM()` in `progressAggregate.ts` — never a second formula for the same number), `bodyweight_reps`/`reps_only` → top-set reps, `bodyweight_weighted` → added weight, `bodyweight_assisted` → **assistance, inverted** (less assistance over time = the improvement, so the line trending down is the win), `duration`/`weight_duration` → top-set duration, `distance_duration`/`floors_duration`/`steps_duration` → top-set distance; an unrecognized/future type falls back to `est1rm` rather than crashing. `computeExerciseProgression` groups sets by `date|workout_id`, excludes `type==='warmup'` entirely, and picks the SINGLE BEST set per session (never an average) — a `dropset` can never win (lighter by construction), a `failure` set legitimately can (a real top effort). An `est1rm`/`addedWeight` exercise gets a toggle to **Session Volume** (Σ weight×reps that session, warm-ups excluded) — never offered for a rep/duration/distance exercise, which has no weight-based "volume" to switch to. Guardrail copy (always visible, never buried in an ℹ️, matching the Muscles feature's tone) covers: the Epley ±10%-at-low-reps caveat and its 12-rep cutoff (`est1RM` returns `null` above 12 reps — those sets are excluded from the estimate), volume-vs-strength, added-weight understating true load, the assisted-exercise inversion, a rep-range-swing warning (`repRangeVariedSignificantly`, triggers at a ≥4-rep swing across the period), "read the trend over sessions, not day to day," and an explicit disambiguation from the Muscles body map (a muscle's total weekly dose) — this chart is not that.
  - **Weekly Training Volume** (`WeeklyVolumeChart.tsx`) — the sports-scientist agent's #1-priority chart: total weekly tonnage (Σ weight×reps, warm-ups excluded, weight-based exercise types only via `computeWeeklyVolumeTrend`) as a **trend**, not the Muscles feature's current-window snapshot, with a 4-week rolling average line (`rollingAverage`) overlaid. Uses its own `ComposedChart` (bar + line, two independent series) rather than the shared `BarLineChart` (which only supports one dataKey driving both a bar and a line). Guardrail: tonnage is a training INPUT, not a stimulus or outcome — it conflates load and reps freely (100kg×5 and 50kg×10 tally identically) and shifts with exercise-mix changes; read it over months, not weeks.
  - **Training Consistency** (`TrainingConsistencyCalendar.tsx`) — a diverging-colour week-cell calendar (`computeConsistencyByWeek`) plus a current-streak readout (`currentStreakWeeks`), grounded in the sports-scientist finding that frequency is largely a volume-equating variable, not an independent driver — the guardrail copy says so explicitly, framing this as an adherence view rather than a second "more is better" signal. **`computeConsistencyByWeek` builds a DENSE week series** (every Monday from the earliest to the latest logged week, explicit `sessionCount: 0` for an untrained gap week) — a real bug caught in test-writing: the original sparse version only returned weeks with data, which silently glued two non-consecutive trained weeks into one fake unbroken streak.
  - **Follow-up review (2026-08-31)** implemented the three originally-deferred items plus each agent's own top "extra" recommendation, reconciling two independent agent passes (a sports-scientist review superseded the strength-coach's initial bucket/threshold proposals where the two disagreed — see each file's own header comment for the reasoning):
    - **`RelativeStrengthChart.tsx`** — bodyweight-normalized strength trend, **`est1rm`-type exercises only** (a ratio built from reps/added-weight/assistance/duration/distance has no interpretable meaning normalized by bodyweight — the strength-coach's separate "total load" formula for `bodyweight_weighted` lifts was considered and dropped, since two different ratio formulas on one chart is exactly the kind of silent misread this feature exists to prevent). `resolveBodyweightForDate` (`progressAggregate.ts`) is a 3-rung ladder — exact same-day match → linear interpolation between two anchors ≤21 days apart → nearest single anchor within ±14 days → otherwise **no point at all** — deliberately never an indefinite carry-forward, which would flatten the denominator across a cut/bulk and make the ratio lie about which side (strength or bodyweight) actually moved. `computeRelativeStrengthTrend` combines this with `computeExerciseProgression(sets, id, 'est1rm')`'s own session points — reusing that function rather than a parallel one. `hevyApi.fetchBodyweightHistory` unions `hevy_body_measurements.weight_kg` (the user's own manual log) with `health_metrics.weight_body_mass` (an automated scale synced through Apple Health), same-day conflicts resolved manual-wins. The chart always plots BOTH lines (ratio + a muted dashed bodyweight line) — never just the ratio — specifically so a rising number during a cut is visibly attributable to the denominator, not silently read as new strength. Estimated (non-same-day) points render as a hollow dot. **No strength-standard reference lines** (1×/1.5×/2×BW-style bands) — what counts as strong varies by height, limb length, age and sex, none of which this app knows.
    - **`RepRangeDistributionChart.tsx`** — a set-count histogram by rep bucket, **boundaries `1–5 / 6–12 / 13–20 / 21–30 / 31+`** (`REP_BUCKETS`, `computeRepRangeDistribution`) — the sports-scientist review's call, not the strength-coach's originally-proposed `6-8/9-12` split: Schoenfeld et al. 2017 (JSCR 31(12):3508-3523) and Morton et al. 2016 (J Appl Physiol 121(1):129-138) found hypertrophy roughly equivalent from ~5 to ~30 reps taken near failure, so there's no evidence for a boundary at rep 8, and the chart deliberately does NOT frame any bucket as "the hypertrophy range." Warmups excluded, dropsets/failure sets counted (each drop its own set, matching `computeExerciseProgression`'s own eligibility). Period toggle 30/90/182 days (default 90) + an optional muscle-group filter (primary-attribution only — deliberately NOT the Muscles tab's fractional secondary credit, since a histogram counts whole sets and crediting half a set to a second bar would double-count it). No target-distribution overlay.
    - **`WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart.tsx`** — the sports-scientist review's top-priority "what else": the SAME currency the Muscles tab already uses (hard sets/muscle/week vs `MUSCLE_LANDMARKS`' MEV-MAV) — the one training measure in this app with an actual dose-response meta-analysis behind it (Schoenfeld 2017; Pelland 2025) — but as a per-week **trend** rather than a single rolling-window snapshot. `computeWeeklySetsPerMuscleTrend` reuses `muscleMap.ts`'s `contribution()`/`HEVY_TO_SLUG` exactly (primary 1.0, secondary 0.5), no parallel volume model; `fetchTrainingHistory` was extended to also join `hevy_exercise_template_muscles` (the same query `fetchHevyExerciseTemplates` already does) so `TrainingExerciseTemplate` carries `secondary_muscle_groups` for free. A muscle chip row (reusing `MAJOR_MUSCLES`/`labelForSlug`) picks one muscle at a time; the chart shades the MEV–MAV band and an MRV reference line, scaled by `scaleLandmarksForExperience` when an athlete profile exists.
    - **`WeeklyChangesPanel.tsx`** ("Big Changes This Week") — ships **instead of** an acute:chronic workload ratio (ACWR), which the strength-coach review explicitly advised against: ACWR's injury-prediction evidence is team-sport running/GPS load, it has drawn sustained statistical criticism (mathematical coupling between the acute and chronic windows it computes from, unstable "sweet spot" thresholds), and this app's OWN Weekly Volume guardrail already documents tonnage as conflating load and reps — a risk flag can't be built on a quantity already flagged as ambiguous, and a solo lifter's log has none of the sample size the original research relied on. `computeWeeklyChangeFlags` is a plain per-exercise change detector: a top-set-load jump ≥10% or a set-count jump ≥30% vs the **median** of up to the 4 preceding weeks (median, not mean, so one deload week can't manufacture a flag on the return; requires ≥3 prior weeks of history), plus an unconditional `'new'` flag for any exercise not trained in the last 8 weeks (or ever) — unfamiliar-movement soreness is real and otherwise invisible in this data. No score, no colour-coded "risk," no threshold band.
    - **`RecoveryLoadPanel.tsx`** ("Recovery Inputs Alongside Load") — sleep and resting-heart-rate trends alongside weekly tonnage, as **three stacked lanes sharing one weekly X axis**, deliberately NOT a dual-axis overlay (which lets two arbitrary scales be tuned until any two series look coupled — a causality claim made with a scale factor instead of words) and NOT a computed composite "readiness" score (this app's own house rule forbids synthetic derived metrics — see Health's "no derived sleep metrics" precedent). `recoveryAggregate.ts` (new, pure, import-free of runtime deps) computes weekly sleep (mean nightly hours from `computeSleepSummary`) and weekly resting HR (median from `computeDailySeries('resting_heart_rate', ...)`), both keyed to the SAME `mondayOf` weeks `progressAggregate.ts`'s tonnage trend uses — one definition of "a week" for the whole panel — and both gated at **≥4 tracked nights/days per week**, else null (a broken line is more honest than an imputed one). **An HRV lane was deliberately left out**: different HRV measures (e.g. SDNN vs RMSSD) must never share a line or an average, and this app's own rule is to verify an external field's exact shape live before shipping copy that names it — add it once that's confirmed against a real payload.
    - **Deliberately still not built**: a "Stalled & Progressing" exercise roll-up table (the strength-coach's other "extra" suggestion — real value, but a genuinely new UI surface, an OLS-slope-over-time helper plus a sortable table, rather than an extension of what's here; tracked as a fast-follow, not silently dropped).
    - **Verified:** `scripts/verify-training-progress.cjs` — 63 assertions against the real un-mocked `progressAggregate.ts`/`recoveryAggregate.ts` (Epley cutoff, per-type dispatch, warmup exclusion, best-set-not-average selection incl. the dropset/failure-set distinction, the assisted-exercise inversion, the dense-week-series fix, streak termination at a real gap, the bodyweight-resolution ladder's every branch, rep-bucket boundaries and filtering, weekly-change-flag detection incl. the new-exercise/insufficient-history/no-change cases, primary+secondary muscle credit, and the sleep/RHR minimum-tracked-days gate).
  - **Exercise pickers sort by recency, not alphabetically** (`ExerciseProgressChart.tsx`/`RelativeStrengthChart.tsx`, 2026-09-01) — both compute a `lastUsed` date per exercise from the fetched sets and sort descending, so the lift you just did is at the top instead of buried among 40+ alphabetical names.
  - **`TrainingInsightsPanel.tsx` ("Training Analysis")** — a third sports-scientist agent review (2026-09-01), this time answering the user's explicit ask to actually READ what they're doing well/poorly, not just look at more charts. Rendered FIRST in `ProgressTab`, above every chart it summarizes. **Deliberately a fixed rules engine (`trainingInsights.ts`), never an AI call** — the agent's own explicit call: PT Coach is a dated, one-shot, snapshot-archived opinion where non-reproducibility is bounded and auditable, but this is a *standing* view the user re-opens repeatedly and reads as the app's canonical verdict on their log — two loads of the same data producing two different verdicts would be the app contradicting itself about a fact, not a tone difference, and an LLM reliably launders a heuristic threshold ("MEV") into a paraphrased "scientific minimum" over enough renders. The same log always produces the same text.
    - **Every `Finding`** (`{id, tier, positive, text}`) carries an exact number traceable to the user's own log and one of three tiers, rendered as a coloured pill so a heuristic can never read as a measurement: **Measured** (arithmetic on the log, no interpretation) · **Evidence-based** (an interpretation backed by cited meta-analytic work, inline citation in the sentence) · **Heuristic** (a practitioner convention with no trial support for the exact number — every MEV/MAV/MRV-based finding is this tier). `sortFindings` renders measured → evidence-based → heuristic. **NEVER_HIDES applies to absence of data too**: a rule that can't fire due to insufficient history renders an explicit "can't assess this yet, because X" line (the rep-range-varied skip in `computeExerciseTrendFindings`, the relative-strength null case) rather than the section silently vanishing.
    - **Gate:** needs **≥6 complete weeks** (`lastCompleteWeek(anchorDate)` — the current, still-in-progress week is excluded from every trend rule everywhere in this file, since a Wednesday always looks like a collapse) or the panel renders only an "not enough logged history yet" notice.
    - **Rule categories, each reusing an EXISTING aggregation function rather than a parallel one** (per an explicit user instruction to maximize shared structure): **A. Consistency** (`computeConsistencyByWeek` → strong-adherence / untrained-gap-weeks / frequency-collapse) · **B. Volume trend** (`computeWeeklyVolumeTrend` → up/down/flat vs. a 4-week rolling average taken 8 weeks earlier, calendar-anchored via `shiftWeek` rather than array-position lookback so a gap week can't misalign the comparison) · **C. Weekly sets per muscle vs. landmarks** (`computeWeeklySetsPerMuscleTrend` + `MUSCLE_LANDMARKS`/`scaleLandmarksForExperience` → zero-sets / persistently-under-MEV / persistently-over-MRV, the last one explicit that "over MRV" is not asserted harmful — it depends on unmeasured recovery) · **D. Rep-range distribution** (`computeRepRangeDistribution` over the trailing 90 days → a single-bucket concentration ≥70%, or zero 1–5-rep sets across ≥100 total — the clearest actionable strength-vs-hypertrophy gap this page can state) · **E. Relative strength** (`computeRelativeStrengthTrend` → a real strength gain vs. **the ratio moving because bodyweight moved, not strength** — the one rule this whole chart exists to make legible — vs. a genuine regression; a single global "can't compute, too few weigh-ins" notice when `bodyweightAnchorCount < 3`, skipping every per-exercise E rule rather than guessing) · **F. Per-exercise trend** (`computeExerciseProgression` → progressing/stalled/regressing via a first-3-vs-last-3-session mean comparison).
    - **One documented simplification from the agent's full spec**: "stalled vs progressing" (F) uses the first-3-vs-last-3-session mean rather than a least-squares slope over session index — simpler, directly testable, and the agent's own spec already leaned on the identical mean-comparison convention for the bodyweight-strength rules (E). A true OLS-slope "Stalled & Progressing" roll-up table remains the same tracked fast-follow noted above.
    - **`buildTemplateMuscleMap`** (`muscleMap.ts`, new) — the primary/secondary-slug mapping `WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart.tsx` and this panel both need was previously duplicated inline in the chart component; extracted to one shared function both now call, so there's one mapping to get right instead of two near-identical loops.
    - **Verified:** `scripts/verify-training-insights.cjs` — 31 assertions against the real un-mocked `trainingInsights.ts` (the partial-week exclusion, every A–F trigger condition incl. the bodyweight-attribution rule and the rep-range-varied skip, the tier sort order, and the limitations cross-check below).
  - **A second, follow-up sports-scientist review (2026-09-01)** — asked explicitly "is the suite sufficient, or is there more real value" and "the charts look confusing, what's the ideal simple form" — answered plainly: **the evidence-backed ground is covered; stop adding charts.** The one remaining well-evidenced variable (proximity-to-failure/RIR) is a *logging* gap, not a chart gap — **verified live against production data that `hevy_sets.rpe` exists as a column but is 100% null for this user** (checked via a throwaway script against the real Supabase REST API, per the user's explicit one-off opt-in to live data this session — no rule was built on a field with zero real values; revisit once RPE is actually logged). VO2 max, circumference measurements and session-duration/density were each explicitly rejected (device-estimated, tape-measurement noise on par with months of real growth, and confounded by supersets/rest with no per-set timestamps to fix it). The review found **one correctness defect it called the suite's most serious**, since fixed:
    - **`athlete_limitations` cross-check (real, LIVE-CONFIRMED bug, fixed).** Before this fix, a muscle-under-MEV finding could tell a user to add volume to a muscle they'd deliberately restricted — verified against this user's own live `athlete_limitations` rows (an active `heavy_hip_hinge`/`limit` restriction exists right now). Fixed via `muscleMap.ts`'s new `limitedSlugsFromLimitations(limitations)` — the SAME `PATTERN_AFFECTED_SLUGS[movement_pattern] ?? []` lookup `WorkedMuscles.tsx` already uses for its own flagged-muscle state, one mapping not two, worst-case-wins when two limitations disagree on one muscle (`avoid` never downgraded to `limit`). `'monitor'`-severity limitations are excluded — that severity means "watch it", not "restricted". `computeMuscleFindings`'s zero-sets and under-MEV findings now take an optional `restriction` and REFRAME rather than suppress (`NEVER_HIDES`: a deliberately low number is still a real number) — e.g. *"…At this dose you're most likely maintaining rather than building. You have an active limit restriction that reaches this muscle — this may be exactly why, and if so there's nothing to act on here."* **Caveat carried forward, not silently patched:** `movement_pattern` is free text on the DB side (069's own rejected-CHECK precedent), so this cross-check inherits the SAME exact-string-match limitation `WorkedMuscles.tsx` already has — verified live that this user's own two limitations (`unilateral_balance_left`, `heavy_hip_hinge`) do NOT match any of the nine `MovementPattern` enum keys and so currently produce no cross-check output for them either; normalizing free-text movement-pattern phrasing to the enum is a separate, deliberate follow-up, not something this fix quietly assumes away.
    - **Chart legibility fixes** (three real bugs the review caught in the code, not just a design opinion):
      - **`computeWeeklyVolumeTrend` was a SPARSE series** (only weeks with tonnage) — unlike `computeConsistencyByWeek`, which was deliberately made dense for exactly this reason. Consequence: `rollingAverage` silently averaged the last 4 ARRAY ENTRIES rather than the last 4 CALENDAR weeks, so a "4-week average" could quietly span 7+ real weeks after a break, and the chart's x-axis spacing didn't correspond to elapsed time. Fixed with the same dense-fill technique (every week from first to last tonnage week, explicit zero for an untrained one) — this is also what lets `trainingInsights.ts`'s calendar-anchored `computeVolumeFindings` and `WeeklyVolumeChart` agree on the exact same number for the same week. `isoWeekKey` (now redundant with `mondayOf`) was removed.
      - **The current, in-progress week was plotted as if it were complete** in `WeeklyVolumeChart` and `WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart` — `trainingInsights.ts` already excludes it via `lastCompleteWeek()`, these two charts didn't. Worse in `WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart`: its headline badge (the single most prominent number on the card) read off `chartData[chartData.length - 1]`, i.e. the partial week — understating real weekly volume until the week was actually over. Both now filter to `weekStart <= lastCompleteWeek(today)` before computing chart data (and therefore the badge).
      - **`RecoveryLoadPanel`'s sleep/resting-HR lanes used a pure `['auto','auto']` Y-domain** — auto-scaling a 7.1→6.9h week into a visual cliff is the exact "two arbitrary scales tuned until series look coupled" hazard the panel's own header comment rejects for a dual axis, just committed by accident on a single axis. Fixed with `domainWithMinSpan` (sleep ≥2h, resting HR ≥10bpm) so ordinary week-to-week noise reads as noise; the zero-based tonnage bar (see below) is untouched.
      - **Two non-zero-baseline Bar bugs, visually confirmed via a live browser screenshot pass**: `ExerciseProgressChart` (via the shared `BarLineChart`, whose `['auto','auto']` Y-domain is correct for Health-tab charts that intentionally zoom into a narrow range, but made a trend chart's early bars look almost invisible while later ones looked disproportionately tall) and `RecoveryLoadPanel`'s tonnage bar (no domain at all, defaulting to a non-zero auto minimum). Fixed by adding an opt-in `yDomain` prop to `BarLineChart` (default unchanged — every Health-tab call site is untouched) and passing `[0,'auto']` from `ExerciseProgressChart` (except for the inverted assisted-exercise metric, whose values are deliberately negative), plus an explicit `domain={[0,'auto']}` on `RecoveryLoadPanel`'s tonnage `YAxis`.
      - **`RepRangeDistributionChart` legibility pass**: added on-bar percentage `LabelList`s (so the finding's own "81% sat in 6–12 reps" sentence is visible on the chart itself, not just in prose), `maxBarSize` (5 categories no longer stretch into blocky rectangles), and `compactAxisTick` on the Y-axis for consistency with the rest of the tab.
    - **Deliberately NOT built in this same pass**: the full visual redesign the review recommended — deferred to a research pass first (below), then shipped in a follow-up round.
  - **A visual-redesign round (2026-09-01)**, implementing the prior review's five presentation recommendations after a research agent confirmed each has real precedent in shipped products (Hevy's own Statistics tab overlays multiple muscle groups on one graph; Garmin's Training Load Focus renders per-category mini-panels side by side; MacroFactor's dashboard uses 100%-stacked horizontal bars for proportion-based data; Strava's Relative Effort uses a plain weekly bar against a target/suggested-range band, not a heatmap; WHOOP's Weekly Performance Assessment buckets a week into "met goals" vs "opportunities to improve"):
    - **`WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart.tsx` → a SMALL-MULTIPLES GRID.** The one-at-a-time chip picker (pick one muscle, see one chart) is gone — all `MAJOR_MUSCLES` now render as a `grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-3 lg:grid-cols-5` grid of tiny sparkline cards (56px-tall `Line`, no axes/grid — a genuine sparkline, not a shrunk full chart), each with its own MEV–MAV band and a `X/wk` badge. "Which muscles am I under-dosing" is a comparison question a one-at-a-time picker forced holding numbers in memory across clicks to answer; a grid answers it at a glance. Same pass replaced the MEV–MAV band's stacked-`Area` hack with a real `ReferenceArea` (semantically a background, not a data series) and made the MRV line neutral grey rather than red — red asserts a danger the guardrail copy immediately retracts ("whether that's too much depends on... nothing here measures").
    - **`RelativeStrengthChart.tsx` → INDEXED TO 100.** The dual-axis-ish ratio-line-plus-separate-bodyweight-line is gone; `progressAggregate.ts`'s new `indexRelativeStrengthTrend` rebases both `est1rmValue` and `bodyweightKg` to 100 at the window's first point, plotted on ONE shared axis — "+6% strength, −4% bodyweight" now reads directly with zero mental division, which is the standard finance/data-viz technique for comparing two differently-scaled series (confirmed against real precedent; no fitness app was found doing this for a strength context specifically, but nothing beats it either). Built ON TOP of the existing `computeRelativeStrengthTrend` output, not a parallel computation.
    - **`RepRangeDistributionChart.tsx` → a single 100%-STACKED HORIZONTAL BAR.** Direct MacroFactor precedent (its dashboard stacks macro composition the same way, segment width = share, specifically because the reasoning is proportion-based). Hand-built with flex divs rather than forced through Recharts' categorical-bar machinery for a single row — simpler and more predictable. Colours are deliberately CATEGORICAL (`BUCKET_COLOR`, five distinct hues), never a green→red ramp, which would assert a "good"/"bad" end the literature doesn't support. The documented industry caveat (a 100%-stacked bar can make a viewer forget they're looking at a proportion, not a count) is why the total working-set count (`"318 working sets in this window"`) stays printed next to the bar, not buried in a tooltip.
    - **`TrainingConsistencyCalendar.tsx` → a plain bar chart + the user's OWN target.** The diverging week-cell heat-strip (a GitHub-contribution-graph-style single row of colour-coded squares, needing a fill-depth legend lookup per cell) is gone, replaced by a `Bar` chart of sessions/week with a `ReferenceLine` at `athlete_profile.training_days_per_week` when set. No fitness app was found using a heat-strip for training consistency specifically, and independent design criticism of the GitHub-heatmap pattern itself is about a different failure mode (activity ≠ quality) rather than praise for its legibility as a trend view — a bar's height is directly readable with zero legend, and a target line converts "3 sessions" into met/missed without inventing a compliance score.
    - **`trainingInsights.ts`'s `groupFindings`** — Training Analysis findings are now grouped by **actionability** ("✅ What's working" / "🔎 What to look at" / "❓ Can't assess yet") rather than sorted flat by evidence tier, with the tier rendering as a small per-item pill instead of the primary sort key — direct WHOOP precedent (its Weekly Performance Assessment buckets a week the same way). **One documented simplification**: the underlying `Finding.positive` is three-valued (true/false/null-as-neutral-fact), but real precedent groups in two actionable buckets, not three — neutral facts (a flat volume trend, an over-MRV note that already says "nothing to fix if you're recovering fine") fold into "What to look at" alongside genuine regressions. "Can't assess yet" (`relative-strength-null`, `exercise-varied-*`) is pulled into its OWN always-visible group — the NEVER_HIDES payoff a flat list buried mid-scroll.
    - **Verified:** both `scripts/verify-training-progress.cjs` (68 assertions — added `indexRelativeStrengthTrend`'s rebasing-to-100 and empty-input cases) and `scripts/verify-training-insights.cjs` (37 assertions — added `groupFindings`' three-bucket split and its nothing-dropped guarantee) grew in this round; live browser walkthrough against production data confirms all five redesigned sections render correctly with real numbers and zero console/runtime errors.
