  - **Weekly-chart tooltip date ranges (real user-reported bug, 2026-09-01).** Every weekly Progress chart showed a single Monday date in its hover tooltip (`3 Aug`) — ambiguous for a WEEKLY value (is it the week's start? end? the day something happened?) and, on `WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart`'s sparklines specifically, the chart had no `XAxis` at all, so hovering showed a number with no date context whatsoever. `dateFormat.ts`'s new `fmtWeekRange(weekStart)` (`"3 Aug – 9 Aug"`) is now the header/label on every weekly tooltip: `WeeklyVolumeChart`'s custom `VolumeTooltip` computes it from the hovered point's own `weekStart` instead of the short XAxis label, `TrainingConsistencyCalendar` and all three `RecoveryLoadPanel` lanes use a `labelFormatter` for the same purpose (a shared `weekRangeLabelFormatter` in the latter, one function for all three lanes), and `WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart`'s sparklines gained a hidden `<XAxis dataKey="weekStart" hide />` purely so recharts has a category to feed that `labelFormatter` — the sparkline itself stays axis-free. `VolumeTooltip`'s row labels were also tightened from a trailing `"· that week"`/`"· 4-week avg"` to a leading `"That week: "`/`"4-week rolling average: "` — the two numbers shown together at one hover point are genuinely different quantities for the SAME week (a weekly total vs. a smoothed trailing average), and the report that prompted this fix read the un-labelled pair as the chart being "shifted" rather than two different numbers. `scripts/verify-training-progress.cjs` gained 2 assertions for `fmtWeekRange` (a plain week and one crossing a month boundary); live browser hover tests against production data confirm all four fixed tooltips now show a real range ("13 Jul – 19 Jul", "17 Aug – 23 Aug", "6 Jul – 12 Jul") with clearly labelled values.
- **Decision engine (2026-09-02, in progress — see `docs/progress-redesign/PLAN.md` while it's still landing, delete that file once this section is the settled record).** Real user feedback on everything above: it's a wall of charts and findings with no per-exercise decision, no "current program" concept, and no answer to "am I ready to add weight." Built from two rounds of `sports-scientist` + `strength-coach` agent consultation plus a user + independent second-review correction pass. `ProgressTab.tsx` now renders **`ProgressOverview` → `ExerciseDecisionTable`** first (a third card, `MuscleDoseSummary`, was removed in Round 4 below) — decisions before data exploration — with every pre-existing chart/panel above (`TrainingInsightsPanel` included) demoted behind a "Show supporting charts & analysis" toggle, collapsed by default. Nothing was deleted.
  - **`progressDecisions.ts`/`progressCopy.ts`** (new, pure, sucrase-tested like `progressAggregate.ts`) are the actual engine; `useProgressData.ts` is the one hook that assembles React Query results and feeds them in — no component computes a decision itself.
  - **Two SEPARATE confidence dimensions, never collapsed into one score**: trend confidence (sample size/week span/effect size/rep-range consistency — never touched by effort data) and action confidence (only meaningful for "Increase weight", capped by RPE/RIR availability). A real design correction: the first draft permanently capped every load-increase call at Medium confidence because `hevy_sets.rpe` is 100% null for this user — the user's explicit correction was that RPE must be optional bonus evidence, never a mandatory gate, since they're about to start logging it. When RPE IS present, Hevy's own mapping is used verbatim (`rpeToRir`: RPE 8→2 RIR, 9→1 RIR, 10→0 RIR) — an average RIR ≥2 lets action confidence reach trend level; RPE absent (or a near-maximal RIR 0-1) caps it at Medium and attaches one fixed, user-specified caveat sentence verbatim: *"Effort was not tracked, so confirm that your technique remained controlled before increasing the weight."*
  - **Expectation/target source order, and why historical reps are NOT in it**: (1) the current program's own recorded `hevy_routine_sets.rep_range_start/end`, (2) the athlete's own explicit `exercise_target_overrides` row, (3) a generic exercise-type default always labeled as such, (4) an explicit **"Target not configured"** state. An earlier draft's rung 2 was "the athlete's own observed rep range over their last 4-6 sessions" — a second review caught this as a self-reinforcing-loop risk (a chronically under-performing lifter would have that treated as "the range" and get told to add weight at a low rep count). Historical reps still drive the TREND read; they never define success.
  - **"Current program" is explicit, never inferred** — migration `084`'s `current_program_routines` (a `routine_id` relation, not a single column, since a split needs more than one routine checked at once) is set via `CurrentProgramPicker.tsx` (Training → Coach → Profile). A pure recency heuristic ("routine trained in the last 3-4 weeks") was drafted and explicitly rejected: a vacation, a skipped week, or an old routine trained once by coincidence would all misclassify what's actually current. Recency still gets ONE job — pre-checking a starting suggestion the first time the picker is empty — never run alone by the engine. `progressDecisions.ts::filterToCurrentProgram` scopes every decision to this explicit set (plus any freeform, routine-less session, always treated as current). **Real bug, fixed 2026-09-02, same day it shipped:** with nothing saved yet, `useProgressData` used to fall through to "nothing is filtered" — every one of 44 historical exercises, current or abandoned months ago, counted as equally current, producing an unreliable verdict and a 40+-row table. The user's own live testing caught this immediately. Fixed by a hard gate: zero rows in `current_program_routines` now returns `needsCurrentProgram: true` and **no decisions at all** — `ProgressOverview` renders an explicit "Select your current training program to generate progress decisions" card (with a one-tap "use these" confirm over the same recency-suggested routines, never auto-applied) instead of a verdict, and `ExerciseDecisionTable`/`MuscleDoseSummary` render nothing until a selection is confirmed.
  - **Muscle preferences are `priority | exclude_direct`, deliberately not a `priority | deprioritized` binary** (migration `084`'s `athlete_muscle_preferences`, via `MusclePreferencesList.tsx`, mirroring `LimitationsList.tsx`'s own visible/editable/deletable pattern exactly). A real semantic correction: "no direct ab training" is not the same claim as "abs matters less" — `exclude_direct` suppresses ONLY the "you have no direct work for this muscle" warning; every indirect/secondary credited set from other exercises keeps counting normally, nothing is zeroed. `MuscleDoseSummary` shows **every muscle actually trained under the current program**, never just a priority-filtered subset (a second-review catch: filtering the default view can hide a genuine deficiency in a non-priority muscle like quads or back) — priority muscles get a highlighted border, `exclude_direct` muscles show their real dose relabeled "Excluded by preference" instead of a warning, nothing is removed from the grid.
  - **`review_workload` is a PROGRAM-level decision, never a single exercise's own status** — `computeProgramDecision` returns `{ progressVerdict, workload }` as two separate facets (rendered side by side in `ProgressOverview`, never merged): `progressVerdict` answers "is progress real", `workload` (`continue`/`review_workload`/`ease_off`) answers "should the training load change" — structurally requires ≥2 different exercises declining beyond the metric-kind-specific noise band AND a corroborating signal from a different source (currently: a ≥2-week unfavorable sleep trend, gated at the same ≥4-tracked-nights/week bar `RecoveryLoadPanel` already uses). A single declining exercise always keeps its own honest exercise-level status (`watch`), never gets relabeled at the program level.
  - **`progressVerdict` is THREE states, not four** (`progressing`/`mixed`/`insufficient_data`) — a same-day user correction: the first draft's `confirmed`/`likely`/`stable`/`insufficient_data` split was exactly the kind of taxonomy a page meant to be read in a few seconds shouldn't need. The evidence sentence underneath (`"3 of 4 analyzable movements improved"`) still carries the precision; only the headline collapsed.
  - **`ExerciseDecisionTable.tsx`** is a desktop table / mobile stacked-card pattern (this repo's standard responsive rule) with a tap-to-expand drill-down in place — evidence sentences, the two confidence pills, the RPE-absent caveat when relevant, and the resolved expectation source. **"Immediate actions" is capped at 5** (Increase/Plateau/Watch first, Keep excluded entirely — a same-day fix after the user reported 40-50 rows dumped on the main screen with no hierarchy) and **"Not enough data" exercises collapse into their own closed section**, never inline in the main list where they used to outnumber the exercises with a real decision.
  - **Muscle dose "expected" is derived from the current program's OWN routine structure, not a generic landmark** — a same-day user correction: `MuscleDoseSummary` originally compared weekly sets against the generic RP-framework MEV/MAV table (the same one `WorkedMuscles`/`WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart` use), and the user correctly rejected this as untrustworthy the moment a specific program is in play ("bu uyarılar programının gerçek beklentisine dayanmıyor olabilir"). `useProgressData` now walks every routine in `current_program_routines`, credits each exercise's own working-set count via the same `contribution()` primary/secondary seam, and sums it per muscle — "**Routine expectation**" is what ONE full pass through the current program calls for. Documented simplification, stated in the UI's own tooltip rather than hidden: this assumes the program is intended to run roughly once a week; a faster/slower cycle reads as over/under its own target even when followed exactly. Each muscle card also shows a **6-week sparkline** of its own weekly-sets trend (`MuscleDoseCard.weeklyTrend`) — the user's explicit ask was to SEE growth over time, not infer it from a single current number — plus a computed `direction` (`improving`/`declining`/`flat`, comparing the trend's first vs. last point). `exclude_direct` muscles render literally as **"Direct training excluded by preference"** and nothing else — no set count, no band, no warning.
  - **Extended, not replaced**: `ProgressSetRow` gained optional `routine_id`/`rpe` fields, and `hevyApi.ts::fetchTrainingHistory` now selects both (neither was fetched before). Two pre-existing bugs found during this audit were fixed in place: `muscleMap.ts::bandForWeeklySets` now takes an optional pre-scaled `Landmarks` override so `WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart`'s badge color and its drawn MEV-MAV band always agree (they used to disagree by exactly the experience-level ±15% scaling factor); `computeConsistencyFindings` now reads `athlete_profile.training_days_per_week` instead of a hardcoded `median >= 2` bar.
  - **`InfoBubble`** (the jargon-explanation Popover, first built inline in `WorkedMuscles.tsx`) is now `src/shared/components/InfoBubble.tsx` — every technical term across the decision engine (trend/action confidence, RPE/RIR, routine expectation, priority/exclude_direct) uses it, per the user's explicit request that no term go unexplained on this page.
  - **Verified:** `scripts/verify-progress-decisions.cjs` — 57 assertions against the real modules (RPE→RIR mapping, current-program filtering including the freeform-always-counts rule, both confidence dimensions independently, the 4-rung expectation order, every `ExerciseStatus` transition including the plateau evidence bar, and `computeProgramDecision`'s 2-exercise+signal requirement for `review_workload`). Not yet exercised live in a browser (no Supabase login available in the authoring session) — `tsc --noEmit` and `npm run build` both clean.
  - **Pending manual steps**: migration `084` (`current_program_routines`/`athlete_muscle_preferences`/`exercise_target_overrides`) not yet applied — every new surface degrades to its empty/loading state until it is, per this repo's pre-migration-safe convention.
  - **Deliberately still not built** (real value, flagged rather than silently dropped): a per-exercise chart with a true time-scaled axis and routine-change markers (the drill-down currently shows evidence/text only, no chart); a dedicated editor UI for `exercise_target_overrides` (the table/API/hooks exist, nothing writes to them yet).
  - **Round 3 (2026-09-02, from live user testing of the merged PR #412; see `docs/progress-redesign/PLAN.md` for the full record).** Two classes of bug, both reported against real numbers from the user's own log.
    - **The classification/confidence logic itself.** `ExerciseDecision` gained a `currentState` field (`previous`/`latest` as real `{weightKg, reps}`, `loadChangePercent`, and an OPTIONAL, explicitly-labeled `estimatedStrengthChange` for `est1rm`-kind exercises only) — the evidence array's first line is now ALWAYS the literal last-comparable-vs-latest-workout numbers (raw sets are the primary result; estimated 1RM is secondary, tooltip-explained, never the headline — fixes a real complaint that a bare unlabeled "10.9 → 12.7" meant nothing on sight). `computeExerciseDecision` now checks `weightChanged`/`loadIncreased` FIRST, before the old flat/decline branches — total reps dropping (30→21) right after a weight increase used to read as a plain decline; it now classifies as one of four states depending on whether reps stayed inside the target range (**"Successful load increase"**), fell below the target minimum (**"Load increase may be premature"**), the weight went down (**"Recent deload"**), or the weight held with more reps (**"Rep progression"**). **Trend vs action confidence were being conflated**: a genuine, deliberate weight increase legitimately swings the rep range — the same swing `repRangeVariedSignificantly` flags as "unexplained noise" and forces to Low — so a real 8kg→10kg jump was crushing a 6-session exercise's trend confidence to Low for no real reason. Fixed: `computeTrendConfidence`'s caller only forces Low when the swing is genuinely UNEXPLAINED (`rawVaried && !weightChanged`); a weight-change-explained swing no longer touches confidence at all (two contrast tests lock this in — same swing, with vs without the weight change). New `ReasonCode`s (`LOAD_INCREASED`/`BELOW_TARGET_MINIMUM`/`LOAD_DECREASED`/`REP_PROGRESSION`/`TREND_DOWN`/…) replace evidence-string regex matching in `computeProgramDecision`, and `decisionHeadline()` (new, `progressCopy.ts`) derives the specific on-screen label from `status` + `reasonCodes` — the coarse status bucket (filtering/sorting/tone) and the human-readable headline are now two separate concerns, per the user's own proposed "reasonCodes → dynamic explanation" architecture (evidence-sentence composition itself stays inline in `computeExerciseDecision`, a stated simplification rather than a full second copy-generation layer). The duplicate "Immediate Actions" list is gone — `ExerciseDecisionTable` is the single view now: 5 tabs (Recent Changes — default, sorted most-recently-trained / Ready to Increase / Building at New Weight / Needs Attention / All Exercises) plus a name search box and a sort dropdown (Most recently trained / Action priority / Largest improvement / Closest to progression / Lowest confidence). **Not yet built**: filtering by muscle group, routine/workout, or a date range — flagged as a real fast-follow, not silently dropped.
    - **Decisions/muscle-dose were scoped to the wrong data.** `useProgressData`'s exercise list used to include every `exercise_template_id` ever logged under a current-program `routine_id`, even ones a routine has since swapped OUT of its current structure — a routine's real "9 of 13" exercises read as "6 of 44". Fixed: `templateIds` is now intersected with the union of `activeRoutines[].exercises[].exercise_template_id`, the routine's CURRENT list, not its historical one; `ProgressOverview`'s "Exercise progress"/"Data confidence" cards got clarified captions since their denominators are genuinely different scopes. Separately, muscle dose was being judged MID-WEEK — a Wednesday with 2 of 4 workouts done showed `Gap: -7.5` against the full week's plan before the week was actually over. Fixed: `MuscleDoseCard.direction`/`gap`/`weeklyTrend` now only ever look at COMPLETE weeks (`lastCompleteWeek()`, reused from `trainingInsights.ts`); a new, purely informational `currentWeek` object covers the in-progress week separately (`completedSets`/`remainingPlannedSets`/`workoutsCompleted`/`workoutsPlanned`/`status`) — `remainingPlannedSets` comes from the REAL structure of whichever current-program routines haven't been trained yet this week (per-routine credit tracking, never a proportional guess), and `status` (`on_track`/`behind_pace`/null) is a soft read against what the routines actually done so far should have produced, never against the whole week's plan, and never anything before the week's first workout is logged. `computeCurrentWeekMuscleDose` (the judgmental piece) was extracted to `progressAggregate.ts` as a pure function specifically so this fix is sucrase-verifiable, not just eyeballed in the hook.
    - **Verified:** `scripts/verify-progress-decisions.cjs` grew from 57 to 85 assertions (reasonCodes/currentState/decisionHeadline, all four load-increase classifications, the weight-change-explained-confidence contrast pair, and `computeCurrentWeekMuscleDose`'s five cases).
  - **Round 4 — the Exercise Progress Engine rewrite (2026-09-03; full narrative + a multi-round algorithm-review record: `docs/training/progress-engine/`).** A deeper correctness pass than Round 3's fixes could reach without a real architecture change: the per-exercise pieces of `progressDecisions.ts` (`ExerciseDecision`, `computeExerciseDecision`, `ExposureSummary`, `CurrentStateSummary`) are **retired** (the file's per-exercise functions still exist, dead, alongside the still-live `computeProgramDecision`/`ProgramDecision` the program-level verdict still uses) in favour of a new, reusable, versioned engine: `src/features/training/progress-engine/` (`ALGORITHM_VERSION` in `policies.ts`, stamped on every result).
    - **Complete ordered set vectors, finally.** `fetchTrainingHistory` now selects `hevy_workouts.title` and `hevy_sets.index` (both real, `NOT NULL` columns, verified against the migration DDL) — a session's real workout title and real per-set order reach the engine for the first time; `normalize.ts::buildCanonicalSessions` groups by `workout_id` (never date alone) into a `CanonicalExerciseSession` carrying every non-warmup set, never reduced to one "best set" before the decision layer.
    - **Four+ independent facets, not one collapsed status** (`comparability.ts`): `observedTransition` (a pure LOAD_INCREASED/DECREASED/UNCHANGED/NO_COMPARISON fact) / `repDelta` / `rangeCompliance` / `evaluationScope` (ALL_PRESCRIBED_WORKING_SETS / LOGGED_SETS_ONLY / TOP_SET_ONLY / NOT_EVALUATED — copy never claims more scope than was actually inspected) / `dataQualityFlags` (MISSING_PRESCRIBED_SET / EXTRA_UNPRESCRIBED_SET / MIXED_LOAD_SESSION / a reserved, **never-emitted** PROGRAM_CHANGED — detecting a real historical target-range change needs point-in-time target snapshots this app doesn't record, see `DATA_AND_LIMITATIONS.md`) / `currentAction`. A data-quality issue never replaces the factual transition — it narrows `evaluationScope` and downgrades `evidence.recommendation` instead, landing on `CONFIRM_AT_CURRENT_LOAD` rather than either a false-confident or a blocked read.
    - **`SessionLoadStructure`** (`uniform_working_load` / `top_set_and_backoff` / `mixed_load`) — a session's sets are never collapsed to one `weightKg` when they don't share one clean load or backoff shape. A 2-set heavier-then-lighter session is *always* a valid backoff by definition (only one possible "rest" value); `mixed_load` genuinely needs ≥3 sets with real internal inconsistency. A mixed-load session forces `evaluationScope`/`rangeCompliance` to `NOT_EVALUATED` and `currentAction` to `HOLD_STEADY` — but all-history PR detection still runs off its own single heaviest set, since a PR fact doesn't require a clean structure.
    - **Two structurally separate trend axes** (`trend.ts`), replacing one unlimited-lifetime "overall" trend: a configurable-window `recentProgressTrend` (default the last 8 comparable sessions, always disclosed to the user as "based on the last N") and a load-cycle-scoped `currentLoadProgress`. All-history PR/event detection (`events.ts`) is never windowed — an earlier successful load cycle keeps its `LOAD_PR` forever even once outside the recent window. `linearFit`'s directional-consistency measure (least-squares slope + RMS residual over session index) replaces the retired `repRangeVariedSignificantly`: a monotonic climb (20→21→22→23→24) reads as `ACCUMULATING`, never "high variation" the way a raw max-min-spread check would have wrongly flagged it; `ACCUMULATING` itself now requires a real slope past `accumulationSlopeFloor`, never merely "not negative" — a flat-but-early read is honestly `TOO_EARLY_TO_JUDGE`/`BUILDING_BASELINE` before ever reaching `POSSIBLE_PLATEAU`.
    - **A raw load decrease is neutral, never "deload," for an external-load metric** (`REVIEW_LOAD_REDUCTION` — intent is genuinely unknown) — but the identical raw decrease is the POSITIVE direction for an assisted-bodyweight exercise, via one centralized `isPositiveLoadChange` inversion in `policies.ts` rather than a per-branch special case.
    - **Next Target carries a real per-set-position floor**, not just a total (`targets.ts`) — `minimumSetReps` is the latest session's own reps, position-for-position; a candidate like `8/6/8` fails against a floor of `[8,7,6]` even though its total (22) matches, because position 2 regressed. `minimumTotalReps` is a derived convenience number, never the sole check.
    - **`RULE_CATALOG`** (`ruleCatalog.ts`) — every reason/event/action code classified into `science`/`product_rule`/`program_policy`, with a verification assertion (`scripts/verify-progress-engine.cjs`) that every code the engine can actually emit has a catalog entry.
    - **RPE/RIR removed as a dependency entirely** — not read anywhere in `progress-engine/`, never caps/lowers `evidence.recommendation`, no "effort not tracked" caveat anywhere.
    - **Weekly Muscle Dose removed** (`MuscleDoseSummary.tsx` deleted, confirmed zero remaining consumers first) — superseded by this engine; `muscleMap.ts` and every other muscle-mapping consumer (Muscles tab, `WeeklySetsPerMuscleChart`, `TrainingInsightsPanel`) are untouched, as is the collapsed "Show supporting charts & analysis" section.
    - **`ExerciseDecisionTable.tsx` rebuilt** on `ExerciseProgressResult`, same inline-row-expansion interaction: the real exercise GIF (shared `exerciseMedia.tsx` resolver, same "Fix GIF" control `ExerciseTemplatesTab` uses), both evidence labels (exact wording **"Limited/Moderate/Strong evidence"**), deterministic event chips, full previous/latest session cards, "Show all sessions", a real "Show progress chart" (`BarLineChart`, real dates), and evidence-level/date-window filters alongside the existing tabs/search/sort. **Not yet built**: muscle-group and specific-routine filters — need per-exercise metadata this component doesn't receive yet, flagged as a fast-follow.
    - **Verified:** `scripts/verify-progress-engine.cjs` — 72 assertions against the real un-mocked engine (load-structure classification, canonical session building, the per-pair comparability read incl. the PROGRAM_CHANGED-never-emitted and set-count-mismatch-blocks-raw-total-comparison guarantees, `buildNextTargets`'s per-position floor, `linearFit`/`computeCurrentLoadProgress`'s every reachable state, windowed-vs-all-history event detection, five full worked examples — Hammer Curl, Chest Press, Leg Extension ×2 incl. the precedence-override case, Back Squat, Assisted Pull-up — `isPositiveLoadChange`, the copy formatter, and `RULE_CATALOG` completeness). `scripts/verify-progress-decisions.cjs` dropped from 85 to 76 assertions (only the 9 covering the now-deleted `computeCurrentWeekMuscleDose` were removed; `computeProgramDecision`'s own coverage is unchanged and still passes). Confirmed against the REAL production `ProgressOverview`/`ExerciseDecisionTable` components (not a mock) via a temporary, unpublished local harness aliasing only the data-source hook to realistic fixtures computed through the real engine — not yet exercised against a live Supabase login (no access in the authoring session, a recurring, already-documented constraint in this file).
- **Muscles sub-tab** (`WorkedMuscles.tsx` + `muscleMap.ts`): a stylised front/back body (`react-muscle-highlighter`, MIT) on a dark panel, coloured by an **evidence-based training-VOLUME algorithm** (designed with the `strength-coach` + `sports-scientist` agents, `.claude/agents/`). **Not** a self-relative heat ramp — an **absolute diverging scale** vs per-muscle landmarks. Currency = **weekly hard working sets** per muscle (warm-ups excluded via `hevy_sets.type`); each exercise credits its PRIMARY muscle 1.0 and each SECONDARY 0.5 via the pluggable `contribution(templateId, slug, role)` seam (`ROLE_WEIGHTS`; future: per-exercise-per-muscle % from `CONTRIBUTION_OVERRIDES`/DB, incl. tertiary). Window total ÷ (days/7) → weekly-equivalent, compared to `MUSCLE_LANDMARKS` (per-muscle MV/MEV/MAV/MRV, RP framework) → 6 diverging **bands** (`BANDS_META`: grey not-trained · blue below-maintenance · teal maintenance · **green optimal MEV–MAV** · amber high · red over-MRV). **Colours are ONE fixed palette, `BODY_MAP_COLORS` (`components/muscleBandColors.ts`), not theme tokens** — the body always sits on a dark `bg-scrim` stage, and the light-theme tone values made cyan/teal/green indistinguishable there with untrained grey brighter than trained muscles (a real regression from the 2026-09 restyle, reported as "they are all the same colour"). The days-since-trained view is a list (`improve/MuscleRecencyList.tsx`, tone dots), not a second body. Period 30 days (default) / 90 days, data via `hevyApi.fetchMuscleVolume(from,to)` (paginated). UI (details never skipped, per user): diverging legend + ℹ️ `InfoBubble` popovers explaining colours/caveats, under-dosed & over-MRV lists, push:pull + quad:ham balance chips, side-filtered top chips dotted with band colour, per-muscle detail (sets/wk + band + target MEV–MAV + exercises with set counts and `ExerciseThumb` GIFs + training-day history), and an "unattributed work" line for cardio/full_body/other (primary dropped, secondaries still count). `HEVY_TO_SLUG` covers the COMPLETE Hevy `primary_muscle_group` enum (20 values, verified against OpenAPI + live DB: 0 unmapped, 0 missing templates).
- **Exercise demo GIFs** (`exerciseMedia.tsx` → `ExerciseThumb`): Hevy's API returns no exercise media, so animated GIFs come from **JahelCuadrado/ExerciseGymGifsDB** (1323 exercises, jsDelivr CDN, CORS-open, free — no key/proxy). Every actual GIF request goes browser→CDN directly, never through our own hosting/Supabase — the manifest JSON (names/URLs/instructions, no binary data) is fetched once per session (`useExerciseImageDb`, `staleTime: Infinity`) and thumbnails load lazily, so this adds no server load either way. `matchExercise` (now in `exerciseGifResolver.ts`) fuzzy-matches Hevy titles by normalized name tokens + an equipment bonus (threshold 0.5) with a graceful no-match fallback. Tapping a thumbnail opens a modal with the larger GIF + instructions. Wired into `ExerciseTemplatesTab`, `RoutinesTab`, `HevyWorkoutDetail`, the Next tab and the Muscles detail list (all pass `templateId`, not just `title`). **Every GIF URL is anchored to the single `GIF_SOURCE` constant** in `hooks/useExerciseImageDb.ts` (`https://cdn.jsdelivr.net/gh/JahelCuadrado/ExerciseGymGifsDB@v1.1.0`) — self-hosting later = mirror the repo into Supabase Storage and swap that one constant, no UI/matching changes.
  - **Manual override (migration `082`, `exercise_gif_overrides`, 2026-09-01)** — the fuzzy matcher has no ground truth to correct itself against, so a wrong match (or a real exercise the dataset just doesn't have) had no fix short of a code change; the user hit real wrong matches and asked for a way to fix them. `resolveExerciseGif` (moved into the new, import-free `exerciseGifResolver.ts` — `exerciseMedia.tsx` itself now pulls in the override React Query hooks, which transitively import the live Supabase client, breaking `scripts/verify-exercise-gif-overrides.cjs`'s sucrase-require if the pure logic stayed there) checks a per-user override **first**, keyed on the exercise's stable `exercise_template_id` — **never its title**, which the user can rename inside the Hevy app itself, silently detaching a title-keyed override from the exercise it was meant to fix. An override always wins outright, no threshold. `ExerciseGifPicker` is the editor UI, wired in ONLY on `ExerciseTemplatesTab` (the one canonical list of every exercise, not a pencil icon on all six render sites) — search the SAME already-loaded `useExerciseImageDb` dataset for a better match, or paste any public GIF URL directly for an exercise the 1323-entry dataset has no entry for at all (`source: 'manual'` vs `'exercisegymgifsdb'`, informational only). Still fetched from the public CDN either way — the override only changes which URL is used, never how/where GIFs are hosted. Pre-migration-safe: a read degrades to `[]`, a write throws a named "migration 082 not applied" error (`athleteProfileApi.ts`'s own convention). **Verified:** `scripts/verify-exercise-gif-overrides.cjs` — 5 assertions (override wins outright, falls through cleanly with no override, null on no match at all, `templateId` omitted still works for pre-existing call sites, an override never leaks across template ids sharing a similar title).
- `TrainingMonthCalendar` (Log → Hevy) — month only, Monday-first weeks including the neighbouring months' days (`calendar/monthGrid.ts`), the current week on a tinted band. One mark per day (`dayMarkOf`): solid success bar = trained, warn ring = planned today, info ring = upcoming, danger outline = missed; a Strava-orange bar = Strava activity. Today's number is ringed in the accent; a week-totals column ("1/4" against `training_days_per_week`) from a 22rem container; one ⓘ legend. Plans come from `calendar/calendarPlans.buildPlansByDate` (one-off training blocks + recurring templates projected from `effective_from`, ids `<template>__<date>`; also used by the popup via `useTrainingPlansByDate`). The selected day's rows open the `training-session` popup; "Plan a session" only today and later.
- `RoutinesTab` expanded view: one bordered row per exercise — order number + demo GIF + set chips + notes.
- **`RoutineModals` — Hevy payload traps (each a real 400):** the payload must match the Hevy OpenAPI schema exactly (no extra keys). Set fields = `type, weight_kg, reps, rep_range:{start,end}, distance_meters, duration_seconds, custom_metric` (NO `rpe`/`index`); exercise = `exercise_template_id, superset_id, rest_seconds, notes, sets` (NO `index`/`title`). (1) **`rep_range: null` → 400** "must be of type object" — send `rep_range` only when a range is entered, otherwise OMIT the key and send fixed `reps`. (2) **`folder_id` is not allowed on PUT** (400 "not allowed") — valid on create (POST) only. `formToPayload` builds payloads this way; `hevy-api` also defensively strips null `rep_range` and drops `folder_id` from update bodies. Set inputs render per exercise `type` via `setFieldsForType`.
- Hevy exercise `type` is the `CustomExerciseType` enum (`weight_reps`, `reps_only`, `bodyweight_reps`, `bodyweight_weighted`, `bodyweight_assisted`, `duration`, `weight_duration`, `distance_duration`, `short_distance_weight`, `floors_duration`, `steps_duration`) — typed as `string`, humanised in `ExerciseTemplatesTab`.
- Edge functions normalise Hevy responses (`unwrapEntity`) — Hevy may return an entity directly, wrapped, or array-wrapped. Incremental sync reads `event.workout` (updates) / `event.id` (deletes).
- **Body measurement payloads (three stacked real bugs, live-verified):** (1) Hevy 400s on ANY `null` field in `PUT /v1/body_measurements/{date}` ("Expected number, received null") — the form used to send the full field set with nulls for blanks, so EVERY save failed regardless of input; empty fields must be **OMITTED** (partial entry is legit — both `handleSave` and `hevy-api` strip them). (2) That PUT returns **2xx with an EMPTY body** — `hevyRequest`'s unconditional `res.json()` threw "Unexpected end of JSON input" AFTER a successful write, so even a valid full form reported an error; `hevyRequest` now parses tolerantly (empty/non-JSON → null). (3) **MERGE semantics** — Hevy's PUT replaces the WHOLE day and the DB upsert used to null out omitted columns, so a partial save (e.g. tapping the weight suggestion chip alone) silently WIPED the other values already recorded for that date in both Hevy and the local DB (**real data-loss incident**). `hevy-api` now loads the existing row for the date, overlays only the provided fields, and sends/stores the full merged set; the modal prefills stored values for the selected date so you see what a save keeps. Suggestion chips only fill a form field — they never write anything. Form fields are `type="text" inputMode="decimal"` with a digits+single-dot sanitizer (comma→dot) plus one-tap chips prefilling latest weight/body-fat from Apple Health (`weight_body_mass`/`body_fat_percentage`, 60d window).
- **Sync scope:** workouts use the events-delta feed (`/v1/workouts/events`). Routines, routine folders and body measurements have NO event feed, so `hevy-incremental-sync` also re-fetches those three collections in full and upserts them on every Sync (a routine created in the Hevy app appears on Sync, no full re-import needed). Exercise templates still only come via full re-import.
- **Planned session ↔ task auto-link:** `RoutinesTab`'s "Plan routine" creates a task with `source_type='training_session'`, `source_id=routine.id` (migration `039`), linked to its one-off schedule block via `time_blocks.task_id` (migration `077`). When the matching Hevy workout is logged from that routine, the inlined `upsertWorkoutToDb` deletes the task — its linked `time_blocks` row goes with it automatically via `task_id`'s `ON DELETE CASCADE` (see Edge Functions). Freeform workouts (no `routine_id`, or a mismatch) can't be auto-matched — the workout cards in **Log → Hevy** show a manual close-out button when an open training-session task falls on the same calendar day. **Migration `043`'s old bidirectional "block delete cascades to task" / "block date syncs task due_date" triggers were retired by migration 077** — the task's deadline and its schedule slot are independent facts now: deleting the plan's `time_blocks` row (e.g. via `DayAgenda`'s ✕) never touches the task, and editing the block's date/time never moves the task's `due_date`. The task survives; only the time slot changes.

DB (Hevy): `hevy_workouts`/`_exercises`/`hevy_sets`, `hevy_routines`/`_exercises`/`hevy_routine_sets` (routine sets also have `rep_range_start/end`), `hevy_exercise_templates`(+`_muscles`), `hevy_routine_folders`, `hevy_body_measurements`, `hevy_workout_events_cursor`.

#### Health page (`/health`, 2026-09-27)

`src/features/health/` (moved out of Training). `HealthPage` has ONE period control (Today / 7 / 30 / 90 days / 1 year, in `?date=&period=` so Daily's links land on the right day) and a profile chip (birth year, sex, height — `athlete_profile`, migration `110`). **Hero "How you're doing"** = the six tier-1 metrics of a fact-checked ranking (evidence × data available on a Series 11 × real interest data — `docs/training-health/research/research-rank.json`): sleep (last night + 7-night vs 7 h, wake-time spread), steps (7-day vs the 7–8k band), exercise this week (minutes vs 150 + Hevy strength days vs 2), resting HR (vs your own 60-day baseline), weight (7-day average, kg/week), overnight vitals (HRV vs your ±1 SD band + how many vitals are outside your usual range). **No composite score.** Each tile opens a sheet with a chart, trend stats, a reference ladder built from `classify` itself (`benchmarks/referenceLadder.ts`), how to improve, caveats and sources. VO₂ max is deliberately NOT a hero tile (sporadic, reads low) — it leads the Cardio fitness window with its date. **The page is in windows (tabs, `?section=`, `HEALTH_SECTIONS` in `components/sectionTypes.ts`) — the owner preferred them over the one long scroll it briefly was:** Overview (hero + insights `buildHealthInsights` + the profile card while incomplete) · Sleep · Activity (rings, steps, exercise week, energy, a collapsed **Other workouts (N)** card, more activity, mobility) · Heart & vitals · Body (the smart scale only) · **Goal progress** (`?section=goal`; old `cut` links still open it) · Cardio fitness. There is no Workouts window any more (2026-09-29): strength workouts (`workoutKinds.isStrengthWorkout` — Apple's "… Strength Training") live only under Training, the rest are Activity's Other workouts card; `?section=workouts` opens Activity. Only the open window mounts; each window is a PageBoard (`healthBoards.ts`): its main chart card in main, trend and timing cards beside it, the mini-metric grids after them (a grid that hides without data always sits under a card that is always there); Body is `BodyScaleCard` + `BodyWeightTrendCard` + `HevyMeasurementsCard`. The period + date bar (`HealthRangeBar`) sits directly under the section tabs and drives all of them (hidden on Goal progress, which shows its own 14/28/56-day window dates); each hero sheet has an "Open the … tab" button. Compact tier-3 cards are hidden without data. Pure modules: `healthWindowStats.ts` (one window rule for every headline — `verify-health-window-stats`), `healthTrendStats.ts` (7/30/90-day change, weekly rate, best/worst week, variability, usual ranges — `verify-health-trend-stats`, 107), `bodyweight.ts` (the one merged series — `verify-bodyweight`), `benchmarks/healthBenchmarks.ts` + `healthGuidance.ts` (`verify-health-benchmarks`, 202). Hooks fetch one long window per metric (≥186 days) so hero, sections and trends share a download.

**Round 3 (2026-09-27, owner feedback).** The range bar puts the PeriodToggle first and the DateNav after it with a fixed-width, dates-only label — numeric `21.09 – 27.09` (year on both ends outside the current year, `27.09` for one day — `healthDateLabels.numericSpanLabel`, the owner's format for this bar only; `verify-health-date-labels`), so nothing shifts. Weekly bars label their week as a range ("7–13 Jul", `weekRangeLabel`). **"Last night" is only the night filed under the selected day** (`nightEndingOn`) — hero, Sleep window, Daily's HealthCard and Training → Next say "No sleep recorded last night" instead of showing an older night. **Heart & vitals** opens with "What your numbers say" (`vitalsReading.ts`, `verify-vitals-reading`, 99): per signal value · your usual (the 60 days BEFORE the judged day) · status · one plain sentence, and an overall verdict that is a count of what's off, never a score or a diagnosis. **Body** shows the smart scale (weight + 7-day average, body fat %, lean mass as plain lines) plus "Logged in Hevy" (Round 4); body fat/lean come only from the scale currently in use (`currentDeviceSeries` — two scales read ~2 points apart). **The one bodyweight series is now scale-first** (`bodyweight.ts`: scale via Apple Health → scale photo report → Hevy only on days with no scale reading; Apple rows written by Hevy count as hand-typed) — live data showed every report/Hevy weight duplicated a scale reading.

**Round 4 (2026-09-28, owner feedback).**
- **Overview tiles:** each shows its band pill with "Lower/Higher is better", an **Aim:** line and one plain sentence (`BenchmarkInfo.plain`, which covers every metric; every `MetricExplainer` opens with plain + direction + aim). Citations stay in the sheets.
- **Aims agree with their band** (`benchmarks/aimGuidance.ts`, pure, exported from `healthBenchmarks`): resting HR and HRV against your own usual (resting HR compared unrounded); weight against the goal weight (`day_targets`), else the BMI 18.5–24.9 range for your height (`healthyWeightRange` gives the exact edges `classifyBmi` calls healthy — 180 cm → 59.8–80.8 kg; nothing about the goal while it loads; the Weight sheet has "Edit goal"); everything else from its `classify` band. `summarizeVitals` takes a per-reading `good` direction, so HRV above your usual is `better`, never `outside`. `Aim.more` holds sentences for the sheet only.
- **Workouts** (Activity → Other workouts, and Training's "Open Apple workout"): each opens the `health-workout` popup (`modals/HealthWorkoutEntityModal`, `components/workout/`): time in ACSM heart-rate zones against max HR estimated from age (only with HR samples; `workoutStats.timeInZones`, `verify-health-workout-stats`), kcal/min, and the Hevy session logged at the same time (`useMatchedHevyWorkout`, the same `workoutHealthMatch` rule) with sets, volume, pace (sets/min) and RPE, plus "Open in Training" (`{kind:'training-session', workoutId}`). List rows carry a "Hevy · <title>" chip; Training's `WorkoutHealthStats` has "Open Apple workout". When the popup below already shows the target, the link reads "Back to …" and closes the top popup (`useLinkIsBack`), so the stack never grows.
- **Body → "Logged in Hevy":** the whole Hevy row (weight, fat, lean mass as entered in Hevy, plus tape measurements) — Latest = the newest entry on or before the viewed day (newer ones are counted, not shown), changes, and history — collapsed by default behind "Show earlier entries (N)" (`components/body/`, `hevyMeasurements.ts`, `verify-hevy-measurements`). Logging or editing opens the `body-measurement` popup by date. Never merged into the scale charts.

**Goal progress** (`health/goal/`, `GoalReportCard`; was the Cut report): every verdict follows the phase in `day_targets.goal` (Cut / Maintain / Gain; read-only here — change it in Your goal). Pace as %BW/week from the fitted trend — cut 0.5–1 % (Helms 2014, Garthe 2011), gain 0.25–0.5 % (Iraki 2019), maintain ±0.25 % (heuristic) — with the smallest kcal change back into range; fat vs lean from the scale (a change counts only past the bioimpedance noise floor, Looney 2024; never mixes two scales) → a path verdict (losing fat keeping muscle / losing some muscle / recomposition / gaining mostly fat …) and concrete steps; goals for weight, body fat %, muscle mass and phase start (the one goal on `day_targets`, migration 113 — see Food → `useDayTargets`) with progress and a projected date. One card on a phone; from 1280 its goals, protein and calories-vs-scale blocks become their own cards (`GOAL_BOARD`). **Calories vs the scale** compares only paired days — a full diary (≥ max(800, 60 % of the calorie target)) AND a complete Apple day — and states the window, burned vs logged, the last 7 days alone, the scale's own deficit (−trend × 7,700), the days left out and why, and why Activity/Food's 7-day cards differ (`energyBalance.paired`, `recent7`, `scaleDeficit`); the suggested logged intake comes from the paired days. The headline and every calorie/protein step come from THE goal decision (see Food → `useNutritionCoach`; always the 28-day window, noted when another window is picked). Pure: `energyBalance.ts` (`verify-energy-balance`, 87), `bodyGoal.ts`/`goalPath.ts`/`goalReport.ts`/`goalSettings.ts` (`verify-body-goal`, 189).

**Muscle watch** (`goal/muscleWatch.ts`, pure; `verify-muscle-watch`, 60 — incl. the owner's own numbers) answers "am I losing muscle?" in one plain sentence from data the app already loads — no new fetch: `useMuscleWatch()` (the ONE place it is assembled) reads the 28-day goal report (its `readings`, pace, protein) and the training history (the current program's main lifts through `plan/improvement.ts` `computeLiftChanges`/`mainLifts` over 4 weeks — the Improvement card's own per-lift rule — plus sessions/working sets). Signals: strength (est. 1RM down > 5 % on 2+ main lifts), scale (lean or report muscle down > 1.0 kg over 4+ weeks since the phase start, one scale only), pace (> 1 %BW/week), protein (< 1.6 g/kg; aim ~2.2 g/kg ≈ 180 g at 83 kg, or 2.3–3.1 g/kg lean mass); lean share of the loss and training frequency are context only. Any one → **watch**; **likely losing muscle** only with falling strength plus one other — a scale drop alone never counts (early-cut water/glycogen). A muscle goal on a cut (or needing > 0.5 kg/month to reach within a year) is named a long-term target, not this phase's. Shown as the `muscle` card under the goals (`GOAL_BOARD`, its own card under the report on a phone), an Overview insight (`buildHealthInsights`' `muscleWatch` input, with a link) and a one-line link on Training → Next and Progress (`MuscleWatchLine`, watch/likely only). A flat body-target row now says the real change and why there is no date (`bodyGoal.noDateReason`: "−0.8 kg since 13.08.2026 · going down while the goal is up · too noisy for a date yet").

#### Health data (Apple Health)

**Apple Health is the sole data source** (the Fitbit Air / Google Health API integration was fully removed 2026-09-01 — see the note at the end of this section). Huawei's own Health Kit API is closed to individual developers (requires an AppGallery-published app), so: Huawei Health on iOS syncs into Apple HealthKit → the **Health Auto Export** app reads HealthKit and POSTs to `health-export-webhook`. Lands in `health_workouts` / `health_metrics` (shared `HEVY_USER_ID`, separate from Hevy's tables).

- **Sleep catch-up (2026-09-27, live-measured root cause).** HAE's "Since Last Sync" run only re-summarises sleep from ~6 h before its PREVIOUS run, and the Watch hands a night to the iPhone minutes after waking — so a run in that gap loses the start of the night (about half the nights, 15 min–3 h each) until a "Previous 7 Days" run. Fix: a sleep-only automation every 2 h, `docs/health-auto-export/06-sleep-catch-up.json` (runbook in that folder's README; idempotent upserts). All three sleep merges (client, `ai-proxy`, `phone-gateway`) drop a session with ≥90 % inside, or <15 min outside, a better-ranked one. `sleepCompleteness.ts` (`verify-sleep-completeness`) flags a possibly incomplete night in the Sleep window.
- **Point-in-time grain, not daily** (migration `041`): `health_metrics` was originally one row per `(metric, day, source)` — a real bug confirmed against production exports; every new point for the same day silently overwrote the previous one, so only the LAST point of the day survived (`active_energy` for a full day showing 0.05 kcal instead of ~230). Fixed by storing every incoming point as its own row keyed `(user_id, metric_name, recorded_at, source)`. `date` stays a plain column **computed from the export's own local-time string at ingest, not derived from the UTC `recorded_at`** (avoids a midnight timezone-shift bug). No aggregation at ingest.
- **`source_family`** — values are `apple | manual` (the CHECK constraint still also allows the historical `fitbit` value so old rows aren't rejected, but nothing writes it any more). The column is `NOT NULL DEFAULT 'apple'` **and** explicitly stamped by `health-export-webhook` (belt-and-suspenders, not either/or). Existing rows are legitimately all `apple` — Huawei data already flows through Apple HealthKit before reaching us.
- **Aggregation happens at query time**, per metric, via `healthMetrics.ts`'s `METRIC_AGGREGATION`: `sum` (cumulative — steps/energy/distance/durations), `average` (rates/levels — speeds/dB/physical effort/respiratory rate/HRV), `minmaxavg` (`heart_rate`'s Min/Avg/Max points, and SpO2 — a locked guess pending live shape confirmation), `latest` (point-in-time — weight/body fat/BMI/resting HR), `sleep` (`sleep_analysis`'s own stage-duration merge). **Unrecognized metrics default to `latest`, the safest fallback.** The pure functions live in `healthAggregate.ts` (`computeDailySeries`/`computeHourlyBuckets`/`computeHeartRateDailySeries`/`computeSleepSummary`) and deliberately ignore `source` when merging (a day can have differing source strings, e.g. `"Furkan's Apple Watch"` at midnight vs `"Furkan's Apple Watch|Lasci"` once a second device joins — the UI shows one number; raw rows stay source-separated in the DB).
- **No cross-source resolver any more.** A whole priority-ladder system (`resolveSourcePoints` + `healthSourceDefaults.ts`) used to pick one winning device stream per hour/day/night when Apple and Fitbit both wrote data for the same window. Removed on the user's explicit 2026-09-01 decision, since Apple Health is now the only source and does its own internal source resolution — every `health_metrics` row for a metric+window is summed/merged as-is, with no per-window stream competition. `collapseIntraStreamMinuteDuplicates` (below) is unrelated and stays — it fixes a same-STREAM duplicate, not a cross-source one. There is no more per-section Auto/Apple/Google display switch (`SourceToggle.tsx` was deleted) — every Health chart just shows the one source there is.
- **Intra-stream workout twins (real bug, fixed 21/07/2026):** starting a Fitness-app workout makes HealthKit hold OVERLAPPING step samples (regular + workout-associated); HAE exports each with only its START time, so one stream delivered the same minutes twice as float-noise twins (20/07/2026 showed **15,362** steps vs Apple's own **5,731**; 88 of 94 minutes were twins inside ONE stream). `collapseIntraStreamMinuteDuplicates` (`healthAggregate.ts`, `sum`-metrics only, applied after grouping) keeps only the largest same-minute-same-stream point as a display backstop; the automation's **Time Grouping = Hours** setting is the real source-level fix (Apple dedupes before export).
- **Steps/active/basal energy inflated 2-3x, TWO rounds, both live-confirmed 2026-09-06 right after the user triggered Health Auto Export's 7-day and 30-day reconciliation automations.**
  - **Round 1:** HAE's `source` field is a `"|"`-joined list of every device that contributed to an aggregated interval — but the join is neither deduped nor consistently ordered between exports. The same hour, same value, arrived as `source: "Furkan's Apple Watch"` from the regular sync and later as `source: "Furkan's Apple Watch|Furkan's Apple Watch"` (the SAME device listed twice) from the reconciliation export — a different string, so it passed the `(user_id,metric_name,recorded_at,source)` unique key as a NEW row instead of upserting over the existing one. Fixed at ingest: `health-export-webhook`'s `canonicalizeSource()` (dedupes the `"|"`-joined parts, sorts them, folds a stray NBSP inside "Apple Watch" to a normal space) is applied before the `source` column is written, so future re-exports of the same device combination upsert onto the SAME row.
  - **Round 2 (same day — round 1 wasn't enough):** basal energy still averaged ~5400 kcal/day (a real BMR is ~1600-2400) and steps still read 2-3x high. Live data showed why: every hour carries not two but often THREE rows — round 1's exact duplicate, PLUS a third at a genuinely DIFFERENT minute with a genuinely DIFFERENT device combination in `source` (e.g. `"06:00:00"` qty=83.7 `"Watch|Lasci 17 Pro"` and `"06:39:34"` qty=64.2 just `"Watch"`) — a per-minute-per-source key still missed this and summed it on top. Root cause: these are not independent per-device contributions — Health Auto Export re-reports the SAME real hour's already-merged HealthKit total every time its sync automations re-fire, with `source` just reflecting whatever raw samples happened to be available to HealthKit's own merge at that moment (confirmed: 77.55 / 77.64 / 77.60 kcal — nearly identical values — for the SAME real hour). Since HAE's own "Time Grouping: Hours" setting means there should be exactly one row per hour, the fix groups by **HOUR ONLY**, ignoring both the exact minute and the source string entirely: `healthAggregate.ts`'s `collapseIntraStreamMinuteDuplicates` (client) and `ai-proxy`'s `collapseDuplicateSumPoints` (its own independent copy, for the same reason described below) now keep the reading landing exactly on the hour boundary (`":00:00"`, HAE's "hour is now closed" delivery) over any mid-hour partial, or the larger of two partials when neither lands on the boundary — never the sum. Verified against real numbers: a naive sum for one partial day of basal energy totaled 3309 kcal; hour-level collapse brings the SAME rows to 1164 kcal (a sane ~80 kcal/hour). Round 1's ingest-side `canonicalizeSource` in `health-export-webhook` is untouched and still valid (it reduces row bloat for future exact-duplicate re-deliveries) — only the DISPLAY-side grouping key changed, from canonical-source+minute to hour-only; `canonicalSourceKey` (the round-1 display-side helper) was removed as dead code since source no longer factors into this grouping at all.
  - **Historical duplicate rows are NOT deleted from `health_metrics`** — every fix here is additive (a grouping/canonicalization step around the existing raw rows), so rows from before either fix keep sitting in the table; they're just no longer double-counted anywhere.
  - **A second, independent copy of this bug exists in `ai-proxy`'s `get_health_stats` tool** (the Ask AI panel / phone `brief`/`sleep`/`ask` actions) — it has its OWN re-aggregation of `health_metrics` (can't reuse the client's `computeDailySeries`), so each round's web-app fix alone left the AI's own answers about steps/energy still wrong until `collapseDuplicateSumPoints` (hand-synced a third time — `ai-proxy` is also a self-contained Deno function) got the identical hour-only fix, applied to the metrics this tool actually sums (`step_count`/`active_energy`/`basal_energy_burned`/`apple_exercise_time`; `heart_rate`/`resting_heart_rate` are averaged/latest and don't need it).
  - **Verified:** `scripts/verify-health-source-dedup.cjs` (6 assertions) and `scripts/verify-ai-health-stats-dedup.cjs` (5 assertions) — both cover the round-1 exact-duplicate case, the round-2 different-minute-different-source case, a full realistic 24-hour day staying sane, boundary-vs-partial precedence in both directions, and genuinely distinct real hours still summing normally.
  - **Any future consumer that re-aggregates `health_metrics`' sum-type metrics outside of `healthAggregate.ts`'s `computeDailySeries`/`computeHourlyBuckets` needs this same hour-level collapse** — check for one before assuming a new surface is safe.
- **ONE day+period selector for the whole tab (`HealthTab`), not per section.** Steps/Energy/Heart/Sleep each used to own their own `useAnchorDate()` + `useState<Period>` and render their own `DateNav`+`PeriodToggle` INSIDE the section body — so changing the day in Steps left Heart on a different day, the control sat at a different scroll depth in every section, and Overview/Body had no day control at all. `HealthTab` now owns that state once, renders the control row **above the section pills**, and passes a `HealthRange` (`{anchor,setAnchor,period,setPeriod}`, `sectionTypes.ts`) down; switching section keeps the day you were looking at. `setAnchor`/`setPeriod` are passed (not just the values) because the chart drill-down writes them — clicking a bar in a Week/Month chart jumps to Day mode on that date. `ActivityRings` and `BodySection` took a `dateStr` prop in the same pass (both hardcoded `todayStr()` and so ignored the control entirely; Body's 90-day window now ENDS at the viewed day). 
- **Date navigation** (`DateNav.tsx` + `dateNav.ts`): every view shows the exact date/range (e.g. "1–7 Jul (this week)") with prev/next arrows and a native date-picker; `rangeForAnchor`/`stepAnchor`/`labelForAnchor` compute the window from an `anchor` (defaults to today, resets on period change, never steps past today). **Stale-anchor bug (real, fixed):** `anchor` was plain `useState(todayStr())` — with the tab open across midnight, "today" moved on but the anchor didn't, so a section silently froze on whatever day it was first opened, and since each section has its own anchor two sections could show two different stale dates (confirmed against live data: fresh same-day rows in the DB, UI days behind). Fixed with `useAnchorDate()`: re-checks on focus/visibility/a 60s interval and advances **only if** the anchor still equals the previously-known "today" — an intentional look-back is left untouched.
- **Chart interaction contract** (one rule, all Health charts): **hover shows values immediately** (no `trigger="click"`); the ONLY things that navigate are explicit links inside the tooltip ("Go to this day →", or "Correct manually" for Sleep) — a bar click never navigates. Tooltips need `wrapperStyle={{ pointerEvents: 'auto' }}` or those links are unclickable. Trend-bar **actions** live in a pinned popover anchored AT the clicked bar (`{date,x,y}` from the Bar's own onClick coords, clamped to the wrapper) — an action strip below the chart was explicitly rejected. Steps/Energy also wire drill-down straight on their own `Bar` (`BarLineChart` exposes `onPointClick`). Steps' ugly default tooltip (`": 514 steps"` — empty series name + colon) uses a custom content renderer like the others.
- **Chart click "frame" (real bug, THREE rounds — root cause found by E2E, generalizable gotcha):** clicking any Health chart drew a black rectangle around it. Rounds 1–2 (focus-outline CSS + `cursor={false}`) were the right ideas but **the CSS never shipped**: the `.recharts-*:focus { outline: none }` rules sat inside `@layer base`, and **Tailwind tree-shakes custom rules inside `@layer` blocks whose selectors never appear in source files** — `.recharts-surface` only exists at runtime inside the library, so the rule was silently purged from every build (verified: zero recharts rules in the served CSS; the focused `svg.recharts-surface` computed `outline: auto 5px`). recharts v3's accessibilityLayer gives that svg `tabIndex=0`, so every click focused it → browser focus ring = the frame. Fix: the rules moved OUTSIDE all `@layer` blocks (bottom of `index.css`). **Rule of thumb: any CSS targeting library-runtime class names must live outside `@layer`.** `cursor={false}` on every Tooltip stays.
- **Canonical chart style** (default for any future Health chart): a translucent `Bar` + a `Line` with dots on top, same colour, via the shared `BarLineChart.tsx`. `rangeKey` (optional min–max band) exists but only `HealthWorkoutDetail`'s HR curve uses it. **Tooltip showed the average twice (real bug):** `BarLineChart` renders a Bar and a Line on the same `dataKey`/`name` (the whole point of the style) and recharts' default Tooltip shows one row per graphical element — fixed with a custom `content` renderer that dedupes by `dataKey`.
- **Mini-metric grid** (`MetricMiniCard`/`MetricMiniGrid`/`miniMetrics.ts`): every HealthKit metric without a full chart gets a small card (max 4 per row: `grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-4`, never more) below its section's main widget — value + Today/7-day-avg/Latest (per the metric's `AggType`) + a one-line explanation. Grouped onto the closest section: Steps (mobility/gait + activity extras + Running Dynamics), Energy (nutrition), Heart (cardio recovery + VO₂ Max), Sleep (respiratory rate, wrist temp), Body (lifestyle & environment). `showTodayCount` adds "N× today"; `showTodayTimes` lists occurrence times (handwashing/toothbrushing). `push_count` ("wheelchair pushes") is very likely misdetected for non-wheelchair users — continuous per-minute fractional samples all day is not what discrete pushes look like; the card points at Watch Settings → Accessibility → Wheelchair, not at our aggregation.
- **`HealthWorkoutDetail.tsx`** — each `health_workouts` row stores the FULL Health-Auto-Export payload in `raw` (jsonb) but only summary columns used to be shown; the per-interval **HR curve** (`heartRateData`), **GPS route** (`route[]`), cadence, distance, avg/max speed, pace, elevation, weather, HR recovery and step count were all ingested then dropped. The detail surfaces all of it from `raw` client-side (no schema change): a `BarLineChart` HR curve using `rangeKey` for the min–max band + a self-drawn SVG route polyline (green start / red end — **no map tiles, CSP blocks external hosts**) + a stat-chip grid. Never-populated cards (gait/nutrition) are unlogged data, not a hardware gap. SpO₂/skin-temp are no longer a hardware gap either as of 20/08/2026 (Watch upgraded SE 2 → Series 11, which has both sensors) — they stay empty until a real Health Auto Export payload confirms the exact field names; don't assume the old "Watch physically can't" reasoning still applies to any card.
- **Health Auto Export operational facts:** importable automation configs live in `docs/health-auto-export/`. Required app settings, confirmed against production data: **Export Version 2** (v1 is legacy — no `id` field, different field shapes), **Summarize ON**, **Time Grouping: Hours**, **all Health Metrics enabled** (not a curated subset), **Date Range "Since Last Sync"** for the recurring automation with a separate weekly "Previous 7 Days" reconciliation automation, **Batch Requests ON** once route data / many metrics / fine grouping are enabled. If a workout arrives without `id`, the webhook falls back to a deterministic key (`name+start+end`) instead of dropping it — still idempotent, just not a real UUID; only a workout with no id AND nothing to build that key from is skipped (counted in `skipped_workouts`). Numeric workout fields (`duration`, `activeEnergyBurned`, `totalEnergy`, `heartRate.avg/min/max`) arrive as either a plain number or a `{qty, units}` object depending on the field — `numOrQty()` unwraps either. Known gap: `stateOfMind`/`medications`/`symptoms`/`cycleTracking`/`heartRateNotifications` payload arrays are not ingested. **ECG** (`HKElectrocardiogramType`) status is more nuanced, researched 20/08/2026 after the user's Watch upgraded SE 2 → Series 11 (which has the ECG electrodes; the SE line never did): Health Auto Export's own docs claim it exports a full record (`{start,end,classification,severity,averageHeartRate,numberOfVoltageMeasurements,voltageMeasurements,samplingFrequency,source}`) rather than only a classification string, but this contradicts a second doc source from the same vendor and was NOT verified against a real export — treat as unconfirmed until one real ECG recording (run manually on the Watch) round-trips through the webhook. If it does arrive, the existing generic metric ingestion needs no code change to store it; a dedicated UI view would. **Auth:** researched across several passes against the vendor's own docs — no HMAC/request-signing exists for this integration; Bearer token over HTTPS is the documented and only supported model, so `Authorization: Bearer <secret>` is correct, not a workaround. Batch-split behaviour (size/count triggers, ordering, partial-failure semantics) is undocumented, so the webhook deliberately depends on none of it: every request is processed independently and idempotently. Platform reminder: HealthKit is unreadable while the phone is locked and background scheduling is opportunistic — delivery is best-effort by OS design, not a bug in either app.
- **Google OAuth token posture (operational, verified hands-on 21/07/2026):** the consent screen in **Testing = 7-day refresh token; Production (even unverified) = effectively non-expiring** — publishing to Production is mandatory for background sync. **A re-consent can omit a fresh `refresh_token` unless `prompt=consent` is forced** — so a scope-union re-consent can leave the UI reading "connected" while the stored token still covers only the old scopes; verify with Google's `tokeninfo` endpoint after any scope change. Fallback if the posture ever fails: an **Internal** Workspace app (no cap, no verification, no 7-day limit) at the cost of employer-tied identity.

### AI (ai-proxy) Detail

Gemini via the `ai-proxy` edge function. Three call modes: the conversational **tool-calling loop** (default), **`responseSchema`** → `callGeminiStructured` (single-shot, `responseMimeType: 'application/json'` + a Gemini-format schema, returns `{ data }`, no tools/no loop — structured extraction, not chat), and **`{ fetchUrl }`** (server-side page fetch because the browser can't hit arbitrary origins under CORS; SSRF guard blocks localhost/private ranges, 8s timeout).

**Generic DB tool layer** over a curated `DB_CATALOG` allow-list: `describe_database`, `db_query`, `db_insert`, `db_update`, `db_delete`, **`db_aggregate`**. Guardrails: default-deny allow-list (token/secret/auth tables unreachable), `access: 'rw'|'ro'` per table (hevy_*/strava_activities/health_metrics/health_workouts/movies/tv_series are read-only — synced externally), **every op force-scoped to `user_id` server-side** (the model never supplies it), no DDL, update/delete refuse empty filters, 16-turn loop cap. `app_error_logs` is readable so the AI can help diagnose failures. `pt_assessments`/`work_notes`/`work_weekly_goals`/`work_pinned_links` are read-only; **`dev_requests` and `wish_items` are read-write on purpose** — the user dictates backlog notes and wishes in chat (and by Siri) and the AI writing a structured row there IS the intended flow; `wish_items`' catalog `rules` carry the never-a-deadline guardrail and the four-way tie-break against `dev_requests`/`ai_memory`/`shop_items` (see Wishes). `day_targets` is `rw` too (singleton, confirm before writing, set `phase_start_date` on a phase change). **Behavioural guardrails in the system prompt:** announce what it's about to do before any create/update/delete, and require **explicit user confirmation before ANY delete** (never auto-deletes). `DB_CATALOG` stays behind `describe_database` (on-demand, not in the prompt). **Gap:** `water_log_entries` (migration 067) is NOT in the catalog — the AI cannot see water logs.

- **`db_aggregate`** — `sum|avg|min|max|count` + `group_by`; enum'd ops only (never expressions), the same filter grammar as `db_query`, group columns validated against the catalog, hard cap ≤366 groups, rows reduced server-side so only the summary reaches the model. This is what makes "no pre-written query per scenario" true for analytics. **Tool-choice ladder** in the system prompt: current value/list → `db_query` · average/trend/compare → `db_aggregate` · whole-day picture → `get_day_summary` · "that thing I saved" → `semantic_search` then `db_query` by id · writes → `db_insert/update/delete` with the confirm rules · external world → the special tools.
- **`semantic_search`** (migration `065`: pgvector + `ai_embeddings` `vector(768)` hnsw cosine + the `ai_semantic_search()` SECURITY-INVOKER RPC) — `gemini-embedding-001` @768, **cosine index → no manual normalize**, vector `limit ≤ 8`, `user_id` filtered inside the vector query; returns `{source_table, source_id, snippet, score}` and the model then `db_query`s the live row by id. **Embeddings locate, live SQL answers — never use a similarity hit as the factual value of anything current-state.** Reindex is user-triggered (Developer → "Reindex AI search"), not embed-on-write. Caveat: the embedding request shape was confirmed from docs, **not a live call**.
- **`run_read_query`** — the opt-in read-only live-SQL escape hatch. **Its security boundary is the DATABASE, not the edge regex:** ai-proxy's normal ops run under the service role (RLS bypassed), so this path instead builds a **user-JWT client** and calls a **SECURITY INVOKER** RPC (`ai_run_read_query`) that runs as `authenticated` (RLS applies), sets `transaction_read_only = on` + a 5s `statement_timeout`, and wraps in `LIMIT 500` before `jsonb_agg`. Containment = RLS + the read-only transaction + migration 044's revoked OAuth-token-table grants + the hard row cap. The edge guard is a best-effort UX filter that **fails closed** (strip comments first, reject quoted identifiers, `SELECT`/`WITH` only, single statement, block `pg_`/`information_schema`/other schemas, check every `FROM`/`JOIN` target incl. comma-joined lists against the catalog, recognise CTEs only as `name AS (`). **The broad DML-keyword regex was deliberately REMOVED — do not re-add it**: it false-rejected reads containing "update" inside a string literal; write-prevention is the read-only transaction's job.
- **Special-purpose tools** generic CRUD can't cover: `get_calendar_events` · **transit** — `plan_trip`, `get_next_transit`, `get_saved_transit`, `search_transit_stops`, `save_transit_stop`, `save_transit_route` · **media** — `get_media`, `plan_media`, `mark_episode_watched` · **health** — `get_health_stats` · **shop** — `get_shop_categories`, `create_shop_category`, `create_shop_item`, `ask_clarifying_question` · **recipes** — `get_recipes`, `create_recipe` · **training** — `update_hevy_routine`, `create_hevy_routine` (both forward to `hevy-api`), `get_athlete_profile` (see Training's athlete-profile note) · `get_day_summary` · `save_memory` (+ rw `ai_memory`: durable notes the user asks the AI to remember, recalled via `db_query`).
- **`ai_memory` — an "ABOUT ME" context block + a management surface.** Two gaps closed together on user request. (1) `aiApi.buildContext` now appends one more query (last in the array, so no existing `get<T>(i)` index shifts) reading `kind IN ('fact','preference')` only — deliberately **not** `note`/`summary`, which stay on-demand-only via `db_query`/`semantic_search` so they never bloat every single turn's context — capped at 12, newest first, rendered as an `ABOUT ME` block right after `DATE`/`TIME` (before `TODAY'S TASKS`): durable identity facts colour how everything else should be read, so they go first. (2) `save_memory`'s tool description now explicitly covers TWO distinct triggers, not one: remembering a single fact/preference (unchanged), and an explicit **"summarize this conversation"** request ("topla ve not al", "bunu özetle", "summarize this conversation", …) — compact the WHOLE conversation so far into one `kind="summary"` call, not just the last message. (3) A new Developer → **Memory** tab (`MemoryTab.tsx`/`MemoryRow.tsx`/`MemoryEditSheet.tsx`, `src/features/ai/api/memoryApi.ts`) lists/edits/deletes every row and is the only path that ever writes `source='user'` (every other row is `'ai'`, from `save_memory`, or `'auto'`) — before this, `ai_memory` had no UI at all: nothing to see what was remembered, nothing to correct a wrong entry. (4) `AIPanel` gained a Summarize header button, gated on `messages.length > 0`, that sends a normal chat message ("Summarize this conversation and save it to memory." / the Turkish equivalent, following the existing voice-chat TR/EN chip) down the same `handleSend` path as any other turn — the model's `save_memory` call still lands in the transcript like any other tool use, nothing hidden.
- **`get_health_stats` — two fixes worth remembering.** (1) It and the catalog both pointed at `health_daily_stats`, a table dropped when migration 041 moved Health to point-in-time grain, so the AI's health answers were silently broken (empty/stale) ever since; rewritten to query `health_metrics` directly and aggregate with the same rules as `healthAggregate.ts` (sum steps/energy, min/max/avg heart rate, latest resting HR, kJ→kcal). (2) **It now returns SLEEP too** — `computeSleepNights` (absolute-time overlap-merge keep-longest, Oslo wake-day attribution, per-night hours + deep/core/rem/awake, last night + period average). That closed the "no sleep data" answers in the phone `sleep` action and the Coach. The prompt tells the AI to *analyze* health data (trends, comparisons, honest criticism), not recite numbers.
- **Latency design for `plan_trip` (was a real 3–4 min pain point).** It is a ONE-SHOT call: the prompt and tool description tell the model to call it directly with the user's own words (`from:"ev", to:"iş"`) and NOT to look things up first — earlier "investigate first" wording caused ~5 sequential Gemini turns (each a full round-trip) vs ~2 now. `plan_trip` resolves both endpoints itself against saved stops/routes loaded ONCE (not re-queried per endpoint), in parallel: empty → default saved stop; an exact `NSR:StopPlace:` id → used as-is (name backfilled from the trip legs, no extra lookup); "home"/"ev"/"work"/"iş" (+variants) → saved-label fuzzy match; else → geocoder top match. When a side won't resolve it returns `{success:false, needs_clarification:true, candidates:[…]}` so the model asks ONE `ask_clarifying_question` instead of firing more search turns. **Only a regex-validated `NSR:StopPlace:` id or finite coords are ever interpolated into GraphQL** (injection safety). Anti-hallucination is structural (report only what the tool returns; call once and report), not extra verification turns. New EnTur GraphQL fields are deliberately NOT added speculatively — verify live first.
- **Model chain + retries.** Gemini returns transient "model overloaded" 5xx that clear within seconds, distinct from a 429 (real quota — surfaces immediately via `throwRateLimit`). Overload waves hit different models' capacity pools unevenly, so retrying a DIFFERENT model beats retrying the same one. `fetchGeminiWithFallback` tries `MODEL_CHAIN` = `gemini-3.5-flash` → `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` → `gemini-2.5-flash` → `gemini-2.5-flash-lite` with jittered exponential backoff, position-weighted attempts (`RETRIES_BY_POSITION` [4,3,2,2]) so better models are tried hard before falling to lite tiers. **Every model id must be live-verified before entering this list** (real bug: two guessed ids 404'd in production; a 404 also used to abort the whole chain — it now skips to the next model). Probe with ai-proxy's `{listModels:true}` debug branch, which returns the key's REAL available models. The client can pass a `model` — it becomes the chain's **preferred starting point**, not an exclusive choice (`AIPanel`'s header picker, localStorage `lasci-ai-model`, "Auto" by default); `AIResponse.model` reports which model actually answered.
- **`thinking_config` per-model body (real bug, round 3):** `thinking_config`/`thinking_level` is a `generationConfig` field that is **Gemini-3.x-only** — explicitly picking `gemini-2.5-flash` 400'd with "Thinking level is not supported for this model" the moment a real user tried it, because ONE fixed request body was built once and reused across every model in the chain. `fetchGeminiWithFallback`'s `body` param became **`buildBody: (model) => AnyRecord`** — the body is constructed PER MODEL inside the retry loop, and `supportsThinking(model)` (`model.startsWith('gemini-3')`) gates whether `thinking_config` is included. Applies to all three call sites (the tool loop, the turn-limit summary, `callGeminiStructured`). `thinking_level: MINIMAL` in the loop — thinking tokens are not the cost leak.
- **Prompt-caching contract (don't regress it).** Live context used to be baked into `systemInstruction`, which made Gemini's implicit prefix cache (~90% off a repeated byte-identical prefix) structurally unable to hit — a ~7,270-token fixed block (system + serialized TOOLS) was billed at full price on every turn of every tool loop. Now: **`SYSTEM_PROMPT` and the per-surface tool declarations must stay byte-identical per call** — never interpolate a date, timestamp or any volatile value into them (instant cache-buster; the current date belongs in the context turn), and serialize tools deterministically. Live context rides as `contents[0]` (`prependContext` in `aiApi.ts`). `buildContext` is skipped for clearly transit-only questions (a conservative client-side `TRANSIT_RE` gate). **`ai_usage_log.cached_tokens`** (← `usageMetadata.cachedContentTokenCount`) is the proof metric and the documented trigger for escalating to explicit `cachedContent`. `ai_query_log` records every `db_query` + live SQL with the exact statement — the substrate for later promoting hot queries into the UI.
- **Tool slicing + model routing.** Slicing is deliberately narrow: **only the bounded `shop` surface is sliced, and the `phone` surface gets NO tools at all** (its brief context is pre-built server-side by `phone-gateway`, so a single-shot answer is the biggest latency win) — **coach and general keep the FULL tool set** so the assistant is never weakened. `SURFACE_MODEL` changes only the **starting** model: `shop: 'gemini-2.5-flash'`, `phone: 'gemini-3.1-flash-lite'` (skips the frequently 503-overloaded 3.5-flash primary, which burned ~7–13s of retry budget before falling through — fatal against the ~25s iOS Shortcuts cutoff). A 503 still falls through the whole chain.
- **Voice chat (`useVoiceChat.ts` + AIPanel):** browser-native Web Speech API — `SpeechRecognition` (prefixed `webkitSpeechRecognition` everywhere that has it) for speech→text and `speechSynthesis` for text→speech. **No key, no edge function, no cost** — nothing server-side changed. Two levels: the mic button dictates into the box (edit before sending), and the header **Voice** toggle is hands-free mode — utterance auto-sends, the reply is spoken, and the mic reopens when it finishes (`speak(text, onDone)` → `startListening()`), so it's a real back-and-forth loop. TR/EN language chip (`lasci-ai-voice-lang`) drives BOTH recognition and the picked voice. Load-bearing details: `speakableText()` strips `**bold**`/emoji so the voice doesn't read markup; `startListening` cancels any speech first (otherwise it transcribes its own voice); **`primeAudio()` must run inside the enabling tap** — iOS refuses synthesis that no user gesture unlocked, and the reply is spoken much later; the panel's close paths reset voice mode and `voiceModeRef` is `voiceMode && isAIOpen` so the loop can't continue behind a closed panel; a silent utterance ends the loop rather than reopening a hot mic. Everything is feature-detected — the controls simply don't render where the API is missing. **Recognition is unreliable inside an iOS home-screen PWA** (a long-standing WebKit gap); on the phone the `AI'a Sor` Shortcut is the voice path (Siri dictation → gateway → Speak Text), so this mainly serves desktop.
- **Vision:** `Message.images` end-to-end via a shared `messagesToContents()` that attaches base64 photos as Gemini `inline_data` parts (chat + structured). `AIPanel` supports the attach-photo button (≤3, thumbnails, image-only send) **and pasted images** (Cmd/Ctrl+V on desktop, paste on mobile — `onPaste`); `parseFoodPhoto()` + `fileToCompactDataUrl` downscale before upload.
- **MCP was evaluated and deliberately not pursued:** GitHub Pages is a static host and cannot run a persistent process, so any MCP layer would need a separate always-on server. The generic `DB_CATALOG` + `db_query/insert/update/delete/aggregate` layer shipped instead, and the "AI proposes, user confirms" invariant lives in the system prompt.
- **Removed / never built:** dead `log_workout`/`get_workouts` (hit the dropped `training_sessions` table — training is Hevy/Strava, read via `db_query`; planned sessions = `time_blocks` `category='training'`). The **Weekly Review** Home card added during the AI phases work was **removed on request** — `weeklyReviewApi`/`useWeeklyReview`/`WeeklyReview` no longer exist in `src/`, and `ai_reviews` (migration 065) is an unused empty table left in place.
- Cost model, security invariants, the architectures evaluated and the pricing snapshot: `docs/ai-cost-capability-analysis.md`.

### iPhone surface (Shortcuts · Scriptable · Web Push)
