**`phone-gateway`** is the single durable entry point (see the Edge Functions table for auth). **12 actions:** deterministic `log_supplement` · `log_food` · `log_water` · `nutrition_today` (includes `water_ml`) · `recent_foods` · `search_library` · `sleep_stats` · `tasks_today` · `import_body_composition`; AI `ask` · `brief` · `sleep` (forwarded to `ai-proxy` with the same secret). The action table in `docs/iphone-examples.md` is **the contract** — Shortcuts and widgets must match it.

**`import_body_composition` (migration `085`, new `body_composition_reports` table)** — a smart-scale "Body composition analysis report" photo is shared to an Apple Shortcut, which OCRs the fixed report layout **on-device** (Apple's built-in OCR, no LLM anywhere in this path) and POSTs the 14 extracted numbers here. Deliberately narrow: only the 14 measured numbers + `measured_at` — no name/gender/reference-ranges/segmental-left-right breakdown (those are either PII, derivable, or the device's own classification judgment, none of which this table stores), no original-image storage, and it never touches `hevy_body_measurements`/`health_metrics` (a third, separate body-composition source — never merged or joined with those).
- **Timezone, never a hardcoded offset.** The report prints a local wall-clock time with no UTC offset of its own; the gateway resolves it to a real instant via a DST-safe two-pass IANA-offset technique (`zonedWallTimeToUtcMs`/`parseNaiveLocalDateTime` in `phone-gateway/index.ts`) using an optional `measurement_timezone` request field (defaults to `Europe/Oslo`, the only zone in play today). `measured_at` in the DB is always the real reading instant, never the upload time.
- **Validation is numeric sanity, never the device's own classification.** Every field is checked for presence/finiteness/a generous sane range, plus two cross-field consistency checks the report's own rounding has to satisfy: `body_fat_mass_kg ≈ weight_kg × body_fat_percent / 100` and `lean_body_mass_kg ≈ weight_kg − body_fat_mass_kg` (tolerance: the larger of 0.15 kg or 0.5% of `weight_kg`, sized to the report's 1-decimal rounding). A validation failure writes nothing and returns a field-keyed `validation_error`.
- **Dedupe is DB-enforced, not just app logic.** A unique index on `(user_id, source, measured_at)` is the real guarantee; the gateway handler selects first so it can answer cleanly (`already_exists` when a re-sent report matches byte-for-byte within a tight re-send tolerance, `conflict` — never a silent overwrite — when the same `measured_at` carries different numbers) and separately catches the Postgres `23505` race for a genuinely concurrent duplicate submission, resolving it the same way instead of surfacing a raw DB error.
- **`source` is a CHECK-constrained text column, currently only `'movinglife_report'`** (AGENTS.md rule 7: CHECK over enum, trivially extendable later) — set server-side, never sent by the client.
- Rows are **immutable** — the gateway never updates one, only inserts or refuses; no `updated_at` column exists because none is ever mutated. `body_composition_reports` is a user-authored table (`trg_audit` attached) and is `ro` in `ai-proxy`'s `DB_CATALOG` (readable for trend questions alongside `hevy_body_measurements`/`health_metrics`; not `rw` — the AI never bypasses the gateway's validation by inserting directly).
- **Codex's job (C6 on the board, `docs/codex-shortcuts.md`) is only the last step** of an already-working OCR Shortcut — POST the extracted numbers here and show a result notification; the OCR/extraction logic itself is explicitly out of scope for that task. Full request/response contract with real examples: `docs/iphone-examples.md`'s `import_body_composition` section.

**The ~25s design constraint (why the AI actions look the way they do):** iOS Shortcuts' "Get Contents of URL" times out at roughly 25s, which is short for a tool-looping LLM. Three deliberate choices, all in `phone-gateway`/`ai-proxy`:
1. **`FAST_MODEL = 'gemini-3.1-flash-lite'`** starts every AI action, skipping the frequently 503-overloaded 3.5-flash primary; ai-proxy still falls through the rest of the chain.
2. **Single-shot, not a tool loop** — `brief` pre-builds its context SERVER-SIDE (`buildBriefContext`: today's tasks + schedule + upcoming training, service-role queries explicitly user-scoped, best-effort per section) and sends `surface: 'phone'`, which `toolsFor()` maps to **no tools**, so Gemini answers in one turn. Gemini rejects an empty tools array, so the key is dropped from the body entirely.
3. **A 22s `AbortController`** on the ai-proxy call returns a clean error BEFORE the client cutoff instead of a silent hang.

**Apple Shortcuts generator** — `scripts/iphone-shortcuts/` (**Codex-owned, do not edit**): `generate.mjs` emits 11 shortcuts — `Log Creatine`, `Su İç`, `AI'a Sor`, `Sabah Brief`, `Beslenme Durumu`, `Uyku Özeti`, `Uyku İstatistikleri`, `Bugünün Taskları`, `Barkod Tara`, `Atıştırmalık Logla`, `Akşam Yemeği Logla`. Rules: the device secret is written as the `__PHONE_GATEWAY_SECRET__` placeholder — **never commit a signed `.plist`/`.shortcut` containing the real secret**. Cross-app contract: **`Barkod Tara` scans with iOS's built-in barcode action, then opens `scriptable:///run/Yemek%20Logla?ean=<code>`** — so the Scriptable script MUST be named exactly `Yemek Logla` and read `args.queryParameters.ean`. Spec + task board: `docs/codex-shortcuts.md`.

**Scriptable** — `docs/scriptable-food-logger.md` (the `Yemek Logla` mini app: recents / own library / Open Food Facts / scanned barcode) and `docs/scriptable-widgets.md` (4 home-screen widgets — `Uyku Paneli`, `Komuta Merkezi`, `Hizli Log Paneli`, `Makro Halkalari` — plus the `HizliLog` runner). **Script names are load-bearing**: deep links resolve by name.

**Web Push (lock-screen morning brief)** — migration `068` creates `push_subscriptions` + the pg_cron job `lascis-morning-push` (`'0 7 * * *'` = 07:00 UTC ≈ 09:00 Oslo in summer / 08:00 in winter), which calls `push-send` via `net.http_post` with `x-cron-secret` read from Vault inside the cron (never in the SQL text). Client: `usePushNotifications` + a toggle in Settings (⚙) → Notifications. The SW push/notificationclick handlers live in **`public/push-sw.js`**, pulled into the Workbox service worker via `vite.config`'s `workbox.importScripts` — a separate file so the generated SW isn't hand-edited. Setup runbook: `docs/web-push-setup.md`. See Platform & Shell for the hard iOS Web Push constraints (installed PWA only, visible notification required).

## Not done yet

The **live backlog is the in-app Dev Requests drawer** (`dev_requests`). This list is only the coarse long-term items that outlive a single session.

- **Kobo Clara BW integration (planned, nothing built)** — reading tracker via KOReader + our own plugin → a `kobo-sync` edge function, "Send to Kobo" from phone/laptop (OPDS feed, later zero-tap), highlights/vocabulary and fun extras. The whole plan, roadmap (Phases 0–6), owner checklist and open decisions: `docs/kobo/PLAN.md`. Firmware rule: never accept a Kobo 5.x/6.x opt-in update.
- **Football data source** — no route, and the `football-api` edge function is no longer in the repo. Calendar-based fixtures are the intended path.
- 🔶 **Exercise demo GIFs** — optional true self-host: mirror ExerciseGymGifsDB into Supabase Storage and swap the single `GIF_SOURCE` constant (still the public jsDelivr CDN today). The manual-override gap is closed (migration `082`, `exercise_gif_overrides` — see the Training Feature Detail section).
- 🔶 **Wishes — deliberately NOT built, don't rebuild it:** no `wish_periods`/`contexts` grouping table · no recurring MM-DD windows, wrap-around resolver or `lead_days` (periods are concrete dates) · no seasonal reset and no annual review prompt · no `lat`/`lon`, map, geofence or proximity trigger (CSP blocks map tiles, the only wired geocoder is EnTur/Norway-only, and `useGeolocation` returns the Oslo fallback as a *success* — a denial would read as "he is in Oslo") · no SQL view and no generated column · no new AI tool, `phone-gateway` action or Shortcut · no 7th glance-board cell and no 5th Daily mode tab (that control is `grid grid-cols-4`). Note that the `windowChips` season chips are the **only** practical way to capture a period — `DateInput` has no calendar popup, so a typed range costs 16 digit taps against a chip's one; don't remove them in favour of date boxes.
- 🔶 **AI media coverage** — the media tools ship (`get_media`/`plan_media`/`mark_episode_watched`, and the catalog documents `rating`, `personal_note`, `status`, `current_season/current_episode`), but: `genres (jsonb)` is absent from the catalog columns; `get_media` returns only `entry_id/title/status(+S/E)` (no rating, genres or dates); and `aiApi.ts`'s `SYSTEM_PROMPT` has **no MEDIA persona block** (just one tool mention) unlike HEALTH/TRAINING/NUTRITION. Also missing from the prompt: "Coming soon is date-derived, not a status".
- **Activity Log / stats widget** (user-facing feed, if ever built) — it must be **immutable append-only**: SELECT + INSERT policies only, and the **absence** of UPDATE/DELETE policies is the enforcement mechanism. Sketched event vocabulary: `task_created`, `task_updated`, `task_completed`, `task_cancelled`, `media_added`, `media_status_changed`, `media_planned`, `media_completed`, `media_dropped`, `ai_action_proposed`, `ai_action_confirmed`, `ai_action_cancelled`, `calendar_event_synced`. **`audit_logs` (migration 037) already covers the CRUD audit trail — don't rebuild that**; this would be a user-facing feed, not an audit table.
- **Kassalapp** — confirm the Norwegian `display_name`/`code` values and the per-100g basis against ONE live sample (see Food).

## Coding Rules (Strict)

### Mobile-first — MANDATORY
Every component mobile-first. `min-h-[44px]` on every interactive element. No exceptions.

```tsx
// ✅
<button className="w-full md:w-auto min-h-[44px]">
// ❌
<button className="w-32 h-8">
```

Hover-only actions need an always-visible mobile fallback. No horizontal page overflow.

Popups: never hand-roll a Headless UI `Dialog` — use `ModalShell` (see Theme & popups below; phone bottom sheet with drag-to-close, centered dialog from `sm:`, Back/Esc aware).

### Theme & popups — MANDATORY (full rules: `docs/design/THEME.md`)
- **Token classes only** (`bg-surface`, `text-fg-muted`, `border-line`, `bg-accent-500 text-on-accent`…) — no hex, `rgb()`, `gray-*`/`amber-*`/`red-500`, `bg-white` or `text-[13px]`-style sizes in new code. Build from `src/shared/ui` primitives (`Card`, `CardHeader`, `Button`, `IconButton`, `PageContainer`/`PageHeader`, `StatTile`, `SegmentedControl`, `EmptyState`, `Skeleton`, `ListRow`); icons are `lucide-react`, emoji only as content.
- **Status = a tone, never the accent** (`<ToneDot>`/`<TonePill>`/`data-tone`, one `*_TONE` map next to each enum). Charts take colours from `useChartColors()`; identity colours (brands, sleep stages, transit lines) stay named literals.
- **Every popup renders `ModalShell`** (local sub-steps: `<ModalShell>`/`Sheet`; drawers: `SideDrawer`). **An entity popup opens with `useEntityModal().open({ kind, id | defaults })`** — requests carry ids and prefill, never rows; the adapter reloads by id and writes through the feature's hooks (no `supabase` in a popup). Adding a kind = `shared/modals/types.ts` + `registry.ts` + an adapter. Never `window.confirm`/`alert` — `await entityModal.confirm({ title })` (or `useEntityModal().confirm`).
- **Data: UI → hook → api.** Components never import `supabase`. Query keys only from `qk` (`src/shared/query/keys.ts`), stale times only from `STALE`, invalidation through named groups (`invalidate(qc, 'taskGraph')`, groups in `shared/query/invalidate.ts`). One action = one hook, shared by every screen that performs it.
- **Writes use `useMutationWithFeedback`** with `invalidates` (and `loadingMessage`/`successMessage` when wanted); callers never toast a mutation's result themselves (see Toast feedback).

### Toast feedback — MANDATORY
Every async action must show feedback on completion. Toasts sit above the tab bar on phones and bottom-left (right of the sidebar) elsewhere.
🟢 success · 🔴 error · 🟡 warning · ⚫ loading.

**Every TanStack Query mutation uses `useMutationWithFeedback`**
(`src/shared/hooks/useMutationWithFeedback.ts`) — there is no bare `useMutation` left in `src/`:
```ts
return useMutationWithFeedback({
  action:         'delete_time_block',   // logError context (required)
  mutationFn:     (id: string) => deleteTimeBlock(id),
  successMessage: 'Deleted',             // optional — omit for silent-success on in-place edits
  loadingMessage: 'Deleting…',           // optional — for slow writes
  invalidates:    ['schedule'],          // invalidation groups or explicit qk keys, run on settle
})
```
This exists because "every async action must show feedback" was hand-copied per call site —
inconsistently applied and easy to forget (real bugs: the daily timeline's drag/postpone/rename/
delete and the to-do list's toggle/delete/reorder had **zero** feedback on failure). The primitive
bakes the guarantee in: error is **always** toasted and logged to `app_error_logs`; success is
silent by default ("edits feel live") unless `successMessage` is given.

**Callers never wrap `mutateAsync` in their own `toast.error`** (it double-toasts). Use
`try { await m.mutateAsync(v) } catch { return }` only to stop a multi-step flow, or
`withProgress(() => m.mutateAsync(v), { loading, success })` (same file) for per-call copy.

For genuinely one-off async actions that aren't a TanStack mutation at all (a button
handler calling an API directly), the manual pattern is still fine:
```ts
const tid = toast.loading('Saving…')
try {
  await doSomething()
  toast.dismiss(tid); toast.success('Saved ✓')
} catch (err) {
  toast.dismiss(tid); toast.error((err as Error).message ?? 'Failed')
}
```

### Reference viewport sizes (design/testing baseline)
Get the current size in the browser console with `window.innerWidth + 'x' + window.innerHeight`:
- **Mobile**: 852×393 · **Laptop**: 1469×680 · **Monitor**: 2450×1130

### Layout width — elements content-sized; pages fill width with columns (MANDATORY)
Cards keep their own width caps; a page uses a wide screen by ADDING columns, never by stretching cards. Pages use `PageBoard` (`src/shared/ui/PageBoard.tsx`, rules in `pageBoardRules.ts`).

- Page wrapper: `PageContainer` (`px-4 sm:px-6 lg:px-8`, left-aligned, `@container/page`). Default cap `max-w-[135rem]` = the widest board; beyond it the page stays left-aligned. `narrow` for forms, `full` only for a page with its own full-width layout. Page background `bg-canvas`, cards `bg-surface`.
- **W1 · Container-relative, not viewport.** Anything placed by PageBoard sizes its inner grids and rings on its own width (`@container` + `@[..rem]:`) or the page's (`@[61rem]/page:`), never viewport `sm:/lg:` — a 24rem side track at 2450 is narrower than a phone-landscape viewport.
- **W2 · Column flow.** Collections flow into content-width columns: `grid-cols-[repeat(auto-fill,minmax(MIN,MAX))]` + `justify-start`; caps: dense rows `15–18rem`, cards `19–22rem`, large cards `24–28rem`; a single content row never exceeds **40rem**. In a wide track use `minmax(MIN,1fr)` (a fixed maximum makes auto-fill count columns by the maximum and leaves a column's worth empty). A grid that gains columns by steps declares its base track (`grid-cols-1`) — an implicit `auto` track grows to a long title's min-content and a card's `overflow-hidden` hides it from the audit. Items of varying height (phases, library groups) never use a row grid: deal them into column stacks by index (`dealByIndex` in `projectBoard.ts`, `PhaseColumns`) or wrap content-sized blocks. Alphabetical multi-column lists use CSS `columns-*` with `break-inside-avoid` rows so the order reads down each column. (Exception: `TodaySummary` uses explicit column steps — positional stability outranks column flow there.)
- **W3 · Controls are content-sized.** Text/search inputs `≤ max-w-md`; info banners `w-fit`; a one-line control is never full-width.
- **W4 · Charts: aspect-locked + capped** by role (small trend `max-w-2xl`, primary `max-w-4xl` = 896px).
- **W5 · Detail on demand.** Secondary info lives in a hover/tap peek (desktop hover + mobile tap).
- **W6 · Measurable.** At 393 / 1469 / 2450: no horizontal overflow; no single-line content > 640px, no auto-fill column > 384px, no text input > 512px, no chart svg > 896px; and at 1469 and 2450 the default view leaves at most one side track (24rem) empty. A page with fewer sections than columns documents where it stops. Check with `node scripts/audit-page-width.mjs --harness <dir>` (`--glance` checks Daily's glance cells full vs empty day).
- **W7 · PageBoard.** One main track (≤ 56rem, a layout may lower it) + 0–3 fixed 24rem side tracks, 1rem gaps. The step is measured on the page's OWN width, never the viewport: < 60rem one stack (phones, tablets) · 60–100rem main + 1 side (1280/1469 laptops) · 100–128rem main + 2 (1920) · ≥ 128rem main + 3 (2450). Each step declares which section goes in which column (`{ top, columns: [[…], { stack, span, sticky }], bottom, main, lead }`); columns are stacks (no masonry, no dense packing), so positions never depend on content. A section that moves column at a step change remounts (desktop resize only). Rules:
  - `lead: n` puts side tracks before main: a rail that drives or sums up the main column (a calendar, a day's totals, an add form) reads first on the left.
  - A section that can be empty goes in the last track, so an empty track is only ever at the far right. A card whose content is bounded (≤ 2 metrics, a few short rows) never spans; the window stops early with empty trailing tracks, documented in its board file (Cardio, Coach, Goal at 2450).
  - A band (`top`/`bottom`) spans only the tracks its own step declares, so a full-width band is declared at every step. Never put a collection in a `bottom` band under a much taller column (it starts after the tallest column ends); give it a column.
  - A section that is its host card's block on a phone is **absent** from step 1; the host decides with `keysAt(layout, useBoardStep())` (Health Sleep breathing, Cardio recovery, the Goal blocks).
  - A `sticky` column pins only while it fits the scroll area (`StickyStack` re-checks on resize). A page header above a board that stops short caps itself to `boardWidthRem(layout, step)`; `useElementWidthRem` (`src/shared/hooks/useElementWidth.ts`) measures a component's own width in rem.
  - Layouts live in `homeBoard.ts`, `dailyBoards.ts`, `foodBoards.ts`, `wishesBoard.ts`, `healthBoards.ts`, `trainingBoards.ts`, `mediaBoard.ts`, `workBoard.ts`, `projectBoard.ts`, `developerBoards.ts`, checked by `scripts/verify-page-board.cjs` (Home, Daily), `verify-life-boards.cjs` (Food, Wishes, Health, Training) and `verify-feature-boards.cjs` (Media, Work, Projects, Developer).
- **Density toggle was tried and REJECTED** (Comfortable/Compact/Dense) — don't reintroduce it.

### `NEVER_HIDES` — a new surface adds rows, never subtracts them (MANDATORY)
**The user's explicit decision, and the reason Wishes is its own table.** No wish/period/window/`kind` predicate may be added to an existing task query — not `fetchTasksForDay`, not `fetchAllTasks`, not `fetchTasksByWeek`, not `useDailyBrief`/`briefRules` or `buildBriefContext`, not `phone-gateway`'s `tasks_today`, not `push-send`'s task query. New surfaces (the Wishes page, the `DayView` resurfacing row, the morning-push segment, `TasksPanel`'s "Open now") **only ADD**. In his words: a season "is only the app's note about when to bring it up by itself; it is never a rule about when you are allowed to see it… The only thing a season changes is when the app speaks first." A row written in July stays readable, searchable and askable-about in July.

The **one** sanctioned exception is the `fetchTasksForDay` `dueFilter` fix (Daily → the `section` day-leak): it removes duplicate *wrong renderings* of a row, never the row.

### Connections live in ONE place — never on a feature page (MANDATORY)
Every external integration's **connect / disconnect / connection status** lives in
**Settings → Subscriptions** (`/settings?tab=subscriptions` — the old `?tab=integrations` still works — renders `ConnectionsTab.tsx`; `/developer?tab=connections` redirects there). Each card also lists its subscriptions (`service_subscriptions`, migration `115`: price, cycle, renews_on, account, `requirement` `required` = the integration needs it / `info` = just FYI; pure rules in `settings/subscriptionRules.ts`, `verify-subscriptions`). Subscription prices carry a currency (NOK/TRY/USD/EUR) and show the other three small beneath, converted with the Home currency query (`useSubscriptionRates` reuses `useCurrencyRates`, no extra calls); the monthly total converts into one chosen currency. **Settings → Integrations and APIs** (`?tab=apis`, `apiRegistry.ts`, `ApisTab`, `verify-api-registry`) is a static registry of every external API/service: docs link, how connected, auth + secret names, direction, cadence, where used, since, limits, caveats. Settings also holds **Places** (Home/Work = saved transit stops labelled Home/Work, `transitPlaces.findPlace`; Transit's To home / To work buttons use them) and **Appearance** incl. **Display size** 80–125 % (root font-size from `useThemeStore`, stamped in `index.html`; 90 % gives a 100 %-zoom laptop the next wider PageBoard step). Older text here saying "Developer → Connections" means this page, and nowhere else. A feature page shows
that integration's DATA; it may LINK to `/developer?tab=connections`, but it must never carry
its own connect button, disconnect button, or status widget.

Before this, the same job was scattered across three unrelated surfaces — Google in the ⚙
Settings menu, Strava inside the Training tab, PlayStation inside the Games tab — so "is X
connected?" had no single answer and reconnecting meant remembering which page happened to own
it. Moved on explicit user request.

**One narrow exception (2026-09-25, user's own call): RE-authenticating an
already-configured integration may also be offered at the point of failure.**
When PlayStation's stored session expires, the npsso paste form renders inside
the Games tab's expired panel as well as in Connections. This serves the rule's
purpose rather than defeating it — the rule exists so "is X connected?" has one
answer and reconnecting isn't a hunt across pages; recovery in place is not a
second status widget. It is bounded: the form is ONE shared component
(`PsnNpssoForm`), and first-time **connect** and **disconnect** stay in
Connections only. Extend this to another integration only if its credential can
also be revoked by the provider behind our back.

**Second narrow extension (owner request 2026-09-25):** the Games page's ⋯ Library
menu shows PSN/Steam status — incl. the npsso
countdown — and offers PSN renew, via the shared `PsnNpssoForm` and the
existing `usePlayStation`/`useSteam` hooks (`TgConnections`, fetched only while
the menu/sheet is open). First-time connect and disconnect stay in Developer →
Connections only.

`ConnectionCard`'s `Status` therefore has a fourth value, **`expired`**: a
credential IS stored, so "Not connected" would be wrong, but the provider no
longer honours it, so "Connected" is a lie. Before this, PlayStation's card
read "Connected" off the mere existence of a `psn_tokens` row and offered
nothing but Disconnect — the one action actually needed (paste a fresh token)
was unreachable from the page that owns connections.

Two kinds of integration sit side by side there and are labelled differently, deliberately:
- **user-authorized** (Google, Strava, PlayStation) — a real per-user token; connect and revoke
  from here.
- **server-configured** (Steam, Hevy, Apple Health) — a single-user secret in Supabase Vault
  with no browser consent step at all, so the card is a read-only status readout. **Never render
  a Connect button that can't actually connect anything.**

Load-bearing details:
- `GOOGLE_SCOPES` (`src/features/calendar/googleScopes.ts`) is the ONE definition of the Google
  consent list — the scope array used to live inline in `SettingsMenu.tsx`. Adding a scope =
  append there + one re-consent; never hard-code a second copy.
- **`useAutoRefreshCalendarToken()` runs in `useAppBootstrap()`** (called once by `AppShell` on
  EVERY shell route) — never on the Connections tab, which would reduce it to "refreshes only
  while that tab is open".
- `StravaWidget` is reused WHOLE inside its card (it owns the OAuth-redirect handling, sync and
  disconnect) rather than reimplemented — one Strava code path, just one home for it.
- The ⚙ menu (avatar) holds the theme switch, accent swatches, Notifications, then links to
  Connections and Developer, and Sign out; the phone More sheet reuses the same pieces.
  `/developer?tab=connections` is deep-linkable.
- Layout (`developerBoards.ts` → `CONNECTIONS_BOARD`): column stacks at every width (the PlayStation
  card is tall whenever its npsso form shows) — one list on phones and tablets; from the laptop two
  labelled columns ("Signed in from here" / "Configured on the server"); from 1920 the accounts use a
  42rem main track and the server-side cards sit in 19–22rem columns.

### Render-time crashes: `ErrorBoundary` (MANDATORY for reverse-engineered payloads)
`src/shared/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx` — the app previously had **no error boundary at all**,
so ONE bad field shape turned into a TypeError during render and React unmounted the entire
tree: a blank white page, which is exactly how the PSN game modal failed (reported as "bir oyuna
tıklayınca boş ekran geliyor"). A `try/catch` around a fetch can never see this — the throw
happens in render, not in the request.

Wrap any surface whose data shape is not contractually guaranteed (the PSN and Steam tabs and
modals are wrapped in `TgAdvancedView`/`TgModals`, the detail panel's blocks in `TgDetailPanel`) so a failure stays inside its own card, is toasted into
`app_error_logs` via `logError`, and offers a Try-again. **Guard the fields too** — a boundary is
the backstop, not the fix: `PsnGameModal` now treats `concept.genres` as string-or-array,
defaults missing `earnedTrophies`/`definedTrophies`, and surfaces the trophy-bridge error
instead of rendering the "no trophy set" message over a genuine failure.

### Other rules
- **English-only project artifacts (MANDATORY, user rule 2026-07-21):** everything in the repo or on GitHub — code, comments, UI strings, error/toast messages, commit messages, PR titles and bodies, docs — is written in English. The ONE exception is generated **on-phone** Shortcut/widget user-facing strings (see Two-AI setup). Chat replies to the user follow the Communication Style section — that rule is about the PROJECT, not the conversation.
- **Date format: ONLY `DD.MM.YYYY` (e.g. 29.09.2026), everywhere, forever (owner rule 2026-09-29).** Times `HH:MM` 24h; a weekday word may precede it. Only exceptions: chart axis ticks and Health's range bar may use `DD.MM`. Never month names, never `/`, never `en-US`. Use the shared formatter in `src/shared/utils/dateFormat.ts`. A date field is `DateInput`: on touch screens the field opens a month calendar (`DateCalendar`, no keyboard — typing left iOS drawing the caret at the sheet's pre-animation position); with a mouse you type DD.MM.YYYY or open the calendar from its icon. `WatchedWhenSheet`'s Other date shows the calendar inline.
- Never hardcode colour literals (`amber-*`, `gray-*`, hex) — use tokens and tones (see Theme & popups).
- **Cut text — `<Truncate>`** (`shared/ui`): never a bare `truncate` / `line-clamp` on user data. One shared ResizeObserver (`useIsTruncated`); inside a tappable row it never becomes a control of its own. Find leftovers with `scripts/audit-text-overflow.mjs --harness <dir>` (`--static` lists bare sites).
- Comments only when WHY is non-obvious.
- Component > ~150 lines → split it.
- No error handling for impossible scenarios. Only validate at system boundaries.
- **Screen vs data — ask, and never report one as the other (owner rule 01.10.2026).** When a request like "X and Y should be one" or "make these the same" could mean only the UI (one rail, one button, one name) OR the stored data (one table, one place, one source of truth), and the two differ in size (schema, migration, edge function, sync), **ask one question before building**. If you do ship a UI-only change, say so plainly in the report: "screen only — storage is still separate". Real incident: "smart lists should be normal lists" was built as a merged rail while the data stayed in two places (Trakt lists vs `media_follows` rules), and the report said they "behave like any other list" — they didn't exist on Trakt. The owner's default when unsure: the data is one; **Trakt should see what the app shows**.
- **Never add a speculative external-API field.** Verify a GraphQL/REST shape live (introspection, one real sample) before shipping code that reads it — the EnTur `nearest` query, the DSLD endpoints and the Hevy enum coverage were all confirmed this way; the still-open Kassalapp label values and the SpO2 shape are flagged as unconfirmed precisely because they weren't.

---

## Key Patterns

**Toast store:** `import { toast } from '../../../app/store'`

**Tasks — one table for every domain.** `tasks` is the single source of truth for tasks across every page; **never create a per-feature task table.** Enums: `domain` `personal|work|media` · `section` `inbox|today|tomorrow|this_week|backlog` · `status` `open|in_progress|done|cancelled` · `priority` `low|medium|high` · **`source_type` `manual|movie|tv_series|media|calendar|ai|training_session|project_item`** (widened by migrations 039 and 047). Canonical queries: Work board `domain='work' AND status != 'cancelled'` · Today `section='today' OR (due_date = current_date AND status='open')` · media plans `domain='media' AND source_type='media'` · inbox `section='inbox' AND status='open'`.

**`tasks.start_date` (migration `069`) is `due_date`'s OPENING edge**, not a second deadline: `start_date=A` + `due_date=B` means "do this between A and B", and `due_date` stays the **sole** deadline representation — so overdue, the brief, the push, `phone-gateway` and `workMeta.ts` learn no new concept. `ToDoItem` renders **one** range chip *instead of* the due chip (never both — that row's own comment records a real 136px-of-393px title-starvation bug) and still takes its colour and ⚠ from `due_date`. `TasksPanel` gains an **"Open now"** group (`start_date ≤ today < due_date`), computed client-side off `fetchAllTasks`' existing `select('*')`, so before 069 `start_date` simply reads `undefined` and the group stays empty — no probe, no query. The window's last day belongs to Today (on `due_date` the deadline is what matters), and Open now only ever takes rows *from* Upcoming, so nothing renders twice. **A window predicate is NEVER added to `fetchTasksForDay`** — a `42703` there takes down `DayView`, `DayQuickRail`, `HomePage` and Home's `TodaySummary` at once. Written on **both** `UnifiedPlanModal` save paths via `TaskWindowField` (season chips reusing `windowChips`' math; it could not live in `taskExtra`, which is a `React.ReactNode` with no access to `form`/`patch`), and `tasksApi` retries the write without `start_date` on `42703`/`PGRST204` (the `recipes.fiber_g` precedent) while create omits the key entirely when unset. Fixed in the same pass: `CreateTaskInput` had no `description`, so the Notes textarea was **silently dropped on create** while edit persisted it.

**Planning — `UnifiedPlanModal`:** The ONE modal for all task/time-block planning (`src/shared/components/plan-modal/`). **No Task/Schedule tab switcher any more (migration `077`, 2026-08-22)** — a `mode: 'task'|'schedule'|'recurring'` prop decides which ENTITY is being created/edited; the user never picks a tab. Passing `task`/`timeBlock`/`scheduleBlock` for an edit always wins over an explicit `mode` and infers it (`task`→'task', `timeBlock`→'schedule', `scheduleBlock`→'recurring'), so an edit caller never has to think about mode at all — only a CREATE call site needs `mode` explicitly. **Nobody mounts it directly any more**: callers open it through `useEntityModal().open({ kind })` with `task` / `time-block` / `schedule-block` (each with `id` to edit or `defaults` to create) or `plan-block` (`blockId`). `PlanEntityModals.tsx` holds the adapters and **the single routing rule** — a block with a `task_id` always opens as its task, including via `time-block` with an id; each adapter loads by id (`useTaskById`, `useTimeBlock`, `useScheduleBlock`), keeps the first loaded row so a refetch can't wipe typing, and shows loading/not-found states. `UnifiedPlanModal` renders `ModalShell`, has **no `supabase` import** (the linked block comes from `useLinkedTimeBlock`/`linkedTimeBlockQuery`; a failed read keeps Save disabled instead of assuming no block), writes through the `useTodos`/`useSchedule` hooks (`withProgress` for Saving…/Saved; deletes confirm via `entityModal.confirm`) — the one deliberate raw api call is the calendar-link persistence write, so its failure isn't toasted as a failed save. Shape it entirely from the request (the same props below) — never edit the modal folder:
- `mode` → required for create; ignored/inferred for edit (see above)
- `config` → `heading`, `hide*/lock*` field keys (no more `tabs`/`defaultTab` — removed with the tab switcher)
- `defaults` → prefills (title/date/startTime/duration/category/color/section/priority/domain/**startDate**/dueDate/**scheduled**…)
- `source` → `{ sourceType, sourceId, taskSourceType }`; `sourceType` is the block's REAL originating entity and MUST be a valid `time_blocks.source_type` (`training_session`/`movie`/`tv_episode`/`project_item`/`calendar`/`manual` — **never `'task'`**, removed from the CHECK by migration 077)
- `scheduleExtra` / `taskExtra` → caller-owned children injected into a tab
- `task` → edit a Task (mode='task') · `timeBlock` → edit a standalone one-off block with **no** linked task (mode='schedule'; a task-linked block is edited via `task` instead — the modal fetches its own linked block internally) · `scheduleBlock` → edit a recurring template (mode='recurring') · `onSaved` → post-save hook
- Rules + changelog live at the top of `UnifiedPlanModal.tsx`; log every logic change there.

**Task ↔ Schedule model (migration `077`, root-cause fix — read before touching either table):** A Task (deadline: `due_date`/`due_time`) and its optional one-off schedule slot (`time_blocks`, a specific date/time to actually do it) are **independent facts**, never kept in sync by a DB trigger any more. `time_blocks.task_id` (a real FK, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, DB-enforced at-most-one-per-task via a partial unique index) is the **ONLY** representation of "this block belongs to a Task" — `time_blocks.source_type` never equals `'task'` again (that CHECK value was removed). `source_type`/`source_id` on a task-linked block instead carry the block's REAL originating entity (`training_session`/`movie`/`tv_episode`/`project_item`/`calendar`/`manual`), independent of `task_id` and never overwritten by it — the actual bug this migration fixes (creating a task+schedule together used to silently discard the real source by overwriting it with `{source_type:'task', source_id:<task id>}`). The old bidirectional due-date↔block-date/time sync triggers and the "deleting/unscheduling a block soft-cancels its Task" trigger are **retired** (dropped, not disabled — their `link_rules` config rows are marked disabled + re-described instead of deleted). The one remaining cross-table effect is one-directional: a Task title edit mirrors onto its linked block's title via a DB trigger (`sync_time_block_title_from_task`, fires from every write door — browser, `ai-proxy`, Google Tasks pull — not just the modal). Tapping a `DayAgenda` row opens the correct editor for whatever was actually clicked, directly — no select-then-✎ second step (a small always-visible ⋯ toggle is the only way to reach the postpone/delete quick actions instead): a recurring row opens `scheduleBlock`, a task-linked one-off row opens the Task itself (`task`), a standalone one-off row opens `timeBlock` directly; there is no "which tab" decision anywhere in that flow. **This routing now lives only in `PlanBlockEntityModal`/`TimeBlockEntityModal` (`PlanEntityModals.tsx`)** — callers just open `time-block` with the block id (or `plan-block`). It exists because `TrainingCalendar`'s own plan-block click handler once got it wrong (opened via `timeBlock` unconditionally, which seeded `alsoCreateTask=true` since `buildInitialForm` had no idea a task already existed, and Save then minted a SECOND task and re-pointed the block at it). `saveSchedule`'s "Also add to Tasks" branch also gained a defensive `shouldCreateLinkedTask` guard (`planModal.config.ts`) that refuses to create a task at all when the `timeBlock` passed in already has one — `timeBlock` is contractually standalone-only, so this should be unreachable, but it's the last line of defense against exactly the TrainingCalendar-class bug. When writing `time_blocks` from a new call site (edge function or client), always set `task_id` for task-linkage and never write `source_type: 'task'` — and remember the two tables use DIFFERENT TV vocabulary (tasks: `tv_series`; time_blocks: `tv_episode` — mixing them up 400s on the CHECK, a real bug `ai-proxy`'s `planMedia` shipped with; `planMedia` now also stamps `season_number`/`episode_number` when the AI was given a specific episode, matching `EpisodesPanel`'s own single-episode rule, so `cleanup_block_on_episode_watched` can actually match the row). A caller with no `source` prop editing an EXISTING task/timeBlock falls back to that entity's own `source_type`/`source_id` (via `blockSourceTypeForTask`/`taskSourceTypeForBlock` in `planModal.config.ts`) — except `tasks.source_type='tv_series'` deliberately does NOT fall back to a block's `tv_episode` (no season/episode numbers to back that up; overclaiming episode identity generically was reconsidered and dropped). "One task = one Google entry" (see the Google Calendar + Tasks section below) is enforced on BOTH the create path and now every edit that newly makes a task calendar-linked or newly links a calendar-linked block to a task (`needsGoogleTaskDedupe`) — the create-only version of this check was a real gap.

**Calendar-status is a TYPED, tri-state contract — never a message-string guess.** `calendarApi.ts`'s `CalendarApiError` carries the real HTTP status (`isCalendarNotFound(error)` checks `error.status === 404`, never `message.includes('404')` — a Google error body can legally carry only a human message like `"Not Found"` with no digits in it at all). `scheduleApi.ts`'s `updateTimeBlock` and `UnifiedPlanModal`'s `linkCalendarEvent` both return a `TimeBlockCalendarStatus` (`scheduleSyncRules.ts`): `'linked'` (confirmed live this call), `'not_linked'` (confirmed gone — a real 404 whose local-id-clear write ALSO succeeded, or an explicit unlink that completed), or `'unknown'` (no token, a network/401/403/429/5xx failure, or the local clear/persist step itself failed — genuinely unconfirmed either way). The policy this feeds: `needsGoogleTaskDedupe`/`needsGoogleTasksFallback` (`planModal.config.ts`) only ever act on a CONFIRMED status — `'unknown'` triggers neither a dedupe-delete of the Google Task nor a create-time Google Tasks fallback, so an unconfirmed calendar failure can never cost the user their ONLY remaining Google representation of a task. `linkCalendarEvent` also compensates for the split-brain case where the remote Calendar event was created successfully but persisting its id locally then failed: it deletes the orphan it just created (reporting `'not_linked'` on success, `'unknown'` if even that compensation can't be confirmed) rather than leaving a real, untracked event behind. `useUpdateTask`'s own Task-title→Calendar-event push (a DB trigger keeps the block's title in sync, but has no OAuth token to reach Google with) is routed through the SAME `updateTimeBlock` call — not a second, independent `updateCalendarEvent` call with its own inconsistent swallow-everything error handling — so every calendar push in the app shares one lifecycle. An explicit unlink (`google_calendar_event_id: null`) with no Calendar token connected no longer silently drops the local link either — every OTHER patch field still applies, but the id itself is left alone (reported `'unknown'`) since nothing here could act on the remote side to justify clearing it.

**Google Tasks outbox — per-task FIFO + typed 404s (migration `079`).** `claim_google_tasks_outbox` (migration 073's `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`) now ALSO only ever claims the OLDEST outstanding row per `(user_id, task_id)` — a newer row for the same task can never be claimed while an older one is still outstanding, even mid-backoff or mid-claim by another worker. Real race this closes: an opt-out `'delete'` and a later Reopen `'force_recreate'` `'create'` for the SAME task, processed out of order, could let the stale delete's unconditional id-clear wipe the BRAND NEW `google_task_id` the create just wrote. `clear_google_task_id_if_matches` (new RPC) is the belt-and-suspenders half of that fix: `processDelete` (both `googleTasksOutbox.ts`'s browser drain and `google-tasks-sync`'s cron mirror) now only clears `tasks.google_task_id` when it STILL equals the id THIS delete targeted, instead of the old unconditional `apply_google_task_snapshot(p_clear_google_task_id=true)`. `GoogleTasksApiError`/`isGoogleTaskNotFound` (`googleTasksApi.ts`, mirrored by hand in the edge function) replace every `message.includes('404')` check on the Tasks API side too. `processCreate` also re-checks the LIVE task row after the Google `POST` returns (a real network round trip the user can act during) — if the task opted out/cancelled/hard-deleted while the create was in flight, the just-created remote task is deleted rather than adopted, and a failed compensation delete enqueues a durable retry `'delete'` outbox row (never just a log line) so a later drain finishes the cleanup.
- **The claim query above didn't actually lock anything — a second review pass caught this against a live `EXPLAIN`, before either migration ever went live.** The original `claim_google_tasks_outbox` computed the per-task `rn=1` filter in a `ranked` CTE (a `row_number()` window function over the base table), then applied `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` in a SEPARATE `claimable` CTE that selected only `id FROM ranked` — not directly from the base table. This is documented Postgres behavior, not a fluke: a locking clause on a query does **not** propagate into a WITH query that it merely selects from; the clause has to be attached to the individual `SELECT` INSIDE the WITH query to have any effect. The live `EXPLAIN` plan for the broken version confirmed it directly — no `LockRows` node anywhere — meaning the whole claim had silently stopped providing ANY concurrency guarantee: two workers could both believe `SKIP LOCKED` protected them while claiming the identical row, reintroducing the exact migration-073 duplicate this function exists to prevent. Fixed by moving `FOR UPDATE OF o SKIP LOCKED` onto a CTE that selects directly from the base table, with the per-task ordering expressed as a `NOT EXISTS` anti-join (`older.task_id = o.task_id AND (older.created_at, older.id) < (o.created_at, o.id)`) instead of a window function in the same query block as the lock clause — the same shape migration 073's own (correct) query already used, just with the anti-join added. A supporting `(task_id, created_at, id)` index ships alongside it.
- **The opt-in trigger branch (migration `078`) had its own real race, found reviewing that migration a second time.** `enqueue_google_tasks_outbox()`'s opt-in branch required `NEW.google_task_id IS NULL` before enqueueing a fresh `'create'` — but a row that was JUST opted out (the very next branch enqueues a `'delete'` for its CURRENT `google_task_id` WITHOUT clearing that column locally; only the drain's own `clear_google_task_id_if_matches` does that, once the delete actually runs) still HAS a non-null `google_task_id` until that delete drains. Re-opting in before it drains hit the `IS NULL` guard and enqueued **nothing at all** — the task ended up `google_sync_enabled=true` with a stale (or soon-to-be-cleared) id and no create ever queued to replace it. Fixed: the opt-in branch now enqueues a `'create'` on EVERY opt-in transition, stamping `force_recreate: true` whenever a `google_task_id` is still present — migration 079's per-task FIFO claim (fixed in the same review pass) guarantees the earlier pending `'delete'` for the SAME task drains first, so `force_recreate` is what lets this create proceed the moment that delete has cleared the id.

**Recurring editor never silently converts a selection.** `RecurringTab` (editing an EXISTING `schedule_blocks` row) uses `RECURRING_EDIT_OPTIONS` (no "No repeat" pill at all — `RecurrenceField` takes an `options` prop, defaulting to the full list for `ScheduleTab`'s CREATE flow, where "No repeat" genuinely means "make a one-off block instead"). `daysForRecurrence` no longer substitutes Mon-Fri when `weeklyDays` is empty — `hasValidRecurrenceSelection(mode, weeklyDays)` (`planModal.config.ts`) is the real gate: `UnifiedPlanModal`'s Save button is disabled and `handleSave` refuses to proceed whenever a `'weekly'` recurrence has zero days checked, for both the recurring editor and the schedule CREATE flow's own repeat picker.

**Recurring templates rendered into the PAST — real bug, fixed (migration `081`).** `schedule_blocks` (migration 004) carried `days_of_week` + `start_time` + `end_time` and **no date bounds of any kind**, so `projectRecurringBlocksForDay` matched a template on every calendar day whose weekday was in `days_of_week`, in BOTH directions, forever — creating a "weekdays 16:30" template on a Wednesday instantly back-filled every Mon-Fri in recorded history with a block that never happened. Fixed with one lower bound: `effective_from DATE NOT NULL` (existing rows backfilled from `created_at::date` — the honest start date for a row that never recorded one, so every day between a template's creation and today keeps rendering exactly as before and only the fabricated pre-creation history stops). `projectRecurringBlocksForDay` now takes `dateStr` as its first argument and gates BOTH branches; the spillover branch is gated on the **previous** day (the day that occurrence actually starts on), so a template effective from today never leaks a "continued from yesterday" tail onto its own first day. `createScheduleBlock` stamps `effective_from: todayStr()` **at the API layer, not any call site** (it's a property of the entity, so `UnifiedPlanModal.saveRecurring` needed no change) and sends the client's own LOCAL today rather than relying on the column's `CURRENT_DATE` default — that default is the SERVER's UTC day, which is yesterday for Oslo between 00:00 and 02:00; it remains the fallback for a writer that sends nothing, e.g. `ai-proxy`'s generic `db_insert`. A row with no `effective_from` (pre-081) is treated as unbounded, i.e. exactly the old behaviour, so the UI degrades instead of hiding every recurring block. **`effective_until` was deliberately NOT added** — the report is one-directional and an end date is a real feature with its own UI and its own "did this routine end?" semantics; adding the column now with no reader and no writer would be the speculative schema this repo's rules forbid. 9 assertions in `scripts/verify-plan-modal-helpers.cjs` §17 (inclusive boundary, the reported past-day case, the pre-migration fallback, and all three cross-midnight spillover cases).

**DayAgenda cross-midnight projection + task-linked click (real bugs, fixed).** A one-off block or recurring template that crosses midnight used to be attributed ENTIRELY to the day it starts on — the after-midnight portion never appeared on the next day's agenda at all, and that day's own booked-minutes/overlap math counted the FULL cross-midnight duration as if it all happened that day. `dayAgendaProjection.ts`'s `projectOneOffBlocksForDay`/`projectRecurringBlocksForDay` (pure, tested) now project every block/template onto EVERY day it actually occupies, each showing only its own `[0,24)` portion — a spillover row (↳ badge, "Continued from yesterday") on the next day for whatever crossed over, with its own synthetic id (never colliding with the canonical row) that still edits/deletes/postpones through the REAL `canonicalId`. Separately: a task-linked row's ✎ used to look the task up in a client-side preloaded map (`linkedTaskMap`) that could still be loading (or briefly stale) when tapped — a lookup miss silently did nothing. It now opens the `task` popup by id, which loads the row itself (loading and not-found states inside the popup) — no tap is ever a silent no-op.

**Pre-existing architecture limitation (documented, not fixed in this pass): a task hard-delete initiated OUTSIDE the browser can't clean up its linked Google Calendar event.** The browser's own `tasksApi.ts::deleteTask` has the user's live OAuth token and does this cleanup itself before the delete. A task deleted by a DB trigger/cascade, by `ai-proxy`'s generic `db_delete` tool, or by the Hevy functions' "close the matching planned-session task" logic runs entirely server-side with the service role and has no user OAuth token to reach Google Calendar with at all — the linked `time_blocks` row still disappears (via `task_id`'s `ON DELETE CASCADE`), but any Google Calendar event it held becomes a real orphan nothing ever revisits. A durable Calendar-outbox (mirroring the Google Tasks outbox's own architecture) would close this properly, but is a genuinely new piece of infrastructure — deliberately out of scope for this refactor's correctness pass. Do not "fix" this with a one-off client-side patch; if it's ever addressed, it should be addressed the same way the Tasks outbox was.

**Games:** see Games Feature Detail above. `games`/`game_platforms` (migration 089) — same main Supabase project as everything else now, no separate client.

**Home widgets:** All use `WidgetShell` + `useWidgetState` and read data through `features/home/hooks/*`. Disable queries when collapsed.

**Daily brief (Home) — rule-based, not AI (2026-09-26, user's call).** The old AI-written digest (`DailyBriefing`/`useDailyBriefing`/`briefingApi` + `fetchCurrencyWeekTrend`) was deleted and replaced by a deterministic brief: `briefRules.ts::buildDailyBrief(input)` (pure, import-free; `scripts/verify-daily-brief.cjs`, 41 assertions) turns already-loaded data into a greeting, ONE headline (priority: overdue tasks → in-progress block → training today → next block → open tasks) and short sections — Tasks (counts + a focus task: overdue > priority > due time), Schedule, Training, Food (kcal/protein left, water pace), Weather advice, Up next to watch, Wishes, Currency. A section with nothing worth saying is omitted, never padded; the same data always gives the same text; no AI call and no cost. `useDailyBrief()` only gathers inputs from existing hooks (no queries of its own). The phone `brief` action (`phone-gateway` → `ai-proxy`) is separate and still AI.

**TMDB images:** `https://image.tmdb.org/t/p/{size}{path}` (e.g. `w342`)

**Google Calendar + Tasks:** ONE unified "Connect Google" consent — the scope list (`GOOGLE_SCOPES` in `calendar/googleScopes.ts`) is `calendar.events` (read + write), `calendar.calendarlist.readonly`, and `tasks`, on one refresh token. Token stored in `useCalendarStore`; a reconnect is required if previously connected with a narrower scope set.
- **Subscribed/secondary calendars never appeared in the agenda — real bug, fixed in TWO rounds.** Round 1: (1) the scope list had no `calendarList`-reading permission at all, so `fetchCalendarList`'s `GET /users/me/calendarList` (`calendarApi.ts`) 403'd — `calendar.events` only grants read/write on *events*, never on calendar list/metadata; fixed by adding the narrowest scope that covers it, `calendar.calendarlist.readonly`, rather than the much broader `calendar`/`calendar.readonly`. (2) Even where the calendar picker already existed and worked (`WeekWidget`'s "⊞ Filter calendars", backed by `useCalendarList()` and persisted to `useCalendarStore`'s `selectedCalendarIds`), only the week/month grid's own dot indicators (`useCalendarEventDatesForRange`) actually looped over every selected calendar — `useCalendarEventsForDay`/`useCalendarEventsForRange` (the hooks `DayAgenda` actually reads for the real agenda list) were hardcoded to `calendarApi.ts`'s `calendarId = 'primary'` default and never looked at `selectedCalendarIds` at all. **Round 2 (still broken after reconnecting + regranting the new scope):** `selectedCalendarIds: null` meant two DIFFERENT things in two places — `WeekWidget`'s own `isCalSelected` treats null as "every calendar is selected" (every checkbox renders green/checked with zero action taken, since opening "⊞ Filter calendars" at all is undiscoverable and never required), but all THREE fetch hooks fell back to `selectedCalendarIds ?? ['primary']` — the same null read as "only primary". A user who never explicitly toggled a checkbox (the overwhelmingly common case) stayed primary-only forever regardless of the OAuth scope fix. Fixed with one shared `useSelectedCalendarIds()` (`useCalendar.ts`) that resolves null to every id from the SAME `useCalendarList()` query the picker already uses, falling back to `['primary']` only while that list genuinely hasn't loaded yet — used by all three fetch hooks (`useCalendarEventsForDay`/`useCalendarEventsForRange`/`useCalendarEventDatesForRange`), merged and sorted chronologically via a shared `sortEvents` helper. **Existing connected users must reconnect once** to pick up the new scope — there is no way to silently widen an already-granted consent.
- **Two-way block ↔ event link (migration `038`):** `time_blocks.google_calendar_event_id` stores the created event id; `scheduleApi.updateTimeBlock`/`deleteTimeBlock` (at the API layer, so ALL paths — modal, agenda edits, task delete — are covered) sync/remove the calendar event when a block moves or is deleted. All calendar calls are best-effort (try/catch + `logError`) — a sync failure never blocks the local write.
- **One task = ONE Google entry.** A task that will also become a calendar event used to produce a Google **Task** *and* an event — two things in Google for one thing here. Fixed: `useCreateTask` takes `skipGoogleTasks`, which `UnifiedPlanModal` passes whenever the task will be linked to an event; `linkCalendarEvent` is **idempotent** (re-reads the block and early-returns when `google_calendar_event_id` already exists, so no orphaned/duplicate events). "Add to Google Calendar" now also appears in **edit** mode, rendered as a disabled "Added ✓" readout when already linked. **Don't "simplify away" the `skipGoogleTasks` branch in `useTodos.ts`** — it's what prevents the duplicate on CREATE.
  - **The dedupe used to be CREATE-only — real gap, fixed.** Editing an ALREADY Google-synced task into a calendar-linked schedule (or checking "Also add to Tasks" on an already-calendar-linked standalone block) skipped the dedupe entirely, since it only ever ran at create time — the task ended up on Google twice. `saveTask`'s edit branch now computes `needsGoogleTaskDedupe(willBeCalendarEvent, googleSyncEnabled)` (`planModal.config.ts`) and, when true, patches `google_sync_enabled: false` — that re-fires migration 071's opt-out trigger branch, which enqueues the outbox `'delete'` that removes the now-redundant Google Task. `saveSchedule`'s `timeBlock` + "Also add to Tasks" branch got the matching `skipGoogleTasks` fix.
  - **Ordering matters — a second real bug found reviewing the fix above.** The dedupe patch (`google_sync_enabled: false`) must NOT be applied before the calendar link is CONFIRMED to have actually succeeded: `useUpdateTask` drains the outbox (deleting the Google Task) synchronously inside its own mutation, so patching the flag first and only then attempting `linkCalendarEvent` meant a failed calendar link (expired token, missing scope, …) left the task with NEITHER Google representation. Fixed: `saveTask`'s edit branch now updates content fields first (no `google_sync_enabled` in that patch), awaits `syncTaskSchedule`'s REAL, confirmed outcome (`linkCalendarEvent` now returns a boolean instead of swallowing everything), and only then — in a second, separate mutation — applies the dedupe if the link actually landed. `needsGoogleTaskDedupe`'s signature dropped its `googleTaskId` parameter for a second, related reason: a task can be `google_sync_enabled=true` with `google_task_id` still NULL (a `'create'` outbox row enqueued but not yet drained) — requiring a real id meant that pending create was never cancelled, and would still fire later and put the task back on Google as a duplicate. `shouldSkipPendingCreate` (`src/features/todo/api/googleTasksOutboxRules.ts`, a deliberately import-free pure module so it's testable without a live Supabase client) is the other half of that fix — both `processCreate` implementations (the browser drain in `googleTasksOutbox.ts`, and the `google-tasks-sync` edge function's hand-mirrored duplicate, since Deno can't import that file) now skip a `'create'` outright when the task has since opted back out (`google_sync_enabled=false`), treating it as a clean no-op rather than a failure.
  - **The SAME ordering hazard also existed on every CREATE path** (`saveTask`'s plain create, and both of `saveSchedule`'s "Also add to Tasks" branches) — each passes `skipGoogleTasks: willBeCalendarEvent` to `useCreateTask` speculatively, before the calendar link is even attempted; a failed link left the task with neither representation, same as the edit-path bug. `needsGoogleTasksFallback(skippedGoogleTasks, calendarLinked)` (`planModal.config.ts`) is the CREATE-side mirror of `needsGoogleTaskDedupe`, and all three create paths now call `reenableGoogleTasksIfCalendarFailed` after the real outcome is known — re-enabling `google_sync_enabled` (which re-fires migration 071's opt-in branch and pushes the task to Google Tasks) exactly when the calendar bet didn't pay off.
  - **`force_recreate` must never bypass the opt-out guard — a real regression caught reviewing the Reopen fix itself.** `shouldSkipPendingCreate`'s two guards originally shared one `!forceRecreate` condition, so a `force_recreate` row (Reopen) also bypassed the "task has since opted out" guard — a real sequence (Reopen enqueues a `force_recreate` `'create'`, then before it drains the task gains a calendar-linked schedule and opts out) could still put an unwanted Google Task back. Fixed: the opt-out check (`!task.google_sync_enabled`) is now unconditional in both `processCreate` implementations; `forceRecreate` bypasses ONLY the "already has an id" guard, which is all it was ever meant to do.
  - **`updateTimeBlock` used to swallow EVERY calendar-push failure the same way — a real gap the dedupe-ordering fixes above didn't close.** Even with the correct ORDER (confirm the calendar link before touching `google_sync_enabled`), the "confirmation" itself was worthless if the remote event had been deleted directly in Google Calendar (outside this app): the push 404s, `updateTimeBlock` only logged it, and the caller — reading the block's own still-non-null `google_calendar_event_id` column — reported "confirmed linked" anyway, so `needsGoogleTaskDedupe` deleted the task's real Google Task, leaving NEITHER Google representation. Fixed: `updateTimeBlock` now returns a real `calendarStatus` (`'linked' | 'not_linked' | 'unknown'`, `scheduleSyncRules.ts`'s `classifyCalendarPushFailure` is the pure 404-vs-everything-else classification, deliberately import-free so it's testable without a live Supabase client) — a CONFIRMED 404 clears the stale local id and reports `'not_linked'` (safe to recreate a fresh event); any OTHER failure (network, rate limit, an expired token, …) reports `'unknown'` WITHOUT touching the local id, since the event is presumably still fine and an unconfirmed failure must never be the reason a task's Google Task gets deleted. Both `syncTaskSchedule` and `saveSchedule`'s `timeBlock` branch now read THIS call's own confirmed status instead of the pre-call block snapshot.
  - **Reopen (↺) never actually recreated the task on Google — real bug, fixed (migration `078`).** Migration 071's "un-cancelled" trigger branch correctly enqueues a fresh `'create'` outbox row (Google Tasks has no undelete), but both drain implementations' `processCreate` (`googleTasksOutbox.ts`'s browser drain AND `google-tasks-sync`'s cron poller) guard `if (task.google_task_id) return` — a guard that's CORRECT for its original purpose (skip re-creating a task whose create already succeeded, e.g. a partial earlier failure) but can't tell that apart from "this id is a known-dead leftover from before the cancel." Fixed: the un-cancelled branch now stamps the payload with `force_recreate: true`, and both `processCreate`s bypass the guard specifically for that flag — every other `create` row keeps the exact same idempotency check as before.
  - **The CRON drain's stale-delete guard was silently dead — real bug, fixed.** `clear_google_task_id_if_matches` (migration 079) resolves its scope via `effective_user_id(p_user_id)`, which for a `service_role` caller returns `p_user_id` **verbatim** (never `auth.uid()` — there is no JWT on this path at all). `google-tasks-sync`'s edge-function helper called the RPC without `p_user_id`, so on the cron path `v_user_id` resolved to `NULL` and `UPDATE … WHERE user_id = NULL` matched zero rows every single time — 079's whole stale-delete-vs-Reopen race protection was live from the browser drain and a complete no-op from the cron drain. Fixed: `clearGoogleTaskIdIfMatches` now takes and forwards a `userId` parameter (`processDelete`'s own `userId`, already available — it's the outbox row's owner), threaded through on both call sites. The browser drain (`googleTasksOutbox.ts`) never had this bug — its RPC call is a real user JWT, so `effective_user_id` uses `auth.uid()` regardless of what's passed — but it wasn't touched, since the fix is edge-function-only.
  - **`applySnapshot` failure after a successful remote create could still duplicate the Google Task — real bug, fixed, then the fix ITSELF found broken by a second review pass.** `processCreate` POSTs to Google first, then calls `applySnapshot` to persist the returned id locally; if that persist step throws — a risk `migration 075`'s own header comment already flagged — a naive retry with `google_task_id` still NULL would call `createGoogleTask` again. The FIRST fix (compensate by deleting the orphan, or — if that delete also failed — durably `enqueueOrphanCleanup` a brand-new `'delete'` outbox row) was itself a real bug once migration 079's per-task FIFO claim landed IN THE SAME REVIEW PASS: a new row for the SAME task_id always sorts AFTER the original (still-outstanding, still-retrying) `'create'` row, and FIFO means the newer row can never be claimed while the older one is outstanding — the stale create would keep retrying (spawning a fresh orphan on every attempt) while its own cleanup queued uselessly behind it, forever. **Rewritten to checkpoint in-place, never a second row:** `processCreate` (`googleTasksOutbox.ts` / `google-tasks-sync`) stashes every state transition into THIS row's own `payload` — `created_remote_task` (the POSTed remote object, once known — reused on retry instead of re-POSTing) and `created_remote_tasklist` (the list it was created into, fixed at creation time so a later list-change on the task can't misdirect a retry's delete/adopt) and `abandon` (set when the remote task must be deleted rather than adopted — opted out mid-flight, or the checkpoint write itself failed) — via `patchPayload`/`patchOutboxPayload`. A retry of the SAME row resumes from whichever state its own payload records; no second outbox row is ever created for this. Closed the SAME day it was introduced, before either shipped independently.
  - **In-flight-edit gap, closed by the same rewrite.** A title/notes/due edit landing strictly between the original POST (fields now frozen/stale) and a LATER retry finally persisting the id would previously never reach Google at all — the DB trigger only enqueues an `'update'` row once `google_task_id` is actually set, which is exactly what the retry loop hasn't managed yet. Fixed: whenever a retry REUSES a checkpointed `created_remote_task` (as opposed to a fresh POST, which already carries current fields), it pushes one more `updateGoogleTask` PATCH with the task's live fields before `applySnapshot` — idempotent (PATCH always sends the full field set, never a diff), so it's free to run on every such retry.
  - **Every Supabase read/write inside the outbox drain now checks its own `{error}` — a real gap across `resolveGoogleListId`/`resolveGoogleParentId`/the task fetches/the row's own final delete.** All of these used to destructure only `{ data }`, so a transient DB failure was indistinguishable from a legitimate "no row" — silently misresolving a task's real list/parent, or (worse) the drain loop's own final `google_tasks_outbox` row delete: a failure there used to still increment `drained`, hiding that the row (and any checkpoint state on it) was still sitting there. Every one of these now throws on its own `error`, which routes into the SAME backoff/retry path a Google API failure already takes.
  - **`tasks.move` retried unconditionally on every retry of an update, even once an earlier attempt's move had already landed and only a LATER step failed — real gap, fixed.** The Tasks API has no documented no-op guarantee for moving a task to a parent/list it's already at; a reordering side effect on a redundant move is plausible, not just theoretical. `processUpdate` now fetches the task's current remote state first (`getGoogleTask`) and skips the `moveGoogleTask` call entirely once it's confirmed already at the target parent (list moves are always attempted — a single-list GET gives no cheap "already there" signal for that case).
  - **The Calendar→Google-Tasks fallback was one-directional in the edit path — real bug, fixed.** `saveTask`'s edit branch already ran `needsGoogleTaskDedupe` (gained a calendar link → turn Google Tasks OFF) but never its mirror: if an already-`google_sync_enabled=false` task's schedule was later unlinked from Calendar (block deleted, or GCal disconnected) so `syncTaskSchedule` now returns `'not_linked'`, nothing ever turned Google Tasks back on — the task silently ended up with **neither** Google representation. Fixed by capturing `wasCalendarLinked` (from the pre-sync snapshot of the linked block) and adding an `else if (!task.google_sync_enabled && needsGoogleTasksFallback(wasCalendarLinked, calendarStatus))` branch that re-enables `google_sync_enabled` — reusing the SAME pure `needsGoogleTasksFallback` function the create paths already use, no new predicate needed. `needsGoogleTaskDedupe`/`needsGoogleTasksFallback` are mutually exclusive (one requires `calendarStatus==='linked'`, the other `'not_linked'`), so the `else if` can never race against the dedupe branch above it.
  - **`deleteTimeBlock` swallowed every Calendar delete failure, not just a confirmed 404 — real bug, fixed.** Unlike `updateTimeBlock`'s own unlink branch just above it in the same file (which already throws on any non-404), `deleteTimeBlock` logged EVERY calendar-delete error (network, 401/403, 429, 5xx) and deleted the local `time_blocks` row regardless — since that row's `google_calendar_event_id` was the only thing tracking the remote event, doing so left a real, permanently untracked orphan on the user's Google Calendar. Fixed to match `updateTimeBlock`'s pattern exactly: only a confirmed `isCalendarNotFound` proceeds to the local delete; any other failure throws instead of deleting.
  - **A second review pass found the write side had NO token refresh at all — real gap, closed by `calendarTokenSync.ts`.** Every Calendar WRITE path (`deleteTimeBlock`, `deleteTask`, `linkCalendarEvent`, `updateTimeBlock`) used to read `useCalendarStore.getState().accessToken` raw — the store's own `expiresAt` was never consulted, unlike the READ-side query hooks (`useCalendar.ts`'s `ensureToken`), which already refresh proactively on a timer. A token could sit expired-but-still-present in the store between refreshes and every write would 401, indistinguishable from "not connected" — the exact class of bug that made `deleteTimeBlock`/`deleteTask` unable to tell "no token" from "token, but stale" and, before the fixes below, treat both as "proceed with the local delete anyway." `ensureValidCalendarToken()` (new file, `calendarTokenSync.ts` — deliberately SEPARATE from `calendarApi.ts`, which stays import-free of the live supabase client/store singleton so `CalendarApiError`/`isCalendarNotFound` stay requirable as pure code by `scripts/verify-plan-modal-helpers.cjs`) mirrors `ensureToken`'s refresh-if-needed logic for use outside a component; `ensureLinkedCalendarEventRemoved(eventId)` (same file) is the shared remote-first removal every delete/unlink path goes through.
  - **`deleteTimeBlock`/`deleteTask` used to only even LOOK for a linked Calendar event when a token happened to already be present — real gap, closed together with the token-refresh fix above.** Both now read the block's `google_calendar_event_id` UNCONDITIONALLY, before ever checking for a token: no linked event at all → delete locally, no token needed; a linked event exists → a token MUST be obtained (refreshing via `ensureValidCalendarToken`) and the remote event MUST be confirmed removed (any non-404 failure throws) BEFORE the local delete is even attempted. Previously, "no token" (or a stale one `calToken()`/`getState()` didn't know was expired) meant the linked event was never even considered, and the local row — its only pointer to that event — was deleted anyway, orphaning it.
  - **`linkCalendarEvent`'s own "does a block already have an event?" lookup never checked its read's `{error}` — real bug, fixed.** A DB read failure there (network, RLS, …) looked identical to "no event linked yet," letting the function go on to create a SECOND Calendar event for a block that already had one. The lookup now throws on its own `error` instead of silently falling through.
  - **Calendar event creation gained a deterministic, client-supplied id — closes the one Calendar-side gap Google's OWN Tasks API can't (a genuine, documented Tasks API limitation, not something app code can fix: `tasks.insert` accepts no client id or `requestId` at all, unlike Calendar's `events.insert`).** `linkCalendarEvent` now POSTs with `id: blockId.replace(/-/g, '')` (a uuid's hex characters are a valid subset of Calendar's own base32hex id charset) — if a PRIOR attempt's POST actually landed on Google but its response never reached us (a network timeout), retrying with the SAME id 409s (`isCalendarConflict`) instead of silently minting a second event; the 409 is caught and the existing event is adopted via the new `getCalendarEvent`.
  - **`fetchEventsForDay`'s `timeMax` was `date+"T23:59:59"` — one second short of the correct EXCLUSIVE boundary** per Google's own documented `events.list` semantics (a match requires `event.end > timeMin AND event.start < timeMax`). Fixed to next-day local midnight; `useCalendarEventDatesForRange`'s own range query had the identical off-by-one and got the same fix.

**DayAgenda's Calendar-event rendering, fixed on TWO fronts (a second review pass, after the local-block cross-midnight projection above had already shipped).**
- **A linked block's spillover row and its OWN Calendar event could both render on the SAME day.** `linkedGCalIds` (the de-dupe set — "this event id already has a `time_blocks` row representing it, don't also render the raw Calendar-API result") was built from `timeBlocks` alone. A block whose OWN `date` is YESTERDAY but crosses midnight into TODAY renders its spillover tail from `prevTimeBlocks` — and per Google's own `events.list` window semantics (a cross-midnight event matches whenever `event.end > timeMin`), that SAME linked event is ALSO returned by TODAY's own Calendar fetch. Without `prevTimeBlocks` in the de-dupe set, it rendered twice: once as the spillover row, once as a separate 'calendar' row. Fixed: `linkedGCalIds` now unions `timeBlocks` and `prevTimeBlocks`.
- **A cross-midnight Calendar event's own startHour/endHour were computed straight from each instant's `.getHours()`**, correct per-instant but assembled into a single startHour=23/endHour=1 pair for a 23:00→01:00 event — a nonsensical (negative-duration) range that broke the overlap check and the booked-minutes sum the moment a Calendar event crossed midnight, mirroring the exact bug the one-off/recurring block projection fix (`dayAgendaProjection.ts`) had already closed for local blocks. Fixed with a new pure helper, `projectCalendarEventForDay(dateStr, event)`, clipping the event onto ONE day exactly like `projectOneOffBlocksForDay` already does for a same-shape local block — 5 new assertions in `scripts/verify-plan-modal-helpers.cjs` (§15) cover the cross-midnight clip, the spillover flag, an unrelated day returning `null`, a normal same-day event, and an all-day event.

**Duration bounds — `time_blocks.duration_minutes` had no real upper (or DB-enforced lower) bound.** The custom-duration input (`DurationField`, `fields.tsx`) had `min={1}` and no `max` at all, and — critically — an HTML `min`/`max` attribute doesn't block a form submit regardless; nothing downstream re-validated the parsed value either (`Number(form.customMin) || 60` lets a negative string like `"-30"` through unchanged, since a nonzero negative number is still truthy). This matters because `dayAgendaProjection.ts`'s whole cross-midnight model assumes a block occupies at most its own start day plus a SINGLE spillover day — a >1440-minute (>24h) block would need a third day's worth of spillover nothing in that file (or `TrainingCalendar`'s dot-per-day rendering, or Home's next-session lookup) was ever built to represent; it wouldn't crash, it would just silently vanish past its second day. Fixed with `clampDurationMinutes` (`planModal.config.ts`, pure, `Math.min(1440, Math.max(1, Math.round(raw)))`, falls back to 60 on a non-finite input) applied at all three `effDuration` computations in `UnifiedPlanModal.tsx` (schedule create/edit, recurring create/edit), the input's own `max={1440}` as a visible hint, and migration `080` (`time_blocks_duration_minutes_range` CHECK constraint, clamping any pre-existing out-of-range rows first so the `ADD CONSTRAINT` itself can't fail) as the real enforcement layer — the client clamp alone doesn't cover `ai-proxy`'s generic `db_insert`/`db_update` tool, which writes `time_blocks` directly with no knowledge of this app-layer clamp at all.
- **Google Tasks field coverage (audited + fixed 20/08/2026).** Per-task real-time sync covers create (title/notes/due), toggle-done (complete/reopen via `status`, `completed:null` to clear on reopen), delete, and — **the one real gap found** — edit: `useUpdateTask` used to be a bare passthrough with zero Google sync, so rescheduling a due date or fixing a typo left the Google-side copy silently stale forever. Fixed: it now PATCHes title/notes/due to Google whenever one of those three fields is actually in the patch (a priority/section-only edit doesn't touch Google — nothing there to update). All sync is soft-fail (`logError`, never blocks the Supabase write), matching the create/toggle/delete precedent. `useSyncFromGoogleTasks`/`usePushToGoogleTasks` (previously dead code, no UI trigger) are now reachable via `GoogleTasksSyncButtons` in Developer → Connections, on `useMutationWithFeedback`. The pull direction (`useSyncFromGoogleTasks`) also gained a fix: it read `rt.notes` from the Google Tasks API response but never carried it into the imported row — a task's detail written in the Google Tasks app used to vanish on import.
  - **Deliberately NOT built** (real Google Tasks fields with no equivalent here, verified via the Tasks API's real resource shape — not guessed): **multiple task lists** (everything reads/writes `/lists/@default/tasks` only; our own `domain`/`section` categorization doesn't map 1:1 onto Google's flat named-list structure, and building that mapping is a real feature, not a wiring fix); **subtasks** (`parent`/indentation — our `tasks` table has no parent-task concept at all, a data-model change, not a sync gap); **manual ordering** (`position` — our own `sort_order` is independent and un-synced either direction); **`links`** (e.g. an attached email) — no use case for a personal task app.

**Schedule cache invalidation:** time-block mutations (`useCreateTimeBlock`/`useUpdateTimeBlock`/`useDeleteTimeBlock`) invalidate the `schedule`/`taskGraph` groups — the WHOLE `['schedule']` namespace + `['calendar']`, not a single day key — consumers read schedule under different sub-keys (`['schedule','day',date]` for Daily/Home/Work via the shared `useTimeBlocks`, `['schedule','training-range',…]` for the Training calendar / Home next-session). **Never give a schedule view its own private query key** (Work's old `['time-blocks']` key went stale because nothing invalidated it). Same class of bug bit the food card — see Food's cache-key note.

**@headlessui/react v2 — UI primitives (never use manual open/close state for these):**

| Use case | Component | Anchor / notes |
|---|---|---|
| Modal / drawer | **`ModalShell` / `SideDrawer`** (`src/shared/modals`), never a raw `Dialog` | they wrap Headless UI `Dialog` with sheet/drag/Back/stacking built in |
| Search autocomplete | `Combobox` + `ComboboxInput` + `ComboboxOptions` + `ComboboxOption` | `immediate` if options should show on focus; `data-[focus]:bg-cream-50` on option |
| Floating action panel | `Popover` + `PopoverButton` + `PopoverPanel` | `anchor="top start"` / `anchor="bottom start"` via Floating UI |
| Dropdown menu | `Menu` + `MenuButton` + `MenuItems` + `MenuItem` | `anchor="bottom end"`; keyboard nav built-in |

All of these handle click-outside close, Escape, focus trap, portal rendering and ARIA. Never add these manually. Anything portalled must use token classes (and, on `/games`, the `tg-portal` class).

Reference files: `src/shared/modals/ModalShell.tsx` + `SideDrawer.tsx` (popups), `src/features/home/components/ruter/StopSearchInput.tsx` (Combobox), `src/features/media/components/PlanThisButton.tsx` (Popover), `src/shared/components/SettingsMenu.tsx` (Menu).

---

## Edge Functions (Supabase)

**Edge Functions are deployed MANUALLY** (Supabase Dashboard, or `supabase functions deploy` via CLI). A GitHub Actions auto-deploy job was tried and removed — it kept failing on the runner and wasn't worth the noise. Manual deploy is clean because **every function is self-contained (no `_shared/` imports)** — the shared Hevy upsert logic is inlined into all 4 Hevy functions, so a Dashboard paste no longer hits the "Module not found `_shared`" bundling error. **DB migrations are also manual** (Dashboard → SQL Editor or `supabase db push`); they do NOT run in GitHub Actions.
