**`verify_jwt = false` applies to ELEVEN functions** — `supabase/config.toml` records them and a CLI deploy applies it automatically; a **Dashboard** deploy needs the "Enforce JWT Verification" toggle set by hand for each: **`hevy-sync`, `health-export-webhook`, `google-tasks-sync`, `ai-proxy`, `phone-gateway`, `push-send`, `esde-sync`, `esde-media-sync`, `esde-content-sync`, `screenscraper-media`, `trakt-api`.** Every one of them authenticates with its own Bearer/custom secret (or, for `screenscraper-media`, a per-game HMAC signature) rather than a Supabase JWT (checked in-code), so leaving platform verification ON makes Supabase reject the call **before our code runs**. This is a real production gotcha, hit twice.

**Correct-in-the-repo ≠ deployed.** Because functions and migrations are manual, a merged commit can still be running old code in production. Sequence any "must be deployed before X" work against the moment the *user-triggerable* path goes live (a Fetch-now button, a new gateway action) — **not** against a cron being enabled; a single button click can write real rows immediately.

| Function | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `ai-proxy` | Gemini AI calls — the generic DB tool layer, special-purpose tools, vision, semantic search, model fallback chain, structured (`responseSchema`) and `fetchUrl` modes. Dual-auth: browser user JWT (validated in-code via `getUser`) OR the iPhone device secret (`x-phone-secret`). Full detail: the AI section above; cost/capability reference: `docs/ai-cost-capability-analysis.md`. |
| `phone-gateway` | The iPhone's single DURABLE entry point (Shortcuts / Scriptable) — authenticates by `x-phone-secret` (== `PHONE_GATEWAY_SECRET`), acts as the single user (`HEVY_USER_ID`) via the service role **server-side** (the service key never touches the device), takes a flat `{action, …}` body (no nested JSON, no expiring JWT). 9 deterministic actions run directly (incl. `import_body_composition`, migration `085`); 3 AI actions forward to `ai-proxy` with the same secret. Full action list + the ~25s latency design: the iPhone section above. Setup + contract: `docs/iphone-examples.md`; rationale: `docs/iphone-web-integration.md`; widgets: `docs/scriptable-widgets.md`; food logger: `docs/scriptable-food-logger.md`. |
| `push-send` | Web Push sender — signs a VAPID push to every `push_subscriptions` row so a notification reaches the lock screen while the PWA is closed. Dual-auth: the pg_cron `lascis-morning-push` job (`x-cron-secret` == `PUSH_CRON_SECRET`, acts as `HEVY_USER_ID`, `{trigger:'morning'}` → a short deterministic tasks+training summary) OR a browser JWT (`{title,body,url}` ad-hoc). Prunes expired subscriptions on 404/410. Migration `068`. Runbook: `docs/web-push-setup.md`. |
| `food-search` | Kassalapp (kassal.app) proxy — Norwegian branded groceries by name (`{search}`) or barcode (`{ean}`), `KASSALAPP_API_KEY` kept server-side. Returns per-100g `BarcodeProduct`s, or `[]` if the key is unset (the client then falls back to Open Food Facts, which it calls direct — CORS-open, no key). `verify_jwt` ON. |
| `google-tasks-sync` | Google Tasks background poller (Phase 3 of the integration below) — drains `google_tasks_outbox` (local→Google, respecting `next_retry_at` backoff, unlike the browser's manual drain which ignores it) then pulls Google→local (`updatedMin` incremental with a 60s look-back once a clean prior run exists, else a full `showCompleted/Hidden/Deleted=true` sync). Self-contained per the Hevy-functions convention — its Google Tasks REST helpers are a deliberate duplicate of `googleTasksApi.ts`'s; update both if the API surface changes. Per-list failure isolation: one Google list's error doesn't block others, but `google_tasks_sync_state.last_success_at` only advances on a FULLY clean run — advancing it on a partial pull would permanently skip that list's missed window, since the next run's `updatedMin` is bounded by this run's timestamp regardless of what actually got processed. Dual auth: user JWT or `x-sync-secret` (cron, migration `072`, every 20 min). **Why migration 072 exists:** the migration-071 RPCs (`apply_google_task_snapshot`/`upsert_task_from_google`/`set_task_parent_from_google`) scope writes via `auth.uid()`, which is NULL for a service-role caller — 072 added an explicit `p_user_id` param to each, honored only when `auth.role() = 'service_role'` (unforgeable by a browser caller), with zero behavior change for existing browser call sites. |
| `calendar-oauth` / `calendar-token` / `calendar-disconnect` | Google OAuth — **one unified "Connect Google" consent**: Calendar + Tasks on ONE refresh token in `user_calendar_tokens` (scope list lives in `calendar/googleScopes.ts`; adding a future scope = append there + one incremental re-consent). |
| `news-proxy` | RSS feed proxy |
| `strava-auth` / `strava-activities` / `strava-disconnect` | Strava OAuth + activity fetch |
| `hevy-initial-sync` | Bulk import all Hevy data (workouts, templates, routines, body measurements) |
| `hevy-sync` | Webhook receiver — new workout from Hevy → upsert. Hevy sends its own bearer secret, so **JWT verification must be OFF** (see the six-function note above); the symptom when it's on is "manual Sync works, nothing arrives automatically". |
| `hevy-incremental-sync` | Events-based incremental sync (workout edits/deletes since the last cursor) + a full re-fetch/upsert of routines, routine folders and body measurements (event-less collections), so Sync surfaces new Hevy-app routines without a full re-import |
| `hevy-api` | Write proxy — web → Hevy (create/update workouts, routines, body measurements). Also the single place the documented Hevy payload traps are handled server-side. |
| `health-export-webhook` | Webhook receiver — Health Auto Export POSTs `{data:{workouts,metrics}}` → upsert into `health_workouts`/`health_metrics`, stamping `source_family='apple'`. Bearer secret, JWT verification OFF. Every incoming metric point becomes its own row, idempotent on `(user_id,metric_name,recorded_at,source)` / workout `id`, with no aggregation at ingest — which is what makes it safe against "Batch Requests" splitting one export into multiple calls in any order. |
| `steam-api` | Proxies the Steam Web API (`STEAM_API_KEY`/`STEAM_ID64` in Vault) for the Games page's Steam tab — owned games, playtime, achievements, level/badges, currently-playing status. No local table (see Games Feature Detail) — read-through only. Normal browser-JWT auth, `verify_jwt` stays ON. Returns `{error:'not_configured'}` rather than throwing if the Vault secrets aren't set yet. |
| `psn-api` | Proxies PlayStation Network via the community `psn-api` npm package (Sony has no official API — see Games Feature Detail). Stores/refreshes tokens in `psn_tokens` (migration `091`) after the user pastes an `npsso`; `verify_jwt` stays ON and the function resolves the caller's id via `supabase.auth.getUser` (same pattern as `calendar-oauth`) to scope that table. **Unverified against a real PSN account** — needs a live pass once deployed with a real npsso. |
| `screenscraper-sync` | Rewritten 2026-09-25 (see "ScreenScraper — the Scrape page"). Browser-JWT auth (`verify_jwt` stays ON) and **owner only** (`HEVY_USER_ID`, else 403). Actions: `status`, `storage` (no ScreenScraper call — `game_media_usage()`, migration `104`), `refresh_systems`, `search` (name + ROM filename/size/CRC/MD5/SHA1/serial + ScreenScraper id, all at once, merged), `candidate` (one entry + its full record), `sign` (fresh proxy signatures for saved games), `apply` (per-field fill/replace/skip, per-media copy/online, title/description variant overrides, filename matches verified before dump-specific values, the small pointer on `provider_data` + the heavy record in `game_scrape_records`, the match marker in `games.ss_jeu_id`, everything journaled with prior values and replaced paths), `find_batch` (≤10), `apply_batch` (≤5, deadline-bounded), `undo` (latest apply per game only), `cleanup` (dry run / delete ScreenScraper copies nothing references). All four Vault credentials on every call (premium hangs off the MEMBER pair). **Security, load-bearing:** no ScreenScraper URL is ever returned, logged or stored (every one carries devid/devpassword/ssid/sspassword, incl. `header.commandRequested` and `hacks[].downloadurl`) — payloads pass through `stripCredentials`, media is either copied into Storage (resized by ScreenScraper itself via `maxwidth`/`outputformat`) or served by `screenscraper-media`; every error is scrubbed (failures land in `app_error_logs`, which `ai-proxy` reads); redirects never carry credentials. **Storage:** nothing is copied without reading the bucket size first and reserving room for the whole save under the user's budget (default 800 MB, hard cap 950 MB) — over the Free plan's 1 GB the whole project is eventually locked with 402. **Quota floor:** refuses below 300 requests / 50 misses left today (the counters are shared with the handheld). The pure logic is GENERATED into the file between `// <ss-shared>` markers by `scripts/sync-screenscraper-shared.mjs` from `src/features/games/scraper/` — edit the source, run the script (`scripts/verify-screenscraper-v2.cjs` fails if it is stale). |
| `screenscraper-media` | Streams one ScreenScraper file (image, manual PDF, video) to an `<img>`/`<video>`/link without the browser ever seeing a ScreenScraper URL: `?j=<game>&s=<system>&m=<their media token>&x=<expiry>&k=<sig>` (+`w`/`f` for a resized image, `e` for video/manual). **JWT verification OFF** (an `<img>` cannot send a header) — every request instead carries an HMAC-SHA256 signature over `jeu|system|expiry` issued by `screenscraper-sync` (key `SCREENSCRAPER_MEDIA_KEY`, else derived from the service-role key), binding ONE game on ONE system until the end of next week, so a leaked link can only fetch that game's own media for a few days. Only fixed widths (120/200/360/640/1280), whitelisted params, whitelisted content types and response headers (+ `nosniff`, a sandbox CSP, inline disposition), browser-cached only until the signature's expiry, their plain-text errors mapped to 404/503 (never echoed), one retry when they say busy, one bare redirect hop. Nothing is stored — this is what makes "Online" cost no Storage. |
| `esde-media-sync` | Device-secret authenticated inventory, optimized covers and guarded ES-DE variant deletion. Cleanup includes original image extensions in the managed per-variant Storage namespace. Refuses an upload that would take the `game-media` bucket past **950 MB** with 413 `storage_full` (reads `game_media_usage()`, migration 104; allows the upload if that function is missing). |
| `esde-content-sync` | Device-secret authenticated source XML snapshots, original non-PDF/non-video image uploads, and expected-hash asset-reference pruning (migration 100). Uses Edge Function environment secrets `ESDE_SYNC_SECRET` and `HEVY_USER_ID`. Same **950 MB** storage guard (413 `storage_full`) as `esde-media-sync`, checked after validation and right before each upload. |
| `trakt-api` | Trakt OAuth, reads and writes (`authorize_url`, `connect`, `status` (+ changes waiting, last error/result), `disconnect` (also clears the outbox), `snapshot`, `import`, `sync`, and live reads `playback` / `calendar` / `lists` / `list_items` + `list_create` / `list_delete` / `list_add` / `list_remove` — see Media → Trakt). `ratings` (MDBList scores, `MDBLIST_API_KEY`) works without a Trakt connection. Dual auth, `verify_jwt` **OFF**: a browser JWT resolved with `getUser`, or the cron's `x-sync-secret` (`TRAKT_SYNC_SECRET`, acts as `HEVY_USER_ID`, sync only); new titles need `TMDB_API_KEY`. Resolves the caller and keeps tokens in `trakt_tokens` via the service role (refreshes when < 10 min left). Redirect URIs are an allow-list in the function and must match the Trakt app. Returns `{error:'not_configured'}` without the secrets, `{error:'reauth_required'}` on a 401. |
| `igdb-api` | IGDB (Twitch) for game length, scores and a page link — see "IGDB" under Games. Browser JWT (`verify_jwt` ON), writes scoped via `getUser`. Secrets `IGDB_CLIENT_ID`/`IGDB_CLIENT_SECRET` (a Twitch developer app); app token by client credentials, kept in memory. Returns `{error:'not_configured'}` without them, `{error:'not_migrated'}` before 121. |
| `esde-sync` | Receives the RP6 handheld's EmulationStation-DE library push (`{action:'import_esde_games', games:[…]}`, ≤150 per batch). **Its own function and its own device secret (`x-esde-secret` == `ESDE_SYNC_SECRET`), deliberately NOT an action on `phone-gateway`** — that gateway is the iPhone's door and carries the iPhone's secret; the handheld is a different device with a different lifecycle, and revoking one must never revoke the other. Same pattern (static revocable secret → acts as `HEVY_USER_ID` via the service role), separate door. Upserts against migration 093's `(user_id, esde_system, esde_path)` key: creates a `games` + `game_platforms` pair per entry, and on a row it has seen before writes **play statistics ONLY** (metadata is a create-time fact — re-sending title/description on every push would silently undo the user's own curation, the same shape as the Hevy body-measurement overwrite incident). Batches are independent and idempotent, so a retry or a full re-push is always safe. JWT verification **OFF**. Contract: `docs/games/screenscraper-integration.md` §10. |

**Hevy upsert logic (`upsertWorkoutToDb`/`upsertRoutineToDb`)** — upsert row → delete exercises → re-insert exercises+sets, plus the "a logged Hevy workout auto-closes its planned-session task" behaviour (if `workout.routine_id` matches an open `tasks` row with `source_type='training_session'`, that task is deleted — its linked `time_blocks` row goes with it automatically via `task_id`'s `ON DELETE CASCADE`, migration 077; no separate `time_blocks` delete). This was a shared `_shared/hevySync.ts` Deno module, but **Supabase Dashboard deploys don't bundle sibling `_shared/` files** (deploy-time "Module not found"), so it is **inlined verbatim into all 4 Hevy functions** (`hevy-sync`/`hevy-initial-sync`/`hevy-incremental-sync`/`hevy-api`) — each self-contained and deployable by any method. **Trade-off: if this logic changes, update all four copies by hand** (a header comment in each flags this). There is no `supabase/functions/_shared/` directory.

### Pending manual steps (deploy checklist)

One list instead of per-feature notes, because otherwise it's impossible to tell what's still outstanding. Everything below is applied by the **user**, not by CI.

| Step | Needed for |
|---|---|
| Apply migrations in `supabase/migrations/` in order (Dashboard → SQL Editor or `supabase db push`) | Any schema change. Client code is written to survive an un-applied migration where noted (Food's `status`/`fiber_g`/`is_temp`/`image_url` fallbacks; AI's best-effort logging writes are no-ops until 064/065). |
| Apply **`089_games.sql`** (`games` + `game_platforms`; brand-new tables, updates no existing row) **and then run the RP5→here data import** (a separate, not-yet-written one-time script — see Games Feature Detail) | The Games feature now reads/writes THIS database instead of the separate RP5 Supabase project. Un-applied is survivable (pre-migration-safe: reads degrade to `[]`/empty stats, writes throw a named "migration 089 not applied" error) — but even once applied, the Games page shows an EMPTY library until the RP5 data import actually runs; that import is intentionally not part of this migration (it needs ScreenScraper field names confirmed live and the RP6 device's own file layout inspected first). **Do not delete/shut down the RP5 Supabase project until the import is verified.** |
| Apply **`090_games_play_dates.sql`** (`games.started_at`/`finished_at`; updates no existing row) | The quick status switch's auto-stamped play dates + the Edit panel's Started/Finished fields (see Games Feature Detail). Un-applied is survivable: `setPlayStatus`'s read/write of these two columns 42P01s like any other pre-089 write, caught by the same pre-migration-safe guard as the rest of `gamesApi.ts` — everything else on the page keeps working, only the dates stay unset. |
| Apply **`069_wish_items.sql`** (`wish_items` + `tasks.start_date`; **updates no existing row**) | Wishes + task windows. Un-applied is survivable: wish reads return `[]`, wish writes throw a named error, `tasks.start_date` writes retry without the column, and `TasksPanel`'s "Open now" stays empty. **`phone-gateway` is NOT redeployed for this feature.** |
| Apply **`070_athlete_profile.sql`** (`athlete_profile` + `athlete_limitations`; **updates no existing row**) | Training's athlete profile + limitations. Un-applied is survivable: every read degrades to `null`/`[]`, every write throws a named "migration 070 not applied" error, `ptCoachApi`'s `PROFİL:` block simply never appears. **`phone-gateway` is NOT redeployed for this feature.** |
| Apply **`077_task_time_block_relationship.sql`** (`time_blocks.task_id` FK + unique index; backfills every `source_type='task'` row by detaching orphans or recovering the real source from the linked task, never deleting a row; drops the old bidirectional due-date/schedule sync triggers + the delete-cascades-to-task trigger; adds the one-way title-mirror trigger; adds `schedule_blocks.category`/`updated_at`) **BEFORE** redeploying `ai-proxy` or any of the 4 Hevy functions below — those redeploys assume `task_id` already exists | The Task/Schedule model fix. Must be applied first: the client and edge-function code shipped in this same change write `task_id` and no longer write `source_type='task'`, so deploying the code before the migration would 400 on the missing column. |
| Redeploy `ai-proxy` whenever `DB_CATALOG`, `TOOLS`, `SYSTEM_PROMPT`, or any tool's own logic changes server-side | The catalog, tool declarations, and every tool's implementation live in the function, not the client. Outstanding: `wish_items` (`rw` + `EMBED_SOURCES`) and `tasks.start_date`, `athlete_profile`/`athlete_limitations` (`rw`) + the new `get_athlete_profile` tool, migration 077's `time_blocks.task_id` column + updated `db_delete`/DB_CATALOG text (`source_type` no longer includes `'task'`), `planMedia`'s TV branch (was writing `time_blocks.source_type='tv_series'`, which the 077 CHECK rejects — tasks and time_blocks use different TV vocabularies, `tv_series` vs `tv_episode`), and `body_composition_reports` (`ro`, migration 085). JWT enforcement stays **OFF**. |
| Redeploy all 4 Hevy functions (`hevy-sync`/`hevy-initial-sync`/`hevy-incremental-sync`/`hevy-api`) after migration 077 | Their shared "close the matching planned-session task" cleanup now relies on `task_id`'s `ON DELETE CASCADE` instead of an explicit `time_blocks` delete keyed on the retired `source_type='task'` value. |
| Apply **`078_google_tasks_force_recreate.sql`** (redefines `enqueue_google_tasks_outbox()` — the un-cancelled/Reopen branch AND the opt-in branch now stamp their `create` outbox row with `force_recreate: true`) **and** redeploy `google-tasks-sync` | Fixes Reopen (↺) never actually recreating a task on Google when its stale `google_task_id` was already dead there, AND (found in a later review pass, before this migration ever went live) re-opting-in while an earlier opt-out's `'delete'` row is still outstanding, which used to enqueue nothing at all — both `processCreate` implementations bypass the "already created" guard specifically for a `force_recreate` row. Un-applied is survivable: Reopen/re-opt-in still update the local row, they just silently fail to reach Google, exactly as before this migration. |
| Apply **`079_google_tasks_outbox_fifo.sql`** (redefines `claim_google_tasks_outbox()` with per-task FIFO ordering via a base-table anti-join — NOT the window-function version this migration originally shipped with, see the doc note above about the live-`EXPLAIN`-caught `FOR UPDATE`-through-a-CTE bug; adds `clear_google_task_id_if_matches()` and a supporting `(task_id, created_at, id)` index) **BEFORE** the browser bundle carrying the new `googleTasksOutbox.ts` ships and **before** redeploying `google-tasks-sync` | Closes the stale-delete-vs-Reopen race (a `'delete'` and a later `force_recreate` `'create'` for the same task, processed out of order, could wipe a brand-new `google_task_id`) and the create-in-flight-opt-out race — and, once the anti-join fix landed, actually provides the row-locking concurrency guarantee its name promises (the original window-function version silently didn't). **Ordering matters here, unlike most other entries on this list**: both `processDelete` implementations now call `clear_google_task_id_if_matches` instead of the old `apply_google_task_snapshot(p_clear_google_task_id=true)` — there is no fallback branch, so shipping the new code against an un-migrated DB would 404 on every outbox delete until the migration lands. |
| Apply **`080_time_blocks_duration_bounds.sql`** (clamps any existing out-of-range `time_blocks.duration_minutes` rows, then adds a `CHECK (duration_minutes BETWEEN 1 AND 1440)` constraint) | The DB-layer enforcement half of the duration-bounds fix (see the Duration bounds note above) — the client-side `clampDurationMinutes` clamp alone doesn't cover `ai-proxy`'s generic `db_insert`/`db_update` tool, which writes `time_blocks` directly. Un-applied is survivable: the client clamp still catches every UI-originated write; only a direct AI/API write could still slip an out-of-range value through until this lands. |
| Apply **`081_schedule_blocks_effective_from.sql`** (`schedule_blocks.effective_from`, backfilled from `created_at::date`, then `NOT NULL` + `DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE`) | Stops recurring templates fabricating occurrences before they existed (see the Recurring-templates note above). Un-applied is survivable: `createScheduleBlock` retries the insert without the column and the projection treats a missing `effective_from` as unbounded — i.e. exactly today's buggy-but-working behaviour, no crash. |

| Apply **`111_body_goals.sql`** (goal weight / body fat % / muscle mass / phase start on `athlete_profile`; updates no row) **and `112_training_skips.sql`** (`training_skips`, owner-only RLS, `trg_audit`), then redeploy **`ai-proxy`** (athlete_profile catalog text + sleep merge rule) and **`phone-gateway`** (sleep merge rule), JWT OFF for both. On the iPhone import `docs/health-auto-export/06-sleep-catch-up.json` into Health Auto Export (steps in that folder's README) | Goal progress goals sync across devices (until 111: device-local with a warning), Skip on a missed session (until 112: the warning shows, Skip errors), whole nights of sleep without waiting for the weekly re-export. |
| Redeploy `ai-proxy` and `phone-gateway` (JWT OFF for both) | `get_health_stats` uses the minute-grain step/energy collapse the web app uses, and both edge copies of the sleep merge honour a manual night (all three sleep implementations now follow the same contract); `ai-proxy`'s `athlete_profile` catalog entry lists birth_year/sex/height_cm. |
| Apply **`110_athlete_health_profile.sql`** (birth_year, sex, height_cm on `athlete_profile`; updates no row) **and** redeploy `hevy-api` + `hevy-incremental-sync` | The Health page's age/sex-based reference ranges (`src/features/health/benchmarks`), and the Hevy write fixes (workout key whitelist, explicit-null body-measurement merge that stops on a failed read, routine pruning after a complete listing). Un-applied 110 is survivable: the profile fields read as empty and the ranges fall back to generic text. |
| Apply **`109_game_storage_record_split.sql`** (redefines the 108 function; changes no row) | The scrape review's storage banner knows how big the saved ScreenScraper record is. Un-applied: the record is counted inside "Database rows" and the banner assumes a first scrape. |
| Apply **`108_game_storage_usage.sql`** (a read-only function; changes no row) | The game detail's Storage block and the scrape review's storage line. Un-applied is survivable: both are simply hidden. |
| Apply **`107_food_source_delete_keeps_title.sql`** (BEFORE DELETE triggers on `recipes`/`recipe_ingredient_library` copy the name into dependent diary rows' `custom_title`; updates no row until a delete) **and** redeploy `phone-gateway` (dates logs in Europe/Oslo instead of UTC) | Deleting a recipe, temp saved meal or ingredient that was ever logged or planned — without 107 the delete fails with a CHECK violation (toasted, nothing lost). Tested against a local Postgres 16 together with 106: the rows keep their title and last totals. |
| Apply **`106_food_macros_computed.sql`** (derived food totals: recompute functions + triggers on `recipe_ingredients`/`recipes`/`recipe_ingredient_library`/`food_log_entries`, then a one-time recompute of every computable recipe and eaten row — idempotent) | Food totals stay correct for EVERY writer, including `phone-gateway` logs and AI inserts, which the web-side `refreshEatenEntries` can't see. Un-applied is survivable: the web app recalculates on its own edits, but a phone/AI log or a direct DB edit could drift until it lands. Tested against a local Postgres 16 with every change type (library edit, ingredient add/edit/delete, servings, planned→eaten, deleted source, manual switch, re-run). |
| Apply **`105_games_provider_facts.sql`** (`games.provider_kind` + `games.first_played_at`; **updates no existing row**) — then run a PlayStation "Refresh playtime" once to fill them | PlayStation apps (streaming/video apps you launched) can be hidden like Steam tools: the importer auto-promotes anything with 30+ minutes to Playing, and without Sony's category an app inflated play time and Most played. Un-applied is survivable: the list read drops the missing columns and retries, and the import retries its write without them — apps simply stay visible, as before. |
| Apply **`103_games_finished_at_repair.sql`** (replaces a completed game's `finished_at` with its last session when the stored date is more than a day AFTER that session; **updates only those rows**, idempotent) | Honest completion dates for games marked completed before the `last_played_at` stamping fix. Migration 102 only filled NULL dates, so rows the old code had already stamped with `now()` — many marked completed in one sitting — kept sharing one day. Un-applied is survivable: those rows just keep the wrong date. |
| Apply **`102_games_hidden_status_and_finished_backfill.sql`** (widens `games.play_status` to allow `'hidden'`; backfills `finished_at` from `last_played_at` for `completed` rows that have none) | The per-title "Hide game" control and honest completion dates. Un-applied is **not** fully survivable for hiding: the CHECK constraint rejects `'hidden'`, so the Hide button errors (toasted, nothing lost) until this lands. The completion-date change is client-side and works either way; only the backfill of already-completed rows needs the migration. |
| Apply **`101_psn_npsso_expiry.sql`** (`psn_tokens.npsso_expires_at`; **updates no existing row**) **and** redeploy `psn-api` | The npsso countdown and the renew-before-it-dies prompt. Un-applied is survivable: `connect` retries the upsert without the column (the `recipes.fiber_g` convention), `status` returns null for it, and the UI reads a null expiry as "unknown" rather than "expired" — the countdown simply doesn't appear. |
| Apply **`091_psn_tokens.sql`** (`psn_tokens` — RLS + `update_updated_at`, deliberately NO `trg_audit` since it holds live secrets; no existing row touched) | The PlayStation "Connect" flow (`psn-api` edge function). Un-applied: `connect` throws on the missing table, the UI stays on the connect form forever — survivable, no crash. |
| Deploy `steam-api` + add `STEAM_API_KEY`/`STEAM_ID64` to Vault | The Games page's Steam tab. Un-applied/undeployed: the tab shows its "not configured yet" state — survivable. |
| Apply **`092_steam_apps.sql`** (`steam_apps` — shared catalog, no `user_id`, `SELECT`-only RLS for `authenticated`, no `trg_audit`; no existing row touched) **and** redeploy `steam-api` (gained `app_details`/`app_reviews`/`current_players` + extended owned-games fields + rarity-joined achievements) | Steam phase 1: the game detail modal's store metadata, review score and achievement rarity. Un-applied is survivable: `app_details` falls back to an UNCACHED live fetch, which works but burns Steam's ~200-req/5-min rate limit fast on a large library — apply the migration before browsing the library heavily. The two store endpoints need no API key, so they work even before the Vault secrets exist. |
| Deploy `psn-api` (after migration 091) — redeploy again after phase 2, which added `played_games`/`purchased_games`/`title_map`/`title_trophies`/`trophy_groups` and folded the trophy summary + region into `profile` | The Games page's PlayStation tab. **Unverified against a real PSN account** — the first real `npsso` paste is the real test; expect to report back what actually broke (Sony's response shapes are reverse-engineered, see Games Feature Detail). No migration is needed for phase 2 — the bridge is called per opened game, so nothing is cached. |
| Apply **`093_esde_sync_key.sql`** (`game_platforms.esde_system`/`esde_path` + three per-variant `esde_*` play-stat columns + the partial unique index; **updates no existing row**) **and** deploy **`esde-sync`** with JWT verification **OFF** **and** set `ESDE_SYNC_SECRET` in Edge Function secrets | The RP6 handheld's ES-DE library push. All three are prerequisites for the FIRST push, not optional hardening — unlike most entries on this list there is no pre-migration fallback, because the columns the handler upserts against simply do not exist until 093 lands. Apply the migration BEFORE deploying the function. Nothing else in the app reads these columns yet, so an un-applied 093 breaks nothing that exists today. |
| Apply **`104_screenscraper_v2.sql`** (`screenscraper_prefs`; `scrape_decisions.prior_values`/`replaced_paths` with the browser reduced to read-only; `games.ss_jeu_id`/`ss_scraped_at`; `game_scrape_records`; the service-role-only `game_media_usage()`/`game_media_scrape_objects()`; **and a one-time repair**: rows the old scraper stamped `external_source='screenscraper'` get their id moved to `ss_jeu_id` and `external_source` set back to `esde` (ES-DE variant) or `manual`, and the matching `game_platforms` rows back to `esde` — this is what un-breaks `esde-sync`'s upsert for those games) **then** redeploy **`screenscraper-sync`**, deploy the new **`screenscraper-media`** with JWT verification **OFF**, and redeploy **`esde-content-sync`** + **`esde-media-sync`** (950 MB storage guard). Optional: a `SCREENSCRAPER_MEDIA_KEY` secret (any long random string) to key the proxy signatures separately from the service-role key | The ScreenScraper rewrite (the Scrape page). **Apply 104 first** — the new function writes `ss_jeu_id`, `game_scrape_records` and the journal's new columns (it falls back without each of them, but then a match leaves no `ss_jeu_id` marker, the batch cannot tell old matches apart, and undo cannot restore replaced values). Deploy both ScreenScraper functions together — the new page calls actions the old `screenscraper-sync` does not have (the page then says "redeploy"), and previews need `screenscraper-media`. Without 104 nothing is copied into Storage (the budget cannot be read, so images are shown online instead). |
| Apply **`094_screenscraper.sql`** (`screenscraper_systems` + the public-read `game-media` storage bucket; **updates no existing row**) **and** deploy **`screenscraper-sync`** (JWT verification stays **ON**) **and** add the four `SCREENSCRAPER_*` secrets to Vault, **then run its `refresh_systems` action once** | ScreenScraper enrichment. `refresh_systems` populates the numeric system ids that ES-DE folder names are joined against (already done — 250 systems cached); without them a filename lookup cannot be made and searches are not narrowed by system. Un-applied is survivable — nothing else in the app reads either the table or the bucket, and `status` reports `not_configured` rather than throwing when the Vault secrets are absent. |
| Vault secrets: `GEMINI_API_KEY`, `HEVY_*`, `HEALTH_EXPORT_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `GOOGLE_TASKS_SYNC_SECRET`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID/SECRET`, `STRAVA_CLIENT_ID/SECRET`, `KASSALAPP_API_KEY`, `PHONE_GATEWAY_SECRET`, `VAPID_*`, `PUSH_CRON_SECRET`, `STEAM_API_KEY`/`STEAM_ID64` | See Environment Variables below. |
| Set "Enforce JWT Verification" OFF for the eleven functions listed above (Dashboard deploys only) | Hevy/HAE webhooks, Google Tasks cron, phone gateway, push cron, ES-DE sync, the ScreenScraper media proxy. |
| `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` as a GitHub Actions **Variable** + apply `068` + deploy `push-send` | Web Push. |
| Supabase Dashboard → Authentication → URL Configuration → Redirect URLs must include `#/reset-password` (Pages URL + localhost) | Password recovery. |
| Health Auto Export app config: Export Version 2, Summarize ON, Time Grouping **Hours**, all metrics, Since Last Sync (+ weekly reconciliation) | Apple Health ingest correctness. |
| Google Cloud consent screen published to **Production** | Non-expiring refresh token for background Google Calendar/Tasks sync. |

---

## Environment Variables

Client vars are baked at build time by `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`; everything else lives in **Supabase Vault only** and is read by an edge function at runtime. No AI or provider key ever reaches client code.

| Variable | Where |
|---|---|
| `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` / `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` | Client build — main Supabase |
| `VITE_TMDB_API_KEY` | Client build — TMDB (client-safe) |
| `VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` | Client build — Google OAuth (Calendar + Tasks + Health consent) |
| `VITE_STRAVA_CLIENT_ID` | Client build — Strava OAuth start |
| `VITE_OXR_APP_ID` | Client build — Open Exchange Rates (Currency widget) |
| `VITE_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` | Client build (GitHub Actions **Variable**) — the VAPID **public** key the browser uses to subscribe to Web Push. Safe to expose. |
| `GEMINI_API_KEY` | Vault — the ONLY AI key; read by `ai-proxy`. (There is no Claude/OpenAI key in this project.) |
| `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` / `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` | Vault — server side of the unified Google OAuth (`calendar-oauth`/`calendar-token`) |
| `STRAVA_CLIENT_ID` / `STRAVA_CLIENT_SECRET` | Vault — `strava-auth`/`strava-activities` |
| `HEVY_API_KEY` / `HEVY_WEBHOOK_SECRET` / `HEVY_USER_ID` | Vault — Hevy API + webhook auth; `HEVY_USER_ID` is the single-user id reused by every server-side path (health webhook, phone gateway, push cron) |
| `HEALTH_EXPORT_WEBHOOK_SECRET` | Vault — Health Auto Export sends it as `Authorization: Bearer <secret>` |
| `GOOGLE_TASKS_SYNC_SECRET` | Vault — `google-tasks-sync`'s cron-path auth (`x-sync-secret`), read from Vault inside migration `072`'s `lascis-google-tasks-sync` cron job (never in the SQL text). |
| `KASSALAPP_API_KEY` | Vault — personal kassal.app token, read by `food-search`. The client never sees it. |
| `PHONE_GATEWAY_SECRET` | Vault — the iPhone's durable device secret (`x-phone-secret`). Authenticates `phone-gateway` AND `ai-proxy`'s phone branch; both act as `HEVY_USER_ID` server-side. Never put a login/service key on the device. Revoke = rotate this value. `docs/iphone-examples.md`. |
| `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` / `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` / `VAPID_SUBJECT` | Vault — Web Push (VAPID) signing keys for `push-send`. Generate with `npx web-push generate-vapid-keys`. `docs/web-push-setup.md`. |
| `PUSH_CRON_SECRET` | Vault — the pg_cron morning-push job's auth (`x-cron-secret` → `push-send`). Read from Vault **inside** the cron (migration 068), never written into the SQL text. |
| `SCREENSCRAPER_DEVID` / `SCREENSCRAPER_DEVPASSWORD` / `SCREENSCRAPER_SSID` / `SCREENSCRAPER_SSPASSWORD` | Vault — read by `screenscraper-sync` and `screenscraper-media`. **All four are needed and the pairs are not interchangeable**: the dev pair identifies the application, the member pair (the screenscraper.fr website login) is what switches the paid membership on. With only the dev pair the account is anonymous — 1 thread, 10k/day, 128 KB/s. |
| `ESDE_SYNC_SECRET` | Edge Function environment secrets (ES-DE exception to Vault storage) — the RP6 handheld's own device secret (`x-esde-secret`), read by `esde-sync`, `esde-media-sync`, and `esde-content-sync`. Deliberately separate from `PHONE_GATEWAY_SECRET` so revoking the handheld never revokes the iPhone's Shortcuts. Revoke = rotate this value. |
| `TRAKT_CLIENT_ID` / `TRAKT_CLIENT_SECRET` | Vault — the Trakt app's OAuth pair, read by `trakt-api`. The secret never reaches the browser. |
| `TRAKT_SYNC_SECRET` | Edge Function secrets **and** Vault — the Trakt cron's auth (`x-sync-secret`); the cron reads it from Vault, `trakt-api` from its env. |
| `TMDB_API_KEY` | Edge Function secrets — the same TMDB v3 key as `VITE_TMDB_API_KEY`; `trakt-api` needs it to add titles that come from Trakt and to tell Completed from Watching. |
| `MDBLIST_API_KEY` | Edge Function secrets — read by `trakt-api` for Rotten Tomatoes / Metacritic / IMDb / Letterboxd scores by TMDB id. |
| `IGDB_CLIENT_ID` / `IGDB_CLIENT_SECRET` | Edge Function secrets — a Twitch developer app's pair, read by `igdb-api`, which gets and renews its own app token from them. Never in the client. |
| `STEAM_API_KEY` / `STEAM_ID64` | Vault — personal Steam Web API key (steamcommunity.com/dev/apikey) + your own SteamID64, read by `steam-api`. No OAuth — single-user, same posture as `HEVY_USER_ID`. |

`SUPABASE_URL` / `SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` / `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` are **injected into every edge function by the platform** — don't add them to Vault and never expose the service-role key to a client or a device.
